# Shaders

Finding, keying and translating shaders (`dm/gfx/tools/shader_split.py`, `dmsh_build.py`, `dmsh_xlate`, `Program.cs`) and their pipelines. Each entry: symptom, cause, fix, the games it was found on. The [symptom index](../agents/symptoms.md) lists every symptom.

## Shader containers

### A file no reader reads

- **Symptom:** the shader split stops on one archive with a Python traceback (an `IndexError` or `struct.error` naming no file), or a reader's `[RED]` line about a format it does not know, and nothing is written; the NSP script loses the build at the split.
- **Cause:** a worker's exception (and `red()`, a `SystemExit`) ended the whole split through the process pool, so one archive of a format revision a reader does not know stopped every other file's shaders too. `nlg_dict` read its index without checking the layout, so an index of another revision gave an `IndexError` instead of a line naming the archive.
- **Fix:** `scan_file` leaves out a file whose reader fails, with `[RED] <file>: <why> (its shaders are left out)`, and the split goes on; at the end it prints `[RED] <n> file(s) could not be read, their shaders are left out (first: …)` and writes its tables. The NSP script shows that line and packs a provisional profile's IPA without those shaders; for a registered profile it stops there, since the profile's tables were split from every file. `nlg_dict` checks that the records and chunks fit the index and every chunk index is in range before reading it. The format itself still needs a reader: the line names the archive and, for NLG, its header bytes.
- **Found on:** Mario Strikers: Battle League (no profile; [seedds/nxnative#282](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/282)): one of its 879 Next Level Games archives, a revision `nlg_dict` does not read. That revision is read now: Next Level Games archives of revision 4.

### A Yaz0 member that expands is not overlap

- **Symptom:** the split leaves out archives whose members do not overlap: `[RED] <file>/<member>.szs/#7: the archive members add up to more than 16 times the file (members that overlap)`, then `[RED] <n> file(s) could not be read`, and a registered profile's build stops there.
- **Cause:** `scan_blob` bounds archives in archives (SARC, PlatinumGames' DAT) so that a damaged or crafted file cannot make its work grow with each level: they nest at most `NEST` (16) deep, and the members a file's archives open add up to at most 16 times the file. It counted a Yaz0 member by what it expands to, then counted that member's members again, so the same decoded bytes were paid twice. A 204 KB SARC holding one layout archive that expands 9 times added up to 18 times the file.
- **Fix:** two budgets, each 16 times the file. The first counts each member by what it takes up in its archive, so it still stops members that overlap. The second counts what Yaz0 members expand to, charged from the Yaz0 header before the member is decoded. Each bound stays what it was, so the work is still within a small multiple of 16 times the file. Charging only the stored size, with no second budget, would have let the work grow by the Yaz0 ratio (about 90 at most) as well. `test_split_nesting.py` covers the layout archive's shape, the fan-out corpus input, a compressed fan-out (stopped after 19 scans; 8^6 without the bounds) and a Yaz0 member that expands 87 times, stopped before it is decoded.
- **Found on:** Mario Kart 8 Deluxe (GitHub issue #640): its race and result layout archives (`UI/<lang>/race.sarc`, `tv.sarc`, `UI/cmn/tv_result.sarc`), 14 files in 4.0.0 and 28 with 4.0.1.

### BNSH in NintendoWare archives and in main

- BNSH (`BNSH\0\0\0\0` + `grsc` block at +0x60) is found inside `bfsha`, particle effects (`esetb`, VFXB), UI layouts (`blarc`), and `main` itself.
- Variation table at +0x80 (count at +0x7c); binary program at variation +0x10; six stage pointers from program +0x08 (vs, hs, ds, gs, ps, cs); NVN2 adds ray tracing at +0x38 and task / mesh at +0x40 / +0x48 ([Task and mesh shaders in BNSH](#task-and-mesh-shaders-in-bnsh-and-indirect-mesh-draws)). A hull, domain or geometry stage, or a ray tracing module at +0x38, stops the split ([A shader the chain does not translate stops the split and the build](#a-shader-the-chain-does-not-translate-stops-the-split-and-the-build)).
- Stage code descriptor: control ptr +0x08, code ptr +0x10, code size +0x18, control size +0x1c. All pointers are relative to the BNSH start.

### BAHS in agl sharcb

- **How it is stored:** agl `sharcb`, the engine utility shaders: code region at header +0x1c (size +0x18). Binary entries in the table area are 16 bytes `{code offset (0x100-aligned), 0, control size, signed offset from this entry to the control section}`. `bahs_shaders` takes an entry whose control section is an NVN2 one with consistent lengths; an NVN1 entry (control size 0x880 / 0x88c, or 0x848 in BAHS version 9, with a code header) stops the split ([A shader the chain does not translate stops the split and the build](#a-shader-the-chain-does-not-translate-stops-the-split-and-the-build)).
- **Pairing:** control sections are shared between programs, so pair by entry, never by searching for code near a control.
- **Found on:** Super Mario 3D World + Bowser's Fury (BAHS version 9, NVN1, in nxnative); the NVN2 form in Donkey Kong Bananza's agl resources and [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)'s `agl_common.sharcb`.

### A control section's words read as a BAHS entry

- **Symptom:** the shader build fails one agl or terrain shader no run ever asks for, with `Ampere instruction 481 at 0x0 (000000000000000000000000020c0481) is not implemented` (a vertex shader's SPH read as an instruction); in the map, a shader whose control stage disagrees with its code (a compute control with a vertex SPH, a pixel control with a vertex SPH) and whose stored control section is longer than the file's real ones (0x200, 0x1100, 0x1400 against 0xe0). Also: no `pso_pairs.tsv` rows from a file whose lowest such entry hides its binary array.
- **Cause:** `bahs_shaders` finds the entries by scanning the table area 4 bytes at a time. An NVN2 control section's +0x38..+0x47 (instruction length, c1 start and end, code length: `L, 0, L, L`) reads as an entry `{code offset L, 0, control size L, control L bytes on}`. When L is a multiple of 0x100 and a later program's control section lies L bytes on, every check passes: the shader pairs that control with whatever code lies at L in the code region. `bahs_variations` finds the binary array at the lowest entry taken minus 0x18, so such an entry below the real ones also hid that file's variation pairs.
- **Fix:** an entry that lies inside a control section another entry names, measured by the 0x48 bytes of it the key takes (`NVN2_KEY_CTL`) rather than the length an entry claims, is dropped. Whether a false entry's section is genuine does not matter, as its first 0x48 bytes are a real control section.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): six such shaders in `agl_common`, `agl_technique`, `agl_technique_pfx` and `tera_common`, none of them ever loaded; one failed the build. `tera_common` gave 175 variation pairs once its false entry was gone. Splatoon Raiders: the same three agl keys (`68bbe98b3bfd9876`, `18be578dfdaaffa0`, `7a4936fccf82094e`), dropped when its tables were split again ([issue #1613](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1613)). Donkey Kong Bananza was split again too and holds no such entry: its tables did not change. Mario Kart World: one compute shader in `agl_common` (`797e77c2ec4341c5`), dropped from its tables ([issue #1592](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1592)).
- **Payload outside the control section:** descriptor-shaped words can also lie after a control section, beyond the control-span exclusion. A nonempty binary record array that fits before the code region and agrees with the scan supplies the entry offsets for both shader selection and variation pairing. The split drops candidates outside those record entry slots. An unrecognized or inconsistent array leaves the scan candidates unchanged.
- **Also found on:** Mario Kart World 1.8.0, Donkey Kong Bananza and [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_split_bahs_entries.py` uses synthetic fixtures for control words, payload outside entry slots, and scan fallback for inconsistent arrays, including empty arrays and arrays overlapping code.

### Zstd files recognised by their magic

- **Symptom:** `shader_split.py` finds 0 shaders in a RomFS of zstd files named `.zst`.
- **Cause:** the split decompressed only `.zs` files.
- **Fix:** it now goes by the zstd magic (`28 b5 2f fd`) whatever the name.
- **Found on:** Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door (8,726 of the RomFS's 9,959 files, the shader archives among them). Of the other 21 games' RomFS on hand only Super Mario Galaxy's `GDDR3.zst` (a Vulkan game, no NVN split) has such a file.

### Task and mesh shaders in BNSH, and indirect mesh draws

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader <key> is not in the shader map` for shaders whose code is in a `.bfsha` the split reads, `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnCommandBufferDrawMeshTasksIndirect`, and grass and ferns missing.
- **Cause:** a BNSH program's stage pointers went only to compute (+0x30). NVN2's task and mesh stages follow at +0x40 and +0x48, so the split saw only the pixel shaders of such a program. The draw takes a GPU address, whose record the game's culling writes earlier in the frame: zeros when it is recorded.
- **Fix:** `bnsh_shaders` reads the two pointers, taking only one that leads to a control section of that stage, as an older BNSH's program ends before them. Metal's indirect mesh draw takes `{x, y, z}`, not `{count, first}`, so `mdx_run` copies the count on the GPU into `{count, 1, 1}` before the render pass, and the task or object function reads `{count, first}` from the record ([Mesh draws with no task shader above 1024 workgroups](rendering.md#mesh-draws-with-no-task-shader-above-1024-workgroups)). Reading the record on the CPU took 73 % of the submit thread in Kokiri Forest, waiting for the GPU.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 491 mesh and 17 task shaders in `Foliage*.bfsha` and `Model*.bfsha`.

### Float atomics in storage memory

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's picture is far too bright and warm wherever the view opens out: lifted blacks, yellow skin, a pale veil. The build log has `Invalid reduction type: F32FtzRn`, easy to miss among thousands of lines.
- **Cause:** RED.F32.FTZ.RN (0x98e, type 3) adds a float to a word of memory, and the translator dropped it with that log line. agl's scene analysis sums luminance this way (`probe_analyze`, `shadow_analyze`, `sky_analyze`, `cube_map_probe_analyze`, `inner_mask_analyze`, and Foliage and TerrainGround variants). The CPU reads those buffers back into exposure: the sums stayed 0, so it applied its full negative correction. The tone map itself was right.
- **Fix:** `InstEmitMemory.cs` `EmitAtomicAddF32`: a compare-and-swap loop on the word, in `EmitAtomicOp`, so ATOM's float form gets it too. A chain change. F16x2FtzRn stays a log line: no shader here uses it. The shader build then turns the loop of an add whose result nothing reads into one `atomic_fetch_add_explicit` on a `device atomic_float` (MSL 3.0; `dmsh_rewrite.py` `float_add_msl`, only the exact loop spirv-cross writes): agl's analysis adds every thread's value into the same few words, so the loops retried for each other. The translator cannot write the native add itself: its storage buffers are `uint` arrays, a float pointer to one is an `OpBitcast` that SPIR-V's logical addressing forbids, and spirv-cross writes that as a `reinterpret_cast` of the loaded value, not of its address. An add traced to no storage buffer (through the memory pool table), a bounded buffer's guarded swap, shared memory and an ATOM whose result is used stay loops: correct, only slower.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py FloatRed` (the translator's loop, and that `float_add_msl` still finds it); `test_dmsh_float_add.py` (the rewrite, and on the GPU the loop and the native add reach the same sums).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 28 of its seen shaders; the frame's mean brightness at Navi's close-up went from 0.71 to 0.38. With the native add, on a MacBook Air M4, `6e28359aa3558f61` (one workgroup of 256 threads, four words) went from 0.25 ms a frame to under 0.13 ms and `1bba09ec4bc8ff67` from 0.40 ms, the exposure the same (mean luminance 116-117 of 255 both ways); the Hyrule Field view's frame p50 went from 23.42 to 22.39 ms, between two builds (PR #1930).

### A float atomic add's retry flag survives the rewrite

- **Symptom:** a shader that reads a float-add loop's retry flag after the loop has an undeclared identifier after the native-atomic rewrite.
- **Cause:** `float_add_msl` removed the flag with the loop, although the flag is declared before the do-block and remains in scope after it. Only the word, sum and CAS temporaries are local to the block.
- **Fix:** retain the flag as `bool <flag> = false` after the native add. The original loop exits only when that flag is false, so later reads keep their value. An unused flag is left for the compiler to eliminate; the matcher and atomic operation are unchanged.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_float_add.py` consumes the flag after both ordinary and exact adds; its GPU sum test and iPhone compile also consume the flags.
- **Found on:** no game: reproduced with a synthetic shader while reviewing the existing matcher.

### Uniform float atomic adds crash Apple's AGX3 compiler

- **Symptom:** on an M5 or M5 Pro Mac on macOS 26.6.2, `[RED] failed to create compute pipeline for <key>_b: Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED` and `[RED] pipeline archive: the compiler service died under a compile` at every start, with `MTLCompilerService` reports ending in `LLVM ERROR: cannot select: … intrinsic(@llvm.agx3.disable.smashing.float)` in `agc.main.constant_program`; the dispatches the compiler did not give leave flat light-blue areas in Kokiri Forest.
- **Cause:** in Apple's AGX3 compiler backend (`AIR-NT 32023.922`), not here: given the inline `atomic_fetch_add_explicit` on a `device atomic_float*` that `float_add_msl` writes, at an address the same for the whole dispatch, LLVM hoists `@llvm.agx3.disable.smashing.float` into the constant program, where GlobalISel cannot select it for `applegpu_g17g` / `g17s` up to macOS 26.6. It compiles from macOS 27.0, and for the M1 to M4 GPUs.
- **Fix:** a workaround, in the chain: `float_add_msl` writes the add as a call of `dm_atomic_add_f32`, a `static noinline` leaf inserted once after `using namespace metal;`, which keeps the intrinsic out of the constant program; the add is still the hardware's float atomic. A source without that line is a build failure (`float atomic add rewrite failed`). None of this game's float adds is in a task or mesh shader, where a noinline call has its own problems on M1 and M2 ([A noinline call in a task or mesh function locks up an M2's GPU](#a-noinline-call-in-a-task-or-mesh-function-locks-up-an-m2s-gpu)): 22 pixel and 17 compute shaders in 1.0.0's library (upstream nxnative 73150dd9).
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_float_add.py` (the helper once, idempotent, the GPU sums; with `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1`, `metal-tt` for `applegpu_g17g` and `g17s` at macOS 26.6, which skips here: this Mac's `metal-tt` compiles that small kernel's inline add); `test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` `FloatRed`. The game's own `278f48371bcf0d58_b` from 1.0.0's library, through `metal-tt` (AIR-TT 32023.922, Xcode 27.2) with the host's constants at macOS 26.6 (2026-11-25, M2 Pro): inline, `cannot select: %175 … disable.smashing.float` for `applegpu_g17g` and `g17s` (it compiles with a macOS 27.0 minimum); through the helper it compiles for `g17g`, `g17s`, `g14s` and `g13g`. Not run on an M5.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game), in nxnative ([GitHub issue #2825](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2825)): compute shader `278f48371bcf0d58_b` (`AglShader.sharcb#BAHS@0x18bb60`) on an M5 Pro and an M5, macOS 26.6.2; offline there, the rewritten shader compiled for every macOS and iPhone GPU tried.

### textureGatherOffsets and a loop over its offsets

- **Symptom:** dotted horizontal rows in blocks of the picture, in smooth and blurred areas, from a Switch 2 game's upscaler: the frame before it is clean and the frame after it is not. The same input gives a different picture each run. Bright scenes show more of them.
- **Cause:** spirv-cross's `spvGatherConstOffsets` calls `gather()` in a loop with `coffsets[i]`. Metal takes that offset only as a constant expression. At the chain's `-O1` the loop stays, the compiler accepts it without a word, and the gathers read texels at offsets that differ between lanes and between runs. agl's NDSR network builds its 28x28 input tile with these gathers, so some of its lanes read zeros for a channel (at Ocarina of Time's first shared-memory barrier, a word's high half was the luma without its green term), and the network drew that as rows.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` `gather_offsets`: each call of `spvGatherConstOffsets` and `spvGatherCompareConstOffsets` becomes its four gathers with the offsets written out, from the constant array the call names. A call whose array is not such a constant stops the shader. A chain change.
- **Squares:** when the four offsets form a 2x2 square (b, b+(1,0), b+(0,1), b+(1,1), in any order), the call is one gather at b with a swizzle (`gather_quad`). Each offset's texel is the (i0, j0) corner of its own footprint. With integer offsets, those corners are the four texels of b's footprint, each wrapped on its own either way, so the result is the same bits. The test compares the one gather with the four on the GPU at 49 positions per lane, from four texels before the texture to four past it, clamped and repeating. Ocarina of Time: 27 of its 32 shaders with gather offsets have squares, all of them compute (the NDSR networks), with 3 to 15 squares each. On an M6, standing on the Kokiri Forest deck, the frame's 4-dispatch compute encoder that holds the network went from 959–961 µs to 935.5 µs (two runs each, same session; the frame's GPU time shows no difference at that size). A contributor measured the network's largest dispatch on an M4 Pro at 845 → 779 µs (discussion #1789).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_gather_offsets.py`. Offline, one captured dispatch of `b106afba1a18faa9`: about 40 % of the pixels differed between three runs of the old shader; the rewritten one gave the same bytes each time, equal to a version that reads the texels explicitly, and took 3.85 ms against 4.16 ms.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 28 NDSR compute shaders of its seen shaders. Every game with agl's upscaler builds it the same way. Not yet checked: whether this is the open cause of Mario Kart World's isolated bright dots.

### `nn::ndve`'s movie enhancement file

- **Symptom:** when an AV1 movie starts, `[RED] shader <key> is not in the shader map` for 11 compute shaders and `[RED] the dispatched program has no compute shader (or it is not mapped)`.
- **Cause:** `Movie/Ndve/Ndve-Nvn2-vid_seq5.dat.zs` holds the compute networks (`roulibr`) that upscale the game's movies, in a wrapper the split did not read: `NDVE`, a header, an NDSR pack, then the networks, each control section with its code area right behind it. The pack's entries are 8 bytes, a shader taking two, so a data entry moves the next shader by 8. The walk went by 16 bytes and lost it.
- **Fix:** `shader_split.py` reads the wrapper (`ndve_shaders`) and walks the pack by entry. On Ocarina of Time the walk also finds 3 more compute shaders in `agl_ndsr.pck`, whose records are the same; a profile gets them at its next split.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_split_ndve.py`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### The NDSR pack's record start

- **Symptom:** `draw skipped: no render pipeline (vertex - fragment -)` in a game's final passes; shaders "not in the shader map", all from `agl_ndsr.pck`. A short synthetic pack found by fuzzing raised `struct.error: iterative unpacking requires a buffer of a multiple of 16 bytes` while choosing the record start.
- **Cause:** the pack's records start at +0x10 in one SDK's version and +0x14 in another's, by the words in its header (Donkey Kong Bananza 0x300, 0x6efeff, 0xc0000; Splatoon Raiders 0x40100, 0x80300, 0x90, 0x20c00).
- **Fix:** `ndsr_shaders` in `shader_split.py` scores up to 16 complete records at each start and takes the start with more pointers to NVN2 control sections. The two starts can leave different numbers of trailing bytes in a short pack; those partial records are excluded from the score, as from the subsequent record walk.
- **Found on:** Splatoon Raiders.

## Control sections and code

### A shader the chain does not translate stops the split and the build

- **Symptom:** the shader split stops with `[RED] <file> BNSH @0x… variation <n>: a geometry shader: this chain translates only the vertex, pixel, compute, task and mesh shaders of an NVN2 game, as Ocarina of Time has no other` (also `a hull shader`, `a domain shader`, `a ray tracing shader`, and `<file>#BAHS@0x…`, `#NDSR@`, `#NDVE@` in place of the BNSH variation), `[RED] <file> BNSH @0x… variation <n> <stage>: not an NVN2 control section (version 0xc9): an NVN1 (Maxwell) shader, which this chain does not translate` or `[RED] <file>#BAHS@0x…: an NVN1 (Maxwell) shader entry: this chain translates only NVN2 (Switch 2) shaders`, and writes no map. The shader build stops before its first job with `[RED] <map>: shader <key> is a <stage> shader in package <package>: this chain translates only the vertex, pixel, compute, task and mesh shaders of an NVN2 game (each in the package of its stage), as Ocarina of Time has no other`, `[RED] <package>: shader <key> has no NVN2 control section (magic 0x98761234, version 0xc9): this chain translates only NVN2 (Switch 2, Ampere) shaders, and an NVN1 (Maxwell) record is not skipped but stops the build`, `[RED] <package>: shader <key> is an NVN2 ray tracing module (control stage 8): this chain has no ray tracing path, as the game has no such shader` or `[RED] <package>: shader <key> is not a <stage> shader (control stage byte <n>, SPH shader type <n>): this chain translates only vertex, pixel, compute, task and mesh shaders, each from the package of its stage`. A vertex shader that writes the layer fails its own build, `[layer output]` in the summary: `the vertex shader writes the layer (render_target_array_index), which this chain and the host have no path for` (the build goes on; the packing script stops at it as a shader regression when the previous build translated it). At run time: `[RED] a shader's control section is not NVN2's (magic 0x98761234, version 0xc9): no shader key for it, as the shader library holds only NVN2 shaders`, then `FATAL program stage <n> has no shader key` when the program is first used.
- **Cause:** the chain keeps only what Ocarina of Time's shaders need. Its shader map holds 42,847 shaders: 22,352 pixel, 15,850 vertex, 4,125 compute, 500 mesh and 20 task shaders, every one NVN2 (Ampere code); none is a hull, domain, geometry or ray tracing shader, and no vertex shader writes the layer. So nxnative's paths for other games' shaders were removed from the chain: NVN1 records (Maxwell code behind a code header, VertexA / VertexB programs, the control fields at +0x700 to +0x754 of 0x880-byte or shorter sections, early fragment tests at +0x718, transform feedback layouts, the profile key `shader_key_skip`), the geometry lowering (compute kernels, their readers, mesh libraries and the vertex shaders' capture entry `main0_gs`), tessellation, ray tracing modules, and layered vertex shaders (the layer entry points `main0_l` / `main0_lz`, made for 4,571 of the game's vertex shaders though only a geometry shader that picks the layer used them; the point size taken out beside a layer output; in the host the triangle class and the layered pass). A shader of that kind is refused where it is first met, by name, instead of left out: a library without it would lack a shader the game may ask for, and the first draw with it would find nothing to load.
- **Fix:** none for this game, whose shaders are all of those five stages and NVN2. A game with other shaders needs the removed paths back from nxnative: `dmsh_geometry.py`, `dmsh_tess.py`, `dmsh_mesh.py`, `dmsh_rt.py`, `dmsh_rt_build.py` and `dmsh_rt_compile.py`, the NVN1 branches of `shader_split.py` and `dmsh_build.py` and the NVN1 key in `hle_nvn.c` `keys_hash`, and the host's stages for them ([Entries for transform feedback, tessellation and ray tracing report in red](nvn-objects.md#entries-for-transform-feedback-tessellation-and-ray-tracing-report-in-red)).
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_split_nvn2_only.py` (the split), `dm/tools/tests/test_shader_package_keys.py` (`CheckRecord`), `dm/tools/tests/test_dmsh_layer_out.py` (`writes_layer`), `t_nvn` (no key for a control section that is not NVN2's).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): the 42,847 shaders above.

### Code area and control section layout

- **Code area:** Ampere code. A graphics stage's starts with its 0x80-byte SPH (version 4; word 0 bits 10..13 the shader type: 1 vertex, 5 pixel), then the instructions, with no code header; a task or mesh shader's starts with an SPH too, which its translation takes off ([NVN2 task and mesh shaders](#nvn2-task-and-mesh-shaders)).
- **Control section:** magic `0x98761234`, version 0xc9 at +4 (`dmsh_ampere.is_nvn2`); +0x30 its own length in BNSH files (0 in BAHS files and in memory, so the key hashes it as zero); +0x38 the instructions' length (the SPH included), +0x40 that rounded up to 0x100, +0x44 the code area's, the c1 constant segment lying between the last two; +0x56 the stage of a compute (5), task (6) or mesh (7) shader, 8 for a ray tracing module, absent from a short graphics section (next entry); compute's thread group at +0x58 (u32 ×3) and its shared memory at +0x64 (not yet seen nonzero); a pixel shader's early fragment tests at +0x5b ([Early fragment tests from the control section](#early-fragment-tests-from-the-control-section)). The key takes the first 0x48 bytes (`NVN2_KEY_CTL`).
- **Found on:** Donkey Kong Bananza and [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (`dmsh_ampere.py`, `shader_split.py`). NVN1's layout (a 0x30-byte code header and a 0x50-byte SPH, compute instructions at +0x100, the fields at +0x700 to +0x754), found on Super Mario Bros. Wonder in nxnative, is not read any more.

### Optional stage byte in short NVN2 control sections

- **Symptom:** the offline shader build raises `IndexError: index out of range` in `job_for` while reading `rec[ci + 0x56]`, after the shader split succeeds.
- **Cause:** NVN2 identification needs only the 0x48-byte base control header. A valid short graphics control section can omit the optional stage byte at +0x56; the build's test for a ray tracing record read it without checking the section's length. The package's stored code length and control marker agree, so this is not a misplaced control boundary.
- **Fix:** read +0x56 only when the section has it. `check_record` in `dmsh_build.py`, which every record passes, takes a short graphics section's stage from its SPH alone; a section whose +0x56 says 8 (a ray tracing module) stops the build ([A shader the chain does not translate stops the split and the build](#a-shader-the-chain-does-not-translate-stops-the-split-and-the-build)). The synthetic job tests of control lengths 0x48, 0x50, 0x56, 0x57 and 0xe0 went with the ray tracing build; `test_shader_package_keys.py` (`CheckRecord`) tests full-length sections only.
- **Found on:** Splatoon Raiders, [issue #2916](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2916): vertex key `22fff9012db5acaa`, `vs_other.bin` index 81, `agl_common.sharcb#BAHS@0x27ea4`, has a 0x50-byte NVN2 control section. Resolved, 2026-11-17.

### Program keys computed inside `nvnProgramSetShaders`

- **Symptom:** shaders reported missing at random, and their dumped control sections are garbage (Unity). Also: about 130 `[RED] shader … is not in the shader map` keys a boot, nearly all different from run to run, all single-stage programs, with `program has no vertex shader`, and every frame the same picture of grey noise; with `DM_NVN_SHDUMP` set the game draws correctly.
- **Cause:** NVN lets the caller free a program's control section once `nvnProgramSetShaders` returns; Unity does, and the host used to hash it at the first draw, so keys computed later hashed freed memory. The keys were then computed in `SetShaders` but kept by the NVNprogram's address, and Unity 2018.4 copies an NVNprogram after `SetShaders` and draws with the copy: the copy had no keys at its address, so its stages were hashed at the first draw again, from the freed section. `DM_NVN_SHDUMP` hid it, as it points every stage at a host copy of its section.
- **Fix:** the keys are computed inside `SetShaders` (`hle_nvn.c` `prog_key`) and are found by the program's serial, which a copy carries, as the Metal side finds the program's translation. A serial's record stays as long as the process (a copy can outlive the program it was made from; 64 bytes a `SetShaders`). Mario calls `SetShaders` for some programs with a code address that is not mapped yet, so a stage whose code is not in a registered memory pool at that moment is hashed at its first draw, from a host copy of its control section that `SetShaders` keeps.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem Engage and Unity games in general (the source names no other); Super Mario Bros. Wonder (the unmapped code addresses); Ori and the Will of the Wisps (`01008DD013200000`, Unity 2018.4.24f1, 1.2.1: the program copies, 135 stages a boot; no profile yet).

- **Missing control sections (resolved, found by clang-tidy):** `prog_key` must return the hash failure without marking a cached key valid. Previously a serial-less stage returned success with uninitialized output, and a serial-backed stage cached a zero key. Resolution and prefetch now stop with a fatal diagnostic rather than reading an invalid key; `t_nvn` checks both forms and a successful retry after supplying a control section. No game failure was observed.

## Translation

### Optimized shader calls and memory snapshots

- **Symptom:** with shader job `debug = false`, SPIR-V generation throws `GetLocalPointer` / `KeyNotFoundException` or casts an `AstOperation` to an `AstOperand`. Other inputs compile but read a call's output before calling it, lose an unused-return call's side effects, or read local memory after an intervening store instead of preserving its earlier value.
- **Cause:** AST expression propagation treated every single-use assignment as a movable value. SPIR-V call arguments require local storage passed by reference; propagating their temporaries destroyed those lvalues. Moving call results or memory loads changed evaluation order, and dead-assignment removal could discard a call or atomic whose result was unused. A snapshot of an output argument could also move past another call that overwrote it.
- **Fix:** mark address-taken call locals and retain their storage. Propagate only pure expression trees with immutable leaves; keep memory reads and call/atomic effects at their original evaluation points, and retain unused assignments with side effects. This is shared translator behavior, without shader, game or build gates. Default shader builds use `DebugMode` and already skip this AST optimizer; their generated source is expected to remain unchanged.
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch 2 Welcome Tour, while testing original RT bodies in an isolated callable-region prototype. The pointer and effect failures also reproduce in ordinary compute IR without that prototype mode. This does not establish playable RT graphics.
- **Check:** ordinary compute GPU fixtures cover nested calls, constant/expression/formal arguments, output lvalues, return ordering, storage side effects and memory snapshots. An independent aliased Ampere carry fixture fails 7 of 736 state words before the fix and zero afterward; plain register-SSA and debug-mode controls pass both before and after.

### A jump enters a condition whose definition it bypassed

- **Symptom:** Metal warns that a boolean is used uninitialized on a path through a translated shader. A generated `if (!bypass) predicate = ...; if (predicate || bypass)` reads `predicate` before testing the bypass flag; the analogous AND form has the same defect.
- **Cause:** goto elimination widened an existing IF/ELSE condition with the jump flag. The jump can bypass the original condition's definition. Combining those operands does not preserve their original evaluation paths, even when the flag alone determines the combined result.
- **Fix:** materialize the entry condition in a registered boolean temporary, initialized from the jump flag. Evaluate the original condition only on its original entry path. Place that initialization after the enclosure that skips the bypassed statements, so the jump cannot skip the temporary's initialization too. This is shared structured-control-flow lowering, in both debug and optimized translation; it does not initialize arbitrary shader locals.
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch 2 Welcome Tour, six native RT condition sites. Ordinary compute CFGs independently reproduce the same error.
- **Check:** 64 synthetic twelve-block CFGs, including conditional and unconditional jumps, exhaust 2,048 input patterns in debug and optimized modes: 3,145,728 path/output checks, with an eager AST interpreter rejecting every undefined read. The old lowering fails that interpreter and emits 103 conditional-uninitialized warnings per mode; current SPIR-V validates and Metal compiles with `-Werror=sometimes-uninitialized`. The full build's early warnings come from rechecking the previous library for cache prefill: shader `009135d86e77a09e_b` reproduces those exact old diagnostics, while its current source passes the strict compile.

### Tested tools and compiler-specific shader caches

- **Symptom:** a shader build accepts tools that differ from `dmsh_chain.lock`; after an override build, returning to the tested Metal compiler can still restore the override's libraries.
- **Cause:** `own_compilers.py` previously accepted any present Metal compiler or SPIRV-Cross. The shader and fetch-variant caches hashed the recorded Metal version and flags, without the actual compiler or SDK. Prefill checked only a sample's compiled bytes, so a mixed folder's other libraries could be attributed to the wrong compiler. The NSP stage key recorded Xcode's version, which can stay unchanged when its separately downloaded Metal toolchain changes, and did not follow `SPIRV_CROSS` overrides.
- **Fix:** ship a universal SPIRV-Cross built from a pinned commit and require its fingerprint for NVN builds. Require both tested Metal compilers before an NVN game's shader steps; `--allow-other-metal` (`DM_ALLOW_OTHER_METAL=1` for `own_compilers.py`) explicitly permits another present Metal compiler. Shader and fetch-variant keys include the actual Metal compiler and SDK identities. Earlier-library prefill requires that identity on every library's reflection (`chain_compiler`); matching a few sample compiles cannot establish the unsampled libraries' compiler. Unmarked legacy libraries are not imported, and the Metal cache format is bumped to discard entries prefill might have misclassified. NSP and regression keys hash the selected converter's bytes, including overrides; NSP keys also include both SDKs and Metal tools.
- **Found on:** Breath of the Wild, [issue #242](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/242). The report used an untested compiler, but whether that caused the corrupted picture remains **open**. The cache failures were reproduced with stand-in libraries; this does not establish the rendering cause.
- **Check:** `test_own_compilers.py` checks enforcement and the explicit Metal override. `test_shader_toolchain_cache.py` first restored an untested library after switching compiler identities, for both main shaders and fetch variants, and now rejects it. It also checks stage invalidation on actual Metal, SDK and selected converter changes. `test_spirv_cross_pin.py` checks the shipped binary, pin, lock, architectures and licences.

### Metal compiler of a newer Xcode

- **Symptom:** after an Xcode update the build stops with `[RED] this Mac's Metal compiler is not the one the shaders were tested with (dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_chain.lock): metal_macosx Apple metal version 32023.922 …`; `xcodebuild -downloadComponent MetalToolchain`, as the message said, downloads the same untested compiler again.
- **Cause:** since Xcode 26 the Metal toolchain is a system asset that each Xcode downloads in its own build and `xcrun` takes for the selected Xcode (`xcodebuild -showComponent MetalToolchain` names it), whatever the macOS version. When #717 was reported, macOS 27.2 with Xcode 27.1 selected gave the then-pinned 32023.921, with Xcode 27.2 selected 32023.922. The message named the Xcode to install but not that it must be the selected one, and README linked the App Store, which offers only the newest Xcode.
- **Fix:** the stop message names the selected Xcode and its folder, and says to install the tested Xcode from Apple's download page (beside a newer one as `Xcode-<version>.app`), select it (`xcode-select -s` or `DEVELOPER_DIR`), then download its Metal toolchain; README says the same. The lock stays at the one tested compiler: another one needs a regression run before it is recorded, and the lock is part of the chain version. Whether two installed toolchain assets each follow their own Xcode is inferred from `-showComponent`, not yet tried with both installed (**open**).
- **Found on:** no game: [GitHub issue #717](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/717), on macOS 27.2 with Xcode 27.2 (Metal Toolchain 27B47).
- **Check:** `test_own_compilers.py` (the selection step before the download), `test_ci_toolchain.py` (the message's and README's Xcode are CI's).

### Idle MSBuild workers block regression measurements

- **Symptom:** `regress.py` waits for other jobs to end while the only reported jobs are sleeping `MSBuild.dll /nodemode:1 /nodeReuse:true` workers using 0% CPU.
- **Cause:** the busy-machine check treated every `dotnet` process as an active build. Reusable MSBuild workers can remain asleep after their build exits, and later builds can recreate them after a build-server shutdown.
- **Fix:** exclude only sleeping, zero-CPU MSBuild reuse workers whose parent has exited. Active workers, I/O-blocked workers, workers still attached to a parent, and build commands remain busy jobs.
- **Check:** `test_regress.py` supplies the process census that blocked the measurement, with active, I/O-blocked and parented controls.
- **Found on:** no game: Xcode 27.2 toolchain validation on an M4 Pro.

### SPIRV-Cross parse failures

- **Symptom:** SPIRV-Cross can't parse the SPIR-V (`nullptr` exception).
- **Cause:** 2 agl post-effect compute shaders still produce such SPIR-V. Before the compute start-offset fix (Maxwell compute instructions start at +0x100 of the code area, after a code header and its padding; decoded from +0x30, every scheduling word was misread) it was 7: misdecoded code produced the bad SPIR-V. This chain reads no Maxwell code any more ([A shader the chain does not translate stops the split and the build](#a-shader-the-chain-does-not-translate-stops-the-split-and-the-build)).
- **Found on:** Super Mario Bros. Wonder.

### Pixel shader inputs the vertex shader does not write

- **Symptom:** `Fragment input(s) user(locnN) mismatching vertex shader output type(s) or not written by vertex shader` and the draw is skipped; the host reports `[RED] failed to create render pipeline (vertex … fragment …)`. Offline packing fails with `pso_offline: metal-tt failed` for the same reason.
- **Cause:** every pixel shader input must be a vertex shader output. Maxwell attributes are untyped slots: a pixel shader that reads a slot the vertex shader never wrote gets a default, but Metal refuses to build the pipeline. Translating one shader at a time cannot know what the partner reads. The offline archive exposed a second omission: prebuild reads both `shader_pairs.tsv` and BAHS program variations in `pso_pairs.tsv`, but padding read only the first table.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` reads both pair tables and gives each vertex shader the outputs its partners read but it doesn't write, as `float4` (0, 0, 0, 1) (`vs_pad`). A profile without `pso_pairs.tsv` takes the split's copy beside the package, as the archive builder does. The union controls pixel-before-vertex build order and shader cache dependencies ([Shader cache key covering the pairs](#shader-cache-key-covering-the-pairs)); the profile input stamp includes its `pso_pairs.tsv`, and the NSP script's shader reuse key includes the generated fallback. Padding precedes the depth, shadow-input and pulling variants. Rebuild shaders and the host for the new chain. The host caches failed pipelines and reports each once with its vertex / fragment keys; before that, every draw retried the failing compile.
- **Found on:** Super Mario Bros. Wonder: 331 of 9,043 pairs failed; the opening course used none of them, later levels did (about 11,500 failed builds in one 11-minute session). The missing BAHS table was reproduced on Tears of the Kingdom ([#283](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/283)) and Super Mario Odyssey ([#276](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/276)); their notes keep the pair counts and validation.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_pairs.py` covers both table locations, union/deduplication, defaults and cache dependencies; Metal compiles the generated sources for Mac and iOS and builds an archive with regular and pulling vertex entries in both depth modes. `test_nsp_mac.py` checks fallback invalidation and progress ordering; `test_nsp_incremental.py` regenerates a missing fallback. Check all pairs offline after a game build (recipe in [05](../agents/05-debugging.md)).

### Pixel shader inputs the mesh shader does not write

- **Symptom:** `[RED] task / mesh pipeline: task - mesh … fragment …: … is not found in the output from the previous stage`, and the mesh draws of that program are skipped. Ocarina of Time's foliage: the point meshes `3a6340a04bf09797` and `a203347bb7bdbd03` write only a point size and a position, while their pixel shaders (`2a7c48d595feaaeb`, `cd609edc78b467d8`) read generic attribute 5 (pull request #1590).
- **Cause:** the same as [for a vertex shader](#pixel-shader-inputs-the-vertex-shader-does-not-write): a pixel shader input no stage wrote reads a default on the console, and Metal refuses the pipeline. Mesh shaders were left out of that rule twice: `shader_split.py` `var_pairs` paired only vertex keys with pixel keys, so no table held a mesh shader's partners, and `dmsh_meshstage.to_stage` made only the outputs its SPHs enable. It is not point sprite coordinate replacement: the pixel shaders' SPH input maps read generic attribute 5 (not a fixed-function texture coordinate, the only kind the hardware's point sprite select replaces, nor the point coordinate at attribute `0x2e0`), NVN has no point sprite function (only `nvnCommandBufferSetPointSize`), and the same foliage file's triangle meshes leave locations 2, 8 and 9 unwritten that their pixel shaders read.
- **Fix:** `var_pairs` pairs a mesh key with its variation's pixel key as it pairs a vertex key, and `dmsh_build.py` passes `to_stage` what the partners read (`partner_inputs`, which `vs_pad` uses too): the locations neither the vertex nor the per-primitive SPH enables become vertex outputs of (0, 0, 0, 1). A mesh shader's pairs feed its build order and cache key as a vertex shader's do. `pso_offline.py` `plan` and the host's prebuild (`nvn_prewarm.inc` `pre_desc`) leave mesh pairs out: mesh pipelines are built at the draw (`nvn_mesh.inc` `mtk_pso`), not from archive bases. Ocarina's `shader_pairs.tsv` was split again. Of Ocarina's 1,284 mesh pairs (491 mesh shaders), 31 read a location their mesh shader does not write (19 mesh shaders, in the foliage and model files); with the padding Metal built the mesh pipeline of every pair but one.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): the foliage files' variations; pull request #1590 found the failing pipelines and padded at run time on Metal's error text, which this replaces.
- **Then:** one pair padded past Metal's mesh size: [Mesh outputs as wide as their pixel shaders read](#mesh-outputs-as-wide-as-their-pixel-shaders-read).
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_meshstage.py` (`test_pad_unwritten_pixel_inputs`, and a padded point mesh compiled by Metal), `test_dmsh_pairs.py` (`VariationPairs`, with `DM_GAME` set), `test_pso_offline.py`.

### Mesh outputs as wide as their pixel shaders read

- **Symptom:** `[RED] task / mesh pipeline: … Total mesh size (35840) exceeds the maximum mesh size allowed (32768)`, and the program's mesh draws are skipped: Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh `626fbf48041cb82b` with pixel shader `c5eb985e769e317f` (`Foliage.Product.110`, `#BNSH@0x1b1000#var3`), once [padded](#pixel-shader-inputs-the-mesh-shader-does-not-write).
- **Cause:** what Metal counts against a mesh's 32 KB is its declared outputs, the largest vertex count times the vertex struct (35,840 = 160 × 14 `float4`). `to_stage` declared a `float4` for every attribute an SPH names: the mesh writes 26 components a vertex, x alone in four attributes and x, y, z in six, but declared 11 `float4` beside its position, and 3 more padded. The hardware's attribute area holds only the written words.
- **Fix:** with its pixel partners known, `to_stage` declares each output as wide as they can see (`_width`): the components the shader writes, and a w they read but it does not write, which must hold 1. A Metal fragment input wider than the mesh output it links to reads 0 in the missing components (checked on the GPU: `float`, `float2`, `float3` into `float4` read 0 in the rest, `w` too), the hardware's default for x, y and z. `dmsh_build.py` `partner_reads` takes what each partner reads from its SPH input map (`dmsh_meshstage.pixel_reads`, byte 0x18 on, two bits a component); a location its MSL declares that the map does not name counts as all four. Without pairs every output stays a `float4`. The mesh above comes to 160 × (16 + 4 × 4 + 6 × 16 + 3 × 16) = 28,160 bytes, the 3 padded locations kept `float4` as its pixel shader reads their w.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game): all 1,284 of its mesh pairs build their Metal mesh pipelines.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_meshstage.py` (`test_outputs_as_wide_as_read`, `test_pixel_reads`, and Metal compiling narrowed outputs).

### Single-stage programs paired with a cover

- **Cause:** a game that builds one NVN program per GLSLC output and combines vertex and pixel programs per draw with `BindProgram` stage bits has its real pairs only at draw time, so the offline build cannot know which pixel shaders a vertex shader must feed.
- **Fix:** the composite-program path ([Programs bound per stage](rendering.md#programs-bound-per-stage)) does all the pairing at replay. When every pixel shader input is a plain `float4` at fixed locations, `shader_split.py` pairs every vertex shader with a *cover*: the fewest pixel shaders whose inputs together are every input any pixel shader reads. `vs_pad` then gives each vertex shader all of them, and any vertex / pixel combination links.
- **Found on:** 13 Sentinels: Aegis Rim: 838 `ProgramSetShaders` at startup; every pixel shader input is a plain `float4` at locations 1–9 (the SPH input map, words 6–13, matches the translated MSL inputs for all 375 pixel shaders), so the cover is one shader.

### Unwritten components of a written output start as (0, 0, 0, 1)

- **Symptom:** outdoors dim and nothing casts a sun shadow (every shadow-map lookup fails).
- **Cause:** a vertex shader may write only part of an output (Link's Awakening writes a world position's x, y and z) while the pixel shader reads the rest (it divides by w). On the hardware the unwritten components read as (0, 0, 0, 1); Ryujinx gets the same by initializing the components the next stage reads when it links stages. The translator's MSL starts every vertex shader with `main0_out out = {};`, all zeros, so w was 0, and a fragment shader dividing by it got infinite compare values.
- **Fix:** general: `dmsh_build.py` (`vs_init`) sets every `float4` output to (0, 0, 0, 1) right after it, and the translated body overwrites what it writes.
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening (Mac and iPhone): 12,281 of 18,410 vertex outputs never write w. The sun's shadow map (2048×2048, depth 32-bit float) was correct; the surface shaders compute the lookup from a world position the vertex shader passes in `locn24`, divided by its w. Found with `DM_NVN_TEXRAW` on the shadow map, the light matrices from `DM_NVN_CBDUMP`, and a hand-edited copy of one surface shader that wrote the compare value and the stored depth as its colour. Compared with forcing the compare to pass (`compareFunction` always): the same brightness, plus shadows from the fences, the house and the trees.

### Early fragment tests from the control section

- **Symptom:** rows of dark dots along contour lines of alpha-tested surfaces drawn after a depth pre-pass (Tears of the Kingdom: the stone lattice in front of the Great Sky Island start, as reported by MacNativeGames ae8f4f4; not reproduced here). On Switch 2, GPU time: every covered pixel of a shader that writes memory was shaded (PRAGMATA's cabin on an M4 Pro: GPU 19.0 ms a frame, p95 frame 64 ms, a quarter of the frames over 33 ms).
- **Cause:** byte +0x718 of a pixel shader's NVN control section is its early fragment tests flag: the NVN driver's getter is `ldrb w0, [x8, #0x718]` in the `sdk` of all 21 games on hand, and Ryujinx receives it as the 3D engine's EarlyZForce. The translator always passed `earlyZForce` false. Without the attribute, Metal tests depth after a fragment shader that writes memory. In the lattice's G-buffer pass (EQUAL, no depth write, after a LESS pre-pass) that late depth differed by an ulp from the pre-pass along contour lines, the test failed there and the G-buffer kept its clear value. A Switch 2 (NVN2) control section keeps the flag at byte +0x5b: the NVN2 driver reads the pixel shader's flags one byte each at +0x58..+0x62 (`ldr x8, [x0, #8]; ldrb w0, [x8, #0x5b]; ret`, at the same slot of the `sdk`'s seven vtables, one per control section version), and Ampere's SPH has no such bit (NVK sends it as the 3D method `SET_API_MANDATED_EARLY_Z`). Over 70,763 NVN2 pixel shaders of four games, +0x58 is set exactly when the SPH writes depth (word 19 bit 1), and +0x5b on none of those. `job_for` read the flag in its NVN1 branch only, so no NVN2 shader got the attribute.
- **Fix:** general: `dmsh_build.py` (`early_fragment_tests`) sets `early_fragment_tests` in the translation job of every pixel shader whose NVN2 control byte +0x5b is set (`dmsh_ampere.early_fragment_tests`). `dmsh_xlate` passes it to Ryujinx as `earlyZForce`, and the MSL entry point is declared `[[ early_fragment_tests ]]`. A shader whose SPH writes depth (word 19 bit 1) is left as it is, since a test made early would not use the depth it computes. nxnative read NVN1's flag at +0x718 the same way; this chain reads no NVN1 section ([A shader the chain does not translate stops the split and the build](#a-shader-the-chain-does-not-translate-stops-the-split-and-the-build)).
- **Found on:** Tears of the Kingdom: 1,677 of its pixel shaders set the flag, and none of them writes depth. Rebuilding the chain changed exactly those 1,677 shaders' MSL, by the attribute alone. Elsewhere the attribute alone changed 699 of Wonder's 16,268 shaders and 51 of Scarlet's 30,142. Breath of the Wild and ANIMAL WELL set the flag in no shader. Switch 2: [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) 746 of 22,352 pixel shaders, Donkey Kong Bananza 2,855 of 13,546, Orbitals 38 of 16,459 and PRAGMATA 6,175 of 18,406 (the last two have no profile here). On PRAGMATA's cabin route the flag is set on all 81 pixel shaders whose only reason to test late is their own store (the streaming feedback's `atomic_fetch_min`), on 11 of 84 that discard, and on none of its 75 without side effects or its 5 depth writers; with the attribute GPU time fell to 16.6 ms, p95 to 26 ms and frames over 33 ms to 0.2 %, with no flicker and no coarser textures (PR #1846).
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_early_fragment_tests.py` (NVN2's flag, and a long section's +0x718 not read).
- **Open:** with the attribute, a fragment shader that writes memory gives a picture that varies from run to run on Apple's GPU: Tears of the Kingdom's `8f470f5ce5864df7`, a per-fragment visibility flag (2026-10-30; the frame's replays: one digest without the attribute, 3 to 7 in 8 with it).

### Texture-read tracking delayed depth rejection

- **Symptom:** fragment shading takes longer than it should in passes that draw many overlapping objects with shaders that read textures through a pool.
- **Cause:** the host's texture-read mark (`dm_pool_mark`) is a store, so Metal tests a fragment shader that has it against depth after running it, unless the function asks for early tests; `pool_arrays` asks only when the mark is the shader's only store and it does not discard ([A host's own store in a fragment shader moves its depth test](frame-rate.md#a-hosts-own-store-in-a-fragment-shader-moves-its-depth-test)). A shader that discards keeps late tests for every draw, also where an early test would give the same picture. And the check for the shader's own device writes also matched SPIRV-Cross's generic `spvUnsafeArray` accessor `device T& operator []`, which the template defines whether or not a device array is made of it.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain and the host. `dmsh_reflect.py` `pool_early_variant` leaves that accessor out of the check and names a pixel shader whose only store is the mark but that `pool_arrays` left with late tests: `read_only` when it discards, `standard` when the accessor alone kept the tests late. `dmsh_build.py` `finish` gives such a shader a second entry point with the same body and `[[early_fragment_tests]]`, `main0_early` in `<key>_b` (the reflection's `entry_early`, and `early_read_only` for one that discards; `dmsh_side.py` gives it no alpha test or logic operation entry point). The host loads it (`nvn_shader.inc`, `early_fn`) and `pso_desc` takes it in place of `main0` for a pipeline with a depth or stencil attachment, no alpha test, alpha-to-coverage or logic operation and no merged or specialized fragment. A shader that discards takes it only where an early test cannot be seen: a depth test that can reject (not `ALWAYS`), no depth write, no stencil test and no occlusion query whose count the game reads (`nvn_pso.inc` `pso_early`, the key's `early_read_only` in what was padding: prewarm rows from before it read as 0, the regular entry). The depth state does not mark the pipeline dirty, so each draw compares it. `pso_offline.py` gives both entries a base in the offline archive. `DM_NVN_EARLY_DEPTH=0` keeps `main0` for every draw (a measurement switch; `ab` alternates it with `DM_AB`, each phase with pipelines of its own). A chain change.
- **Reach in Ocarina of Time:** of 1,500 sampled pixel shaders 65 are marked and late only because they discard (`read_only`, about 970 of 22,352), none in the accessor class. The 1.0.0 library of chain `401e0eebe72f` gives 1,066 pixel shaders the entry: 994 `read_only`, 72 `standard`. In the Spirit Temple (M2 Pro, macOS 27.2, headless, standing, 2026-11-28) no draw took the `read_only` entry: no pipeline with the key's `PSO_EARLY_RO` was built in three runs. `DM_AB=600 DM_NVN_EARLY_DEPTH=ab` there gave GPU p50 20.86 / 20.88 / 20.85 / 21.15 ms off against 20.90 / 20.84 / 21.06 / 20.77 on, and 30.99 / 31.00 against 31.01 / 30.94 in the scene's heavier part, which covers the 72 `standard` shaders at most; the off phases' own keys recompiled 2,681 pipeline bases whose pixel shader has the entry. Open: a scene with read-only depth draws of these shaders (foliage, particles, decals) is not measured.
- **Open:** the profile's prewarm list (`pso_manifest.tsv`) was recorded before the key had the field, so a read-only draw of these shaders finds no prewarmed pipeline: it is built at the draw, or a hybrid stand-in takes the offline archive's `main0_early` base, until the list is recorded again.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/tools/tests/test_dmsh_pool_arrays.py` (which shaders qualify, the accessor), `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_side.py` (no side entry points for `main0_early`), `t_feedback` (`pso_early`'s gates, the key and `pso_desc`'s choice), `t_pso_skey` (the key's rows), `python3 -m unittest dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (both bases planned).
- **Found on:** Cyberpunk 2077 (Switch 2 update `v262144`), upstream nxnative 1a54371c.

### Dead-code removal dropped the inputs of atomics

- **Cause:** Ryujinx's `Optimizer.RemoveNode` removes a dead node, then follows each source whose last use went away into the node that produced it. It did so even when that producer has side effects, such as an atomic or a call whose result is unused. Such a producer stays in the block, but its sources lost their uses, so the next dead-code pass deleted the chain that feeds it, and the atomic read undefined values.
- **Fix:** general: `RemoveNode` follows only into producers without side effects (`HasSideEffects`). This follows MacNativeGames' translator patch 0002.
- **Found on:** no game on hand so far. Rebuilding the chain changed no shader for this reason in Tears of the Kingdom, Breath of the Wild, Wonder, Scarlet or ANIMAL WELL (94,717 shaders); every MSL change was the early fragment tests attribute.

### Dead-code removal dropped a coordinate of a multi-output instruction

- **Symptom:** a simulation or filter that reads neighbouring texels misbehaves though every constant is right. In the translated MSL a variable is declared with no value and read at once (`int _139; int _141 = _139;`), used as a coordinate. On Ocarina of Time: grass whose blades shift at 10 Hz, with a 6-frame cycle in the depth buffer and a wind velocity texture of strong stripes.
- **Cause:** the same walk as the entry above. `RemoveNode` also followed a producer with another destination still in use. An image read with a dead `.x` and a live `.y` stays in the block, but its coordinates lost their use, and the next pass deleted the chain computing them. So the read took an undefined coordinate. Ocarina of Time's divergence step `04942f42cae10d0f` read its `y - 1` neighbour at a garbage row, so the wind's pressure solve never settled.
- **Fix:** general: `Optimizer.cs` `RemoveNode` follows only a producer all of whose destinations are unused (`DestHasNoUses`). A chain change.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py DeadChannel`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 8 of its 42,845 shaders, among them the wind solver and two NDSR upscaler variants. Mario Kart World: 3 compute shaders, noted open earlier.

### Position invariance across shaders

- **Symptom:** the ground kept stale 32×32 blocks where the G-buffer draw failed the depth test: blocks of a stale image where a depth pre-pass and a later pass with other vertex shaders meet (Odyssey: 32×32 blocks on every fourth frame). With mesh shaders: white shapes on plants for single frames, at other places each frame while the leaves move (Ocarina of Time).
- **Cause:** a game may draw the same geometry with two programs and rely on identical depth, for example a depth pre-pass and then a less-or-equal G-buffer pass. Maxwell evaluates identical position math identically; Metal only does for outputs marked `[[invariant]]` (the translation marks `gl_Position`) when compiled with `-fpreserve-invariance`. Without it, Metal compiled two vertex shaders' identical position math to slightly different depths, so fragments of the second pass failed a less-or-equal depth test against the pre-pass and those pixels kept the previous content.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` passes `-fpreserve-invariance` to every compile. A mesh shader's position is marked too: the mesh stage wrapper (`dmsh_meshstage.to_stage`) declares `[[position, invariant]]`, as the translation does for `gl_Position`.
- **Found on:** Super Mario Odyssey (Mac): the Cap Kingdom ground. Presents 4k were brighter and blocky, 4k+1 and 4k+2 clean, 4k+3 partly blocked, repeating to the decimal across runs. The blocks were already in the HDR target when the G-buffer pass ended, and only with the ground's material (`b6384a85afd3ef94`) drawn. In some frames the game first draws a depth pre-pass (`alRenderDepthOnly`) for objects its occlusion queries report visible (the queries rotate through three slots, hence the cycle), then draws the G-buffer with other vertex shaders and depth test less-or-equal, without clearing the colour targets.
  [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (Mac): the foliage in Kokiri Forest is drawn twice by two different mesh shaders, a depth pre-pass (depth LESS, no colour) and a colour pass with depth EQUAL and no depth write. Pixels where the colour pass missed EQUAL showed white for one frame; how the white reaches the picture was not traced (the scene target's clear value, alpha 0, is the likely source). Without the mark the wrapper's position was a plain `[[position]]`. With it: no flashes in five 121-frame windows on an M4 Max (14 on main), none in 240 frames at a flashing flower on an M4 Pro.
- **Note:** ruled out on the way: the TBDR optimizations, same-pass feedback, the present path. Evaluating the game's conditional rendering on the CPU from earlier frames' results made it worse (the condition belongs to the same frame on the GPU), so the host still ignores the condition there and draws.

### A NaN colour under a zero blend weight

- **Symptom:** white clouds over the ground, coming and going (Ocarina of Time: over the ground mist between Kokiri Forest and Hyrule Field, and over the ferns at the camera in Kokiri Forest while they sway). The scene target holds NaN pixels after a fog or lighting pass and bloom spreads each into a white blot; dropping that pass removes them.
- **Cause:** NVIDIA's float blender has an option, "zero times anything is zero" (`SET_BLEND_FLOAT_OPTION`, method 0x670 of the 3D engine, bit `ZERO_TIMES_ANYTHING_IS_ZERO` in NVIDIA's published class headers), and NVN's driver switches it on in its start-up command list: the word `0x80010670` (an immediate write of 1 to that method) is in the `sdk` module of Ocarina of Time (`sdk+0xbb81ac`, among other immediate writes) and of 12 of 14 other games looked at. A blend term whose weight is 0 is then 0 whatever the colour, so a pixel shader may write a NaN colour with alpha 0 under SRC_ALPHA / ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA blending, its way of adding nothing to that pixel, and the target keeps its value. Apple's blender multiplies as IEEE does and stores NaN (measured on an M6: NaN, +Inf and -Inf at alpha 0 all store NaN; only a factor of ZERO itself leaves its term out), which every later pass reading the target carries on. It is not Direct3D's rule: Direct3D 11.3's specification (17.5) wants NaN kept through blending, 0.0 weights included. The NaN itself is the game's arithmetic, translated exactly: Ocarina of Time's mist shader averages hit positions over a layer whose density is 0 there, a positive hit count with a weight sum of 0 (1/0 × 0), and its FMULs carry no `.FMZ`.
- **Fix:** the rule is the blender's, so it follows the draw's blend state. `dmsh_rewrite.py` `blend_zero` gives every pixel shader with a `float4` colour output a guard before each return of `main0` and a word from the host at fragment buffer `DMSH_BLEND_BUFFER`, two bits per colour output; the build runs it before it makes the other entry points, which call `main0`'s body and take its parameters, so all of them have it. The host (`nvn_pso_desc.inc` `bz_word`, `bz_bind`) sets an output's bits for a floating-point target that blends by a sum or difference with a source colour factor of SRC_ALPHA (zero where alpha is 0) or ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA (zero where alpha is 1), and whose destination factor does not read the source colour. There the guard returns a non-finite rgb as 0, what the zero weight makes of it. With blending off, MIN / MAX, another factor or another target format the bits are 0 and the colour is returned as it is: NVIDIA stores that NaN or Inf too. A finite colour and the alpha are never changed. `DM_NVN_BLEND_ZERO=0` gives 0 for every draw. A draw whose pipeline maps its outputs to other attachments (a pass that reads its targets from the tile: physical target i holds output `lmap[i]`) takes both words under that map, as its pipeline's blending does: `draw_` clears `g_pso_lmap` once it has the pipeline, so it keeps a copy and binds the fragment state itself (alpha test, both words, logic operation) after the shader's arguments, passing `bz_bind` that copy. Until then `bz_bind` ran inside `encode_args`, after the clear, and read the words as for an unmerged pass, and an output drawn into a float target at another attachment number got 0 and kept its NaN; no draw on main maps an output to another number today (`fb_tile_plan` maps each to itself), so no game's picture changed. A pipeline whose two words (this one and the rounding word of [the normalized colour rounding](rendering.md#float-colours-rounded-into-normalized-targets-as-nvidias-colour-unit-does)) are both 0 for each of its targets is built without the guard (upstream nxnative `3cf75061`, PR #3176): `dm_blend0` returns at once under the function constant `dm_blend_off` (`DMSH_BLEND_OFF_FC`), which `nvn_pso.inc` `pso_off` sets true (`FN_OFF_BLEND`, beside `FN_OFF_BORDER`) when `pso_blend_live` finds both words 0 from the pipeline key alone (`bz_word_for` / `rop_word_for`, not the current pass). The pair archive keeps it false (`pso_offline.py` `HOST_CONSTANTS`, `pre_fn`, `shader_load`), so one base still serves every blend state of a pair as a stand-in (`hybrid_get`, `pso_bg_look`'s pulling lookups); mesh pipelines keep the guard.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_blend_zero.py` (the guard and the word in every entry point through `dmsh_side.unit` and `dmsh_fetch.fold`, what `dmsh_entry.fs_uint` reads of a shader unchanged, the rule, each text compiled by Metal's compiler); `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_blend_zero` draws NaN, ±Inf and 2 at alpha 0 and 1 through the replay into an RGBA16F target under seven blend states and reads it back, with the rule and with `DM_NVN_BLEND_ZERO=0` (Apple's blender as it is). It also draws a merged pass (output 0 to attachment 1, an RGBA16F target beside an RGBA8 one) and reads back that the NaN and Inf of alpha 0 are zeroed, with the words `bz_bind` bound (`g_bz_last`).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (Mac, [issue #1839](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1839), PR #2030): the half-resolution mist shader `5ad65c0eefd09c7f` wrote NaN into its RGBA16F target (about 15,000 to 229,000 pixels a frame in three reports), the mist composite `888c1068ac8f7043` carried it into the R11G11B10F scene and bloom made the clouds. Not reproduced on the Mac this was written on (an M6): the village route from the reference save and two passes over the village floor with the camera low and high gave 0 non-finite pixels with `DM_NVN_BLEND_ZERO=0` too (`DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`), so the fix is checked there by the replay test, in the mist's blend state and target format, and by the word being set in play (output 0, on more than 131,000 passes or states of one run). Its cost, standing on the village deck with the resolution fixed (`DM_NVN_GPU_TS=submission`), three runs a side: GPU p50 10.86 ms on main's libraries, 10.95 ms with the guard in every pixel shader (10.91 ms with the word 0 for every draw), submit and present 5.67 against 5.73 ms, 60 fps both; the difference is about the spread between runs (0.06 and 0.11 ms).
- **Open:** a NaN or Inf already in the target under a zero destination weight, and a non-finite weight on a colour of 0, which a shader cannot see; the alpha channel's own weights; the Vulkan and OpenGL shader paths (whether those drivers set the option is not known).
- **Note:** found with `DM_NVN_SNAPRAW` and `DM_NVN_TEXSNAP` (raw floats of every colour target after chosen passes of one frame) and `DM_NVN_MAGSCAN`, then the one shader instrumented beside the library (`DM_MSL_DIR`): the first NaN at `1/w × v` with `w = 0`, every NaN texel stored with alpha 1 (source alpha 0). Ruled out on the way: the fog volumes (finite), FMZ handling (none in the code), stale uniforms (`DM_NVN_UB_DEFER=0`, `DM_NVN_UBP_CHECK`), the depth test, `-fmetal-math-mode=relaxed`. The driver's command word was found by scanning the modules for writes to method 0x670; the code that sends the list was not disassembled, and no console was measured.

### The blend helper's output reference on iOS

- **Symptom:** pixel shader compilation fails with `reference type must have explicit address space qualifier` at `dm_blend0(main0_out& dm_o, ...)`: every iOS build, and a Mac build made on macOS 26. A Mac build made on macOS 27 compiled, so the fault passed where it was written.
- **Cause:** `blend_zero` generated a reference without an address space (the output is a local variable of the fragment function). Metal's language version follows the deployment target: the iOS build names iOS 18, whose language requires the qualifier, and the Mac compile named none, so it took the build Mac's system. Measured with Metal front end 32023.922 on macOS 27.2, a shader with that signature: it compiles with no minimum and with iOS 27.0, and fails with `-mmacosx-version-min=26.0`, `15.0` and `-mios-version-min=18.0`. One chain version therefore gave different results on two Macs.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` declares the helper's output parameter `thread main0_out&`; the helper's behavior is unchanged. Every Mac compile of a game's shaders now names the Mac host's minimum macOS (`MACOS_MIN` in `dm/mac/min_os.mk`, read as `dmsh_chain.MAC_MIN`): `MAC_METAL` (then in `dmsh_build.py`, now in `dmsh_chain.py`: [Tests compiled with copies of the Mac compile](#tests-compiled-with-copies-of-the-mac-compile)), as the host's own libraries already did ([The host's own shaders compiled from source at run time](#the-hosts-own-shaders-compiled-from-source-at-run-time)). The file is part of the chain version and the flag is in each shader's cache key. `test_dmsh_blend_zero.py` compiles the plain, integer-output, side and readable-tile variants with both targets' flags, so the Mac case fails on any Mac.
- **Found on:** The Binding of Isaac: Rebirth: all 10 pixel shaders failed on iOS, while its 9 vertex shaders and all 19 Mac shaders compiled (macOS 27). [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 22,296 of 42,848 Mac shaders failed on two Macs with macOS 26 ([GitHub issue #2272](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2272)); 42,848 built there with the qualifier (a reporter's run).

### Tests compiled with copies of the Mac compile

- **Symptom:** three translator tests (`test_dmsh_gather_offsets.py`, `test_dmsh_wrapv.py`, `test_dmsh_raw_last.py`) failed with a `NameError` or a non-literal value once the Mac compile named its deployment target, and three others compiled with flags the build does not use: `test_dmsh_tg_fence.py` without `-fwrapv` and the deployment target, `test_image_atomic_bounds.py` without `-O1`, `-fwrapv` and the deployment target, `test_shader_pch.py` with a hand copy that would not follow a change.
- **Cause:** the Mac compile command, `MAC_METAL`, was a line of `dmsh_build.py`, a script that reads its arguments and starts a build when imported, so tests took the line out of its source and evaluated it (which broke when the line named `dmsh_chain.MAC_MIN_FLAG`) or copied its flags.
- **Fix:** `MAC_METAL` is defined in `dmsh_chain.py`, beside the deployment target it names, with each flag's reason; that module imports without side effects beyond reading `dm/mac/min_os.mk`, and both files are in the chain version. `dmsh_build.py` (its record and its direct run) and every test take `dmsh_chain.MAC_METAL`. The command is unchanged, word for word: a full build of Bastion on both chains gave byte-identical Metal sources and libraries for the Mac (170) and the iPhone (170), the reflection files differing only in the chain name. `test_mac_metal.py` checks the command, its deployment target and that `dmsh_build.py` keeps no copy. Since the step 15 audit, an import of `dmsh_chain.py` reads nothing: `MAC_MIN`, `MAC_MIN_FLAG` and `MAC_METAL` are its module attributes (`__getattr__`, PEP 562), read from `min_os.mk` when used, so a tool can import the file for its list (`FILES`) without running it (`upstream_sync.py` does; `test_mac_metal.py` imports the file alone, with no `min_os.mk`: the list is there, the flags raise).
- **Found on:** no game; found when f75ac06e changed the line and in a review of the shader tools.

### gl_FragCoord.z against the depth stored for its own surface

- **Symptom:** dark specks flickering in a ground mist, a few pixels each, at different places every frame (Ocarina of Time in Kokiri Forest; the console shows none). Dropping the mist removes them, as does dropping its depth-only pass.
- **Cause:** a pixel shader tests its fragment against a depth image of the same surface, which an earlier pass stored. The mist's fragment shader `5ad65c0eefd09c7f` discards when `gl_FragCoord.z` is above the depth its depth-only pass (fragment `4757b5b373a5406e`, the same geometry) stored, so a fragment should never reject its own surface. NVIDIA stores exactly the z a pixel shader reads. Apple's GPU stores a value at or below the fragment's `[[position]].z`, with any vertex or mesh shader and `-fpreserve-invariance`. On an M4 Pro the stored value is lower at 0.2–3.8% of a triangle's pixels and never higher. An M6 stores exactly the fragment's z, as NVIDIA does (`check_t_zsnap`: 0 of 718,400 pixels, 2026-11-09), so the gap belongs to the GPU generation; other generations are not measured. The gap is at most one ulp of z plus 2^-23 of the depth's step across a pixel (`fwidth(z)`). It happens even for triangles of constant depth. With the stored depth one ulp under, the mist discarded its own surface there. The specks went when the depth-only pass wrote `[[position]].z` as its depth, and the mist's own test was the failing one (a raw dump of its 768x432 target held 41,226 zero pixels).
- **Fix:** general, in the chain: `dmsh_rewrite.py` `frag_z_snap`. Where a `gl_FragCoord.z` read in `main0` meets a value read from a texture in one comparison, through any arithmetic on either side, the read becomes `dm_zsnap(gl_FragCoord.z, value, fwidth(gl_FragCoord.z))`. That returns the texture value when it lies in `[z - ulp(z) - 2^-20 fwidth(z), z)` and z otherwise. A comparison of a fragment with its own surface's stored depth then sees equality, as on NVIDIA, whatever its operator or the scale on each side. A surface outside that window, constants, uniforms, and z read in other functions are unchanged. Not chosen: making the depth-only pass write `[[position]].z` as depth. That moves its depth test after the shader, and a stored depth that differs from the rasterizer's fails an `EQUAL` pass on the same geometry (Metroid Dread's G-buffer after its prepass).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_zsnap.py` (the rewrite on the translator's shapes) and `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_zsnap`. The GPU test draws 2,000 triangles depth-only, then reads the stored depth back in a second draw of the same triangles. Five sets: perspective, w = 1, constant depth, depth < 0.001, depth > 0.999. Through `dm_zsnap` no pixel is above its own stored depth (plain z: 9,511 of 718,400 pixels on an M4 Pro, 0 on an M6, where the rewrite changes no result), and a copy 4e-6 farther stays farther at every pixel.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): 39 of its pixel shaders compare z with a texture value, all rewritten. They are the mist's 4 and its foliage's `z * c[188].w > d * c[188].w`. Elsewhere the rewrite changes 158 of OCTOPATH TRAVELER 0's shaders and 122 of Minecraft Dungeons II's, both `z < d`, which the gap left as NVIDIA's. It changes none of OCTOPATH TRAVELER II's 7,005 shaders that read z, nor Three Houses'.

### Front end at -O1: Apple's control-flow lowering

- **Symptom:** making a pipeline archive prints `Max Unstruct ID 758 in function agc.main in shader 13941 exceeds supported limit`, and one pipeline takes many minutes, in `metal-tt` offline or in the host's prebuild on the device (`Preparing pipelines` holding one count for minutes). The line is a note, not an error: `metal-tt` goes on, exits 0 and writes that pipeline into the archive.
- **Cause:** at the front end's default `-O2`, `CorrelatedValuePropagation` and the `SimplifyCFG` after it fold a translated shader's repeated tests of one value into switches whose cases jump past their own join, so nested `if`s become an unstructured graph. Apple's GPU backend lowers unstructured control flow with per-function IDs; past its budget (255 on `applegpu_g13s`, the check in `libapplegpu-nt.dylib` of AIR-TT 32023.921 picks 255 or 65,535 by a capability not yet characterized), it prints the note and lowers the function another way that is far slower and larger. At `-O1` those two passes do not run; the backend still optimizes the AIR itself, so ordinary shaders come out the same.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` compiles with `-O1`.
- **Found on:** Super Mario Odyssey, [issue #276](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/276): the float body of `alRenderMaterialUber` (fragment `007d7e411ef14ebd`, 16,227 `if`s in one function) took 24.6 minutes and 17.3 MB of `g13s` code at `-O2`, 31 s and 1.3 MB at `-O1`. Its `-O1` graph still goes past the budget (642), at that small cost.
- **Check:** random samples of archive bases from seven games (Odyssey, Tears of the Kingdom, Breath of the Wild, Kirby, Luigi's Mansion 3, Wonder, Pikmin 4; 22 to 400 each) compile at `-O1` to vertex and fragment code within a few percent of `-O2` (Odyssey 400 bases: −0.9% / +0.01%; TotK: +0.1% / −1.0%; Kirby: −3.1% / +0.07%), with no `Max Unstruct ID` note at either level, and the front end is faster.
