It changes no ordinary load. Other stages, unrelated storage descriptors, reserved slots and dynamic word offsets keep their existing paths. This is bound to the NVN compute driver layout, with no game/build gate or pool-array workaround.
- **Found on:** Sid Meier's Civilization VI: all 382 deleted accesses in four compute shaders restored. Their later gameplay use has not been exercised.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_compute_uniform_handles.py`; synthetic Maxwell checks both handle orders and descriptor/offset boundaries, and executes a translated fetch on Metal. `t_tbuf_handle` checks production binding of both uniform words.

### A store only a dispatch's first invocation makes

- **Symptom:** objects from a compute-built list flicker: each is in one of two states, on and off irregularly and independently of the others, while the draws are the same frame to frame and the camera is still. Whole groups may vanish or appear in one frame and stay that way until the list's next rebuild. Shots of consecutive frames show the change per frame jumping between two levels.
- **Cause:** a compute shader's preamble `if (-local.x == workgroup.x << log2(local size)) buffer[k] = 0` resets an append counter on the dispatch's first invocation, while the others add to it hundreds of instructions later. Nothing orders them in the API; it works where the first workgroup starts ahead of the rest. Apple's GPUs start workgroups in no order, so a workgroup that appended before the reset lost its entries to the next one's.
- **Fix:** `first_store` in `dmsh_build.py` recognises exactly that condition, for a flat local size or one with rows (the same test on y and x, with workgroup z zero), and takes the stores out of the shader; the reflection's `first_store` has the host make them in order before the dispatch (`nvn_mtl.m`). The stores may be one guarded block each, and a value may be a constant or a local set to one under the same guard. A guarded store it does not understand leaves the whole shader as it is: a first invocation that does more than plain stores (Mario Kart World's `649e36162cad9e79`, whose setup loops over a uniform's count) keeps the race.
- **Found on:** Splatoon Raiders: `029d9c3ec1fcb39f` (`Hoian_Proc`), the plants' instance lists, the only one of the game's 9 compute shaders with atomics that has it. The change per frame over the plants: 1.1, 2.4 or 4.5 at random, to a flat 1.0-1.3; pixels that jumped and returned the next frame: up to 88 % of them, to at most 21 %. The buffers the shader reads alternate frame by frame, which turned out unrelated. Mario Kart World: `2ce9071c2f22d74f` and `5fea9b7ddb30e8a8` (the scenery lists, four guarded blocks) and `0823b08c2027a38d` (local size 8x8), which the first form of the recogniser missed. Trees and bushes vanished in blocks with a still camera; over a title run a rebuild of the list lost 50 % of its entries on average, some well inside their draw range (406, 202, 797, 104 entries in turn), and 4 % after the fix, with no entry lost inside its range among those logged. `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP` of one frame shows no writer, as the list is rebuilt only every few frames: look over many.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_first_store.py`.

### Goto elimination forcing loops around a whole program

- **Symptom:** `metal` runs for hours on a shader of about 100 MB of MSL, mostly indentation, stuck in LICM (`hoistRegion` → `ScalarEvolution::forgetValue`), then the GPU compiler's connection breaks; even `-O0` and finished libraries crash it. Hundreds of forced one-pass loops nest around the program.
- **Cause:** `MoveOutward` in `GotoElimination.cs` encloses what follows an if in an else when it moves a goto out. When another goto jumps into that range, it needs a loop of its own, and each one encloses the others.
- **Fix:** the else is skipped when a goto would have to jump into it (`HasGotoInto`, the goto's own label included). Only shaders that got a forced loop change.
- **Found on:** Splatoon Raiders: 26 such loops around the whole program in its uber shaders: the mid-size shader went from 176 loops to 0, the largest from 364 to 5, and 8 workers stalled for 5 h 40 before that. Three test shaders then compile in 89 s.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_control_flow.py`.

### A loop exit's subgroup block run by the lanes of every iteration together

- **Symptom:** a warp-aggregated atomic in a loop lands for one leader only: PRAGMATA's compute shader `607ceeefb9478700` elects a leader, the lanes with its key leave the loop and reduce with one `SURED.MIN`, and the others go round again; translated, the lanes that left in different iterations voted together, the ballot mixed keys, and the other keys' minimums were lost. Found by an offline differential check of the shader against the instruction stream; no picture traced to it yet.
- **Cause:** `GotoElimination` turns a conditional branch out of a loop into `f = X; if (f) break;` in the loop and `if (false || f) { B }` after it. For one thread that is the same program, but when B has subgroup operations, the lanes that left in different iterations now run it together, where the GPU runs each iteration's lanes as their own group before they reconverge (BSYNC).
- **Fix:** `StructuredIr/LoopExitCohort.cs`, after goto elimination, moves B into the loop, `if (f) { B; break; }`, leaving the reset `f = false` where B was. Only for a B with subgroup operations (directly or in a function it calls), and only in the exact shape where every thread runs the same statements in the same order: nothing but `if (!f)` blocks between the loop and B, no else, no other mention of f in B, no break or continue in B, and f otherwise only ever set to false. A B that ends the program (`return` or `discard`) keeps no break after it: the code generators take a block's last statement as its only terminator, and `return; break;` was SPIR-V with a branch outside any block, which SPIRV-Cross refused (`Trying to end a non-existing block`; Sonic Frontiers' `0e6f8156033ae07e`). Builds translate in debug mode, so the AST optimizer does not carry a ballot from B to a use elsewhere.
- **Entry guards:** a constant condition needs no guard against an undefined value. `GotoElimination.GuardEntryCondition` keeps its direct logical condition; wrapping a constant `false` label condition in a temporary hid the shape above from `LoopExitCohort` and moved its subgroup block out of the loop again (caught by the control-flow tests in CI). Conditions whose definitions a jump may bypass still use the guarded evaluation.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_control_flow.py`: `test_loop_exit_target_with_subgroup_ops_stays_in_the_iteration` fails without the pass; `test_loop_exit_target_without_subgroup_ops_stays_after_the_loop` checks that a block without subgroup operations stays where it was; `test_loop_exit_target_that_exits_has_no_break_after_it`, a B that ends in `EXIT`. A translator A/B over a game's shader packages shows the reach: the changed shaders lose `if (false || f)` blocks and keep their subgroup calls.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA (12 of its 6,856 compute shaders, `607ceeefb9478700` among them); Sonic Frontiers (80 of its 352 compute shaders, none of its pixel or vertex shaders; one B ends the program); Resident Evil 4 (6 of its 7,568 compute shaders). None in Nintendo Switch Sports Resort's compute, mesh and task shaders, Pokémon Sword's or Kirby Star Allies' compute shaders.

### A ballot under divergent control flow sees every lane

- **Symptom:** with KK3 on, as it was below macOS 27 until upstream nxnative f7f128a4, this game drew scattered coloured lights and a black vertical column of geometry on macOS 26.7.3 (issue #3405). A ballot that should hold some lanes of a SIMD group holds them all: a ballot waterfall (a loop that ballots its pending lanes, takes one key a round and lets the lanes holding it leave) never ends once a group holds two keys, a GPU hang or the watchdog; a warp-aggregated count or append under a branch counts every lane.
- **Cause:** two defects of Apple's GPU compiler, which KosmicKrisp (Mesa's Vulkan on Metal) found, reported and works around (`docs/drivers/kosmickrisp/workarounds.rst`). `KK_WORKAROUND_3`: below macOS 27 the compiler if-converts a branch whose ballot result merges after it and runs `simd_ballot` with every lane active (`if (c) m = ballot(true); else m = 2;` gives `0xffffffff`), Apple FB20113490, fixed in macOS 27. The offline front end keeps the ballot in its branch in the AIR; the back end of the running OS miscompiles it, so it follows the OS the game runs on, not the one that built the library. `KK_WORKAROUND_9`: a ballot before the only `break` of a loop, after an election, sees every lane, also at `-O0` and on macOS 27.2.1; that is the shape [`LoopExitCohort`](#a-loop-exits-subgroup-block-run-by-the-lanes-of-every-iteration-together) writes. Apple has not fixed it.
- **Fix:** a workaround for a platform defect, in the chain: `dmsh_metalwa.py`, which `dmsh_build.py` applies to every shader but vertex shaders before the mesh stage, `compute_loops` and the side libraries. KK3 rewrites each `spvSubgroupBallot` as `simd_or(uint(value) << lane)`, the lane ID passed by value (a `thread uint&` met the out-of-line scalarization fault of [#2825](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2825)), in a helper `dm_mwa_ballot` (`static inline __attribute__((always_inline))`) behind the bool function constant `dm_mwa3_fc` (`DMSH_MWA_FC`, undefined: off), which the helper tests itself (`is_function_constant_defined(dm_mwa3_fc) && dm_mwa3_fc`). The host leaves it off wherever a function declares it unless `DM_NVN_METALWA=1` (`nvn_shader.inc` `mwa_on` / `fc_set_mwa`, in `fn_spec`, `fn_off`, `side_spec`, `pre_fn` and `bf_fn`); `pso_offline.py` `HOST_CONSTANTS` specializes it false the same way, and `cps_new_b` looks up a compute function whose constants are all the host's (`fd_host_fc`), so these pipelines stay in the offline archive. KK9 guards every loop `break;` (never a `switch`'s) of a function with subgroup operations with `if ((ulong)simd_active_threads_mask()) break;`, always. A ballot the pass cannot reach fails the shader (`metal workaround rewrite failed`). Found upstream (upstream nxnative b89b2fee, PR #2397), where KK3 was reproduced on an M4 Pro on macOS 26.6.2 and KK9 on 26.6.2 and 27.2.1, and Fast Fusion's offline archive kept all its 1,397 render bases and 85 compute pipelines (15 compute pipelines went to the device without the host's constant).
- **Reach in Ocarina of Time** (the library of chain `b1450d7b27fb`): KK3 rewrites ballots in 467 shaders (220 compute, 113 mesh, 116 pixel, 18 task), KK9 guards breaks in 2,610 (105 compute, 268 mesh, 2,227 pixel, 10 task). The narrow KK9 shape, a ballot inside a loop that breaks, is in 10 of the 20 task shaders (`02953453962129ce` among them) and 8 compute shaders.
- **KK3 off by default** (upstream nxnative f7f128a4, issue #3405): on an M2 Max on macOS 26.7.3 (`DM_PSO_MTL4=0`) upstream saw the symptom above with KK3 on, and 21,300 clean frames with `DM_NVN_METALWA=0` on the same build. The helper was then only `inline`, and `metal -O1` left `_Z13dm_mwa_ballotbj` out of line in the AIR of large `FoliageShader.sharcb` compute shaders: 16 upstream, 120 compute libraries of this tree's 1.0.0 library (chain `14495341b2c9`). It is now `always_inline`. Whether that alone mends the picture on macOS 26, or macOS 26's back end also gets the `simd_or` form wrong, is not known, so KK3 stays off on every OS; upstream's runs with `DM_NVN_METALWA=1` on an M4 Pro on macOS 27.2.1 were clean.
- **Open:** the KK3 retest on macOS 26 with `DM_NVN_METALWA=1` (the app runs from 26.6; no macOS 26 Mac here); with KK3 off, FB20113490 is not worked around below macOS 27. The KK9 guard's cost over those 2,610 shaders is not measured. No picture or hang of this game is traced to either defect itself; the task shaders' KK9 shape is a lead for the [GPU hangs on M2 Macs](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#open), not shown.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game)'s Mac, an M2 Pro on macOS 27.2.1 (metal 32023.922), with upstream's `t_metalwa`: `kk9_break` wrong on 2,048 of 2,048 lanes at `-O1` and `-O0` as built, on none after the rewrite; every KK3 pattern right as built, as macOS 27 should give. KK3's default: this game upstream, on an M2 Max on macOS 26.7.3 (upstream nxnative f7f128a4, issue #3405).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_metalwa.py` (with `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1` it runs `t_metalwa.metal` on the GPU), `python3 -m unittest dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py`, `t_pso_bundle` (the constant's render base, draw pipeline and compute pipeline found in the archive).

### Ampere carries, PLOP3's second result, POPC / FLO immediates, LEA.SX32 and LEA's negated B

- **Symptom:** none met in a game yet: found by differential testing (single instructions with nvdisasm-checked encodings, run on an Apple GPU and compared with an interpreter on Mesa NAK's hardware-checked folds). A 64-bit three-operand add off by 2^32, `POPC R, 0x800000ff` giving 23, a predicate pair from `PLOP3` that reads its own first result, a negative 32-bit index added to a 64-bit address as if positive.
- **Cause:** IADD3 set its two carry predicates to the bits of the carry count, where the first is sum >= 2^32 and the second sum >= 2 * 2^32 (the high word's IADD3.X adds both); PLOP3 wrote its first destination before computing the second; POPC and FLO applied bit 63 as a bitwise not in every form, where in the immediate form (4) it is the immediate's top bit; LEA ignored `.SX32` (bit 73: the high word is the sign extension of a); LEA negated B arithmetically with `.X` (the bitwise not there) and without it left the `+ 1` of `~B + 1` out of the carry.
- **Fix:** `AIadd3`, `APlop3`, `APopc`, `AFlo` and `ALea` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` follow those meanings.
- **Found on:** no game; encodings from the CuAssembler sm_86 corpus, nvdisasm-guided fuzzing and variants.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`CarriesAndModifiers`).

### FSETP / ISETP pairs on their own accumulator, and IMAD's B

- **Symptom:** a predicate pair that disagrees with itself (`FSETP.LT.AND P0, P1, R2, R3, P0`: P1 combined with the new P0, not the old); a multiply-add off by `2 * a * b` (IMAD read as `a * -b + c`).
- **Cause:** FSETP and ISETP wrote their first predicate before computing the second, which reads the accumulator again, as PLOP3 did ([previous entry](#ampere-carries-plop3s-second-result-popc--flo-immediates-leasx32-and-leas-negated-b)). The decoder read bit 63 (bit 75 in the register-B forms) of IMAD, IMAD.WIDE and IMAD.HI as B's negation; IMAD has none there (nvdisasm prints no `-` on B for either bit), only C's negation.
- **Fix:** `ASetPredPair` computes both results before writing either (FSETP, ISETP); `AImadSrcs` gives B without modifiers to the three IMAD forms (`InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`). Ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1800](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1800)).
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA, from PR #1800; no picture or hang here has been traced to either alone.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`SingleInstructionSemantics`: the four fail on the translator before).

### Ampere jump tables read past their end

- **Symptom:** `BRX with no target`, or code about 20 % larger than it should be.
- **Cause:** a BRX's table in c1 stores no length. The decoder read entries until one failed, so a table ran on into the next one; a target of 0 counted as a jump to the start of the program; and it looked only on one path for the `LDC` that loads the table's base, so a base loaded before an if / else join was not found.
- **Fix:** `FindBrxTargetsAmpere` reads a table one new target per pass, so the next table is known before this one overruns it, and stops at the next known base; an entry of 0 is filler for unused cases. `FindBrxTableAmpere` walks every predecessor and takes the base only when all paths reach the same `LDC`.
- **Found on:** Splatoon Raiders: 18 `Hoian_UBER` vertex shaders, 5 of them used at boot.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_control_flow.py`.

### An Ampere jump table loaded in a block split later

- **Symptom:** `BRX with no target found in the c1 jump table`, although the shader's `LDC` of the table's base is on the only path to the BRX.
- **Cause:** `Block.Split` gives the right half the left half's successors but leaves their predecessor lists naming the left half. `FindBrxTableAmpere` walks those lists back to the `LDC`, so when the block holding the `LDC` was split after its successors were linked (a later branch lands inside it), the walk from the BRX started in the left half, before the load, and met another write of the register or the program's start.
- **Fix:** `Block.Split` points the moved successors' predecessor entries at the right half, for both decoders: the Maxwell decoder's walk from a BRX (`FindBrxTargetRange`) reads the same lists. No other code reads them except for their count.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Pokopia: 16 pixel shaders (an `LDC c[0x1][R20+0x790]`, then a conditional BRA to the BRX).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_control_flow.py` (`test_table_loaded_in_a_block_split_later`).

### MUFU.F16

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 308` refused; a neural upscaler's programs missing, so the frame never reaches the window (a black 3D scene).
- **Cause:** MUFU has an F16 form (bit 73): the source is the half that bits 60..61 select (bit 62 its abs, as in every B slot), and the result goes back as the low half of the register, the high half zero. MUFU is the multi-function unit (op 2 EXP2, 3 LOG2, …), not a multiply.
- **Fix:** `AMufu` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`.
- **Found on:** Splatoon Raiders: 10 compute shaders of `agl_ndsr.pck`.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### MUFU.SIN and MUFU.COS of a large number of turns

- **Symptom:** part of the picture without ambient occlusion, bounded by an arc whose centre is off the screen (the top-left corner), the rest correct; on Donkey Kong Bananza a wedge in the lower right of the SSAO's half-size target when dynamic resolution is at its top (1152x648 for a 2304x1296 scene, docked), a fifth of the picture with the effects at full size (1676x943).
- **Cause:** Ampere's `MUFU.SIN` / `COS` take turns and use only their fraction, which is exact for any float. The translator multiplied the turns by 2π in float and took Metal's `sin` / `cos` of that: the product rounds the fraction away (at 2^20 turns a step of 0.4 rad), and Metal's fast `sin` / `cos` return 0 from about 2^20 turns (6.6e6 rad) on, both of them. Bananza's SSAO (`a6f04605183d9915`) turns its kernel per pixel by (10 (3 (x mod y) + x y) + 5.652) / 2π turns; past x y ≈ 659,000 the rotation (cos, sin) was (0, 0), every sample landed on the pixel itself and nothing occluded it. The SSAO runs at half the scene's size, which dynamic resolution moves: at 838x471 (a 1676x943 scene) the product stays below 395,000, no hole, but angles up to 0.23 rad from the hardware's; at 1152x648 (the docked scene's top, 2304x1296) 5,425 pixels of the lower-right corner (0.7%) have no occlusion; at 1676x943 (the effects at full size) 346,009 (22%).
- **Fix:** `AMufu` lowers both as `sin` / `cos((t − rint(t)) · 2π)` (`InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` `ATurnsToRadians`). `t − rint(t)` is exact and lies in [−0.5, 0.5], where fast `sin` / `cos` are accurate, and a small angle stays itself. The angles are the hardware's (within 2e-7), at every size; past 2^21 turns the fraction itself has only 4 values, as on the Switch. Maxwell code is unchanged (its `MUFU` follows `RRO` and takes radians; a huge angle there is open). Any Ampere shader that passes `sin` / `cos` an angle that large, such as a per-pixel rotation built from pixel coordinates, had the same fault; only Bananza was checked. Every Switch 2 game's shaders that use `sin` / `cos` change (8,258 of Bananza's 23,638 shaders in `regress.py check`): a chain change.
- **Found on:** Donkey Kong Bananza, its SSAO: a corner wedge at the top of dynamic resolution (1152x648), and a fifth of the picture with the effects at full size (1676x943, a local code patch of its effects-resolution levels, 0.5 to 1.0).
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> t_mufu_turns && dm/gfx/tests/t_mufu_turns` (the lowering on the GPU against `cos` / `sin(2π · fract(t))` in double over the SSAO's angles at 838x471, 1152x648, 1676x943 and 2560x1440: the earlier lowering collapses 0, 5,425, 345,255 and 1,893,254 rotations); `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`MufuTurns`: the lowered MSL).

### Scaled shared memory addresses

- **Symptom:** a compute shader's results in shared memory land in the wrong elements, or only part of its memory is cleared. An exposure histogram came back with its bins at about four times their index, the scene several stops too dark.
- **Cause:** LDS and STS with a register carry a stride at 78..79, so the register counts elements of 4, 8 or 16 bytes (stride 1, 2, 3), not bytes. The decoder ignored it. The field is a scale, not the access size: stride 3 occurs on 4, 8 and 16-byte accesses alike.
- **Fix:** `AStride` in `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`, for LDL, LDS, STL, STS and ATOMS. LDSM keeps its mode at those bits (1 is a transposed read, MT88), so it takes its address unscaled (`stride: false`): scaled by that mode, Donkey Kong Bananza's upscaler (`7629d857a0a93833`, 12 of its 27 LDSM) read four times too far along, and the picture came out as an 8x8 lattice of garbage for as long as the upscaler ran, with nothing in the log.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave: 2 LDS and 2 STS with a register in its 16,888 shaders, all in the Lighting compute shaders; `d5bc99ffd183fa20` bounds its register by the element count (0x20) before `[R2 * 8]`. Pokémon Pokopia: 921 accesses with stride 1, 68 with 2 and 3,312 with 3, among its 131,426 shaders; `2118ab534fac88c6`'s histogram increments `[R3 * 4]`. Mario Kart World: its froxel shaders (`03923de3c507135e` and 35 variants) keep a float4 a thread at `[R * 16 + 0x500]`; unscaled, pull request #1151 reports the race picture in rainbow bands by depth slice. The stride is not the access size there: `f94552a2a2bff505` stores 8 bytes at `[R9 * 16]` and 4 at `[R9 * 16 + 8]`.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`Shared`: each stride, on 4, 8 and 16-byte stores in the games' encodings).

### Compute shared memory past 32 KB

- **Symptom:** `[RED] failed to create compute pipeline: Threadgroup memory size (49152) exceeds the maximum threadgroup memory allowed (32768)`, thousands a run; the dispatch is skipped. Once moved to device memory: with more than 512 workgroups in a dispatch, or an indirect one, workgroups found other workgroups' data in their shared memory (no game seen doing so).
- **Cause:** Ampere gives a workgroup up to 100 KB of shared memory, and an Apple GPU 32 KB (`maxThreadgroupMemoryLength`). SPIRV-Cross declares the shader's shared memory as `threadgroup` arrays of its full size.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` `tg_spill`: a kernel whose threadgroup arrays exceed `DMSH_TG_MAX` takes them from a device buffer at compute buffer index `DMSH_TG_SLOT` (`dmsh_abi.h`), one slice of their total per workgroup, at the workgroup's index in the whole grid (in 64 bits). Its threadgroup barriers order device memory too (`mem_device | mem_threadgroup`), and the reflection's `tg_spill` gives the bytes per workgroup. `nvn_dispatch.inc` `dispatch` binds a slice of the submission's arena for every workgroup of the dispatch. An indirect dispatch's grid is in GPU memory: the replay waits for the GPU, as an indirect-count multi-draw does (`mdi_wait`), and reads it first, logging the first such dispatch.
- **Measurement:** before, the slice was the workgroup's index modulo 512, on the assumption that workgroups 512 apart are not on the GPU together, and an indirect dispatch always got 512. Apple's GPUs start workgroups in no order. On an M6 (12 GPU cores), 64-thread workgroups ran up to 537 at once, and groups 512 apart overlapped from 1,024 workgroups on: 2 to 5 of 1,024 and 187 to 427 of 16,384 found their slice taken by another. The 48 KB kernel of the check, rewritten that way, got 126,988 of 262,144 sums wrong over 1,024 workgroups. Pull request #1242 found it, without a game's symptom; Mario Kart World never dispatched more than 3 workgroups of these shaders (below).
- **Found on:** Mario Kart World: 168 compute shaders of `DrivePassShaderDefault` declare 48 KB (`spvUnsafeArray<uint, 12288>`), 84 of them with barriers and none with shared atomics; all 168 compile rewritten. From the title to the first race all their dispatches are direct, of 1 to 3 workgroups, 6 a frame in the race.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_tg_spill.py` (the rewritten kernel compiles where the Metal compiler is installed, and with Xcode runs over 1,024 workgroups on this Mac's GPU, each summing data its workgroup wrote to its shared memory); `make -C dm/gfx/tests check_t_tg_spill` runs `dispatch` over 1,024 such workgroups, direct and indirect, the indirect counts written by the GPU after the CPU's copy named one workgroup.

### Compute dispatches past the pipeline's thread limit

- **Symptom:** on an M1 or M2 only, `[RED] compute pipeline for <key> allows 384 threads a threadgroup, its local size 8x8x8 needs 512 (its dispatches are skipped)`; before the host checked, the pass wrote nothing with no red line and no GPU error: its output kept its old contents (zeros for a volume the pass alone fills). Ocarina of Time loses Kokiri Forest's and Hyrule Field's haze and light shafts (the scene's upper half about 3.7 times darker); the same library is right on an M3 or M4, and the same MSL is right at `-O0`.
- **Cause:** a Metal compute pipeline's `maxTotalThreadsPerThreadgroup` is not always 1,024. On GPUs without Dynamic Caching (`applegpu_g13*`, `applegpu_g14*`: the M1 and M2, and the iPhone GPUs of their generation) it falls with the registers the kernel takes: 1,024 threads at up to 52 32-bit registers, 768 at 68, 640 at 80, 512 at 104, down to 384 at the most a thread can take, 128. The GPU compiler picks the registers without knowing the size the host will dispatch, and the host dispatches the Switch shader's local size (`nvn_mtl.m`), so a kernel compiled past that size's budget gets a dispatch larger than its pipeline allows, and no invocation runs. Ocarina of Time's froxel light injection `9ed48d1011f1b48b` (local size 8x8x8) takes 104 registers on `applegpu_g14g` with plain uniform reads, exactly the 512-thread budget, and 128 once its 195 reads at computed offsets are [bounded as selects](#reads-past-a-uniform-bindings-size) (918e9f34), so its six 128x72x96 volumes stayed zero; seven more froxel kernels of the game (8x8x8) are in the same state. Any change that adds registers to such a kernel can tip it over. On `applegpu_g15*` and `applegpu_g16*` every size up to 1,024 fits (96 registers at most, spilling past them).
- **Fix:** every game, a chain change. `dmsh_rewrite.max_threads`, called by `dmsh_build.py` for every compute kernel after the other rewrites, puts the local size on the kernel as `[[max_total_threads_per_threadgroup(N)]]` when it is above 384. The front end passes it to the GPU compiler (`air.max_work_group_size`), which then fits the kernel's registers to N and spills what does not fit, as it does for a pipeline descriptor's `maxTotalThreadsPerThreadgroup`. Sizes up to 384 are left as they are: no register count can refuse them, and on `applegpu_g13*` / `applegpu_g14*` the attribute changes the code of most kernels that already fit. On `applegpu_g15*` / `applegpu_g16*` the GPU code is the same byte for byte. The host compares each guest compute pipeline's `maxTotalThreadsPerThreadgroup` with the shader's local size when it builds the pipeline (`nvn_mtl.m`), and skips that shader's dispatches with the red line above when the pipeline is too small, so a kernel the rewrite misses is named in the log instead of drawing nothing.
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) ([issue #2425](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2425), M2 and M2 Pro). Measured offline with `applegpu-nt` (Metal toolchain 32023.922, `-arch applegpu_g14g`) on `9ed48d1011f1b48b`'s MSL: 128 registers as built, 104 with the attribute; the plain-read and branch forms (which worked on the M2) 104 and 100; at `-O0` 48. With `applegpu_g16s` (M4 Pro) the code with and without the attribute is identical. Across the games built here, on chain 2b9f460cf42c (`metal-tt` 32023.922, the registers from the GPU binary's metadata, the limit taken as 53,248 registers in steps of 128 threads): kernels above 384 threads past their size's budget without the attribute on `applegpu_g13s` and `applegpu_g14g` alike, [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) 8 of 20 (the same eight an M1 Pro measured at 384 threads), PRAGMATA 73 of 255 (69 of them 1,024-thread kernels at 56 to 128 registers), Minecraft Dungeons II 1 of 39 (`ce4e9fdf514d0196`, 8x8x8, 124 registers), OCTOPATH TRAVELER II 0 of 19 and Fire Emblem: Three Houses 0 of 8; with it none, every kernel at its budget or under (52 registers at 1,024 threads, 104 at 512), and the `applegpu_g16s` code of all 341 the same byte for byte.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_max_threads.py` (the sizes it marks and leaves; with Xcode, the rewritten kernel's IR names its size with `dmsh_build.py`'s command, and the plain one none).

### Uniform references past the first kilobyte

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader <key>_b reflection conversion failed: fetch call 0: frag_pos uniform reference out of range`; the shader does not load and its draws are skipped.
- **Cause:** the reflection keeps a uniform it reads on the host (a pixel-position sample's scale, an interpolated output's affine terms) as slot, vector and component in 16 bits, which held vectors up to 63, the first kilobyte of a uniform buffer; a uniform buffer is 64 KB.
- **Fix:** such references are 32 bits (`nvn_refl.m`): slot << 16 | vector << 2 | component, vectors 0 to 4095.
- **Found on:** Mario Kart World: pixel-position samples whose scale is at c[6] vector 989; 611 load failures in a run of its title.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_refl check_t_feedback`.

### HADD2 and HMUL2 with an immediate

- **Symptom:** the GPU hangs (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`) from the first 3D scene on, or frames of about 88 ms, in a shader with a half-float loop.
- **Cause:** the two-source half-pair operations took their second source from the operand order HFMA2 needs: in forms 2, 3 and 7 that is the unused register field at bit 64, not the immediate, constant or uniform register. A blur stepping its loop with `HADD2 Rd, Rd, imm` stepped by whatever R0 held. An immediate also took modifiers from its own bits 62 and 63, flipping the sign of a negative constant.
- **Fix:** `AHalfSecond` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` for HADD2, HMUL2, HSETP2, HSET2 and HMNMX2; `AHalfSrcs` leaves an immediate's bits alone.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Pokopia: 25,726 of its shaders use those forms; `85446a6753300fc8` is the blur.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`Pokopia`: constants, a negative immediate's sign) and `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`HalfImmediate`: on a live register).

### HFMA2 .H0_NH1

- **Symptom:** wrong shading where a shader needs a cross product or a 2D rotation of half pairs; on Eden-NS2, which first read the mode as a plain negation of the high lane, sun and cloud shadows were lost.
- **Cause:** bit 86 of HFMA2 rotates B: the name gives the lanes high first, so the high lane reads B's low half and the low lane B's high half negated. The decoder ignored the bit.
- **Fix:** `AHfma2` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`. The lane order comes from Eden-NS2's fix (checked there against Switch 2 footage), not from a shader that proves it here.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Pokopia: 10,177 instructions in 7,590 programs. The fix made no visible difference in its tutorial area.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Global memory: descriptors, ATOMG.CAS, INC / DEC

- **Symptom:** a global load, store or atomic at the wrong address in code that uses `desc[URb][Ra.64]` (the descriptor's value added to the address); a shader with `ATOMG.CAS` refused (`Ampere instruction 3a9 ... is not implemented`); an `ATOMG.INC` / `ATOMG.DEC` / `RED.INC` / `RED.DEC` with no effect.
- **Cause:** `AGlobalAddress` added the uniform register in every uniform form. nvdisasm (sm_86) names two forms: `[Ra.64+URb]`, where URb is part of the address, and `desc[URb][Ra.64]` (bit 76 of LDG / STG, bit 71 of ATOMG / RED), where it is a memory descriptor (the compiler's cache policy) and the address is Ra.64 alone. Unreal Engine 5.4's bounded read (`LDG.!P0 R5, [R5 + UR4]`, `GuardedWrites`) is the first form and is unchanged. Opcode 0x3a9 was not registered; INC and DEC have no case in `EmitAtomicOp`.
- **Fix:** `AGlobalAddress` takes the form's descriptor bit; `AAtomgCas` (0x3a9, 32 bits: Rb expected, Rc at 64 the new value, the old value returned); `AGlobalIncDec`, a compare-and-swap loop as ATOMS's, for ATOMG and RED.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`GlobalMemory`: the three fail on `main`). A GPU cross-check of 300 LDG / STG / ATOMG / RED from an nvdisasm-validated corpus (sm_86 and sm_80 compiled code), as one-thread compute shaders through a gmem_map the harness builds as the host does (`dmsh_abi.h` `DMSH_GMEM_MAP_*`), production options: 135 identical to 300. Which forms NVN's compiler emits for games is not known here: the descriptor form is CUDA's usual one, and no game note in this repository shows it.
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run.

### Maxwell ATOM.CAS

- **Symptom:** the shader build counts `Shader instruction AtomCas is not implemented.` among the translator's messages, and the shader still loads: its global compare-and-swap does nothing and its result register keeps its old contents (a hash-table insertion or a lock-free update that never lands).
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's Maxwell `AtomCas` only logged. Its fields, from envytools' `gm107.c` (the 0xeef0 row: `atommem0_imm`, `tabeef0sz`) and nvdisasm's sm_61 records in CuAssembler: Rd at 0, Ra at 8, Rb at 20, a signed byte displacement at 28..47, `.E` at 48, `.64` at 49. `ATOM.E.CAS R3, [R2], R10, R11` compares the word with Rb, swaps in Rb + 1 and returns the old word (CUDA atomicCAS); the second source is implied. Every recorded form has an even Rb and bits 50..51 (Ryujinx's `BcRz`) clear.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`InstEmitMemory.cs` `AtomCas`): a global compare-and-swap on the 40-bit address, Rd the old word, through the same storage buffer or pool table lowering as `ATOM`. The 64-bit form (Metal has no 64-bit compare-and-swap), set bits 50..51 and an odd or RZ Rb fail translation (`ATOM.CAS at 0x…: only the 32-bit form …`), as unsupported image atomic forms do; no compiled code has them.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 -m unittest -v dm/tools/tests/test_global_memory.py`: the old word, the swap, a mismatch, both displacement signs, predication, a 32-bit address, Rd equal to Ra, Rb or Rb + 1, Rd = RZ, and the pool table route, all 1,024 words of the buffer checked on the GPU (`dm/gfx/tests/t_global_memory.m`); the rejected forms fail. Each `ATOM.CAS` case fails on the translator before the fix.
- **Found on:** EA SPORTS FC 27: compute shader `90ce32c070f208a5` (`ATOM.E.CAS R6, [R6], R8, R9`), the only `ATOM.CAS` in the Maxwell games' shaders on this Mac's drives; not met in the menus or a match, so no change on screen is known. Retranslating the game's 3,222 compute shaders changed that one alone. Also reported in LOLLIPOP CHAINSAW RePOP (pull request [#684](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/684), whose profile is not in this repository), in a feedback-compaction compute shader on its prologue route, with no measured change on screen.

### A negative displacement on a 40-bit global address

- **Symptom:** a load through a pointer the shader read from memory gives 0, and a store or atomic through one does nothing, when the instruction has `.E` and a negative displacement (`LDG.E R4, [R2+-0x4]`); a `RED` with a negative displacement does nothing on either route.
- **Cause:** two errors in Ryujinx's Maxwell global addresses. `Get40BitsAddress` added the displacement to the low word and the carry out of it to the high word, but no sign: a negative displacement is 2^32 − k unsigned, whose add carries unless the low word borrows, so the high word came out one too high, 4 GB up. Accesses the translator turns into a descriptor's storage buffer take their offset from the low words and were right; the memory pool table's lookup takes the whole address ([Global memory at an address loaded from memory](#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)), finds no pool there and drops the access. And `Red` passed its 20-bit displacement unsigned (`atommem0_imm` is signed, as `ATOM`'s), 1 MB on instead of back.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`InstEmitMemory.cs`): a negative displacement adds −1 to the high word with the carry; `Red` sign-extends its displacement.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_global_memory.py` (above): its `pointer_*` cases with negative displacements, among them one whose low word borrows (0x2_00000004 − 8), fail without the high-word change, and its `red_*` cases without the sign.
- **Found on:** reviewing the `ATOM.CAS` work of pull request [#684](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/684). Among the Maxwell games' shaders on this Mac's drives, only Sid Meier's Civilization VII has `.E` accesses with negative displacements (one `ATOM` shader, five `LDG` / `STG` in another), and none goes through the pool table, so no game picture is known to change.

### ATOMS.INC / ATOMS.DEC, and ATOMS.CAS's result

- **Symptom:** a shared-memory counter that never moves (`ATOMS.INC`, `ATOMS.DEC`: CUDA's atomicInc / atomicDec, the old value read as 0); a lock-free update on shared memory that loops or takes a wrong branch after `ATOMS.CAS` (its result 0 or 1 instead of the old value).
- **Cause:** `AAtoms` hands operations 3 and 4 to `EmitAtomicOp`, which has no case for them: it logs "Invalid atomic operation" and gives the constant 0, with no access. `AAtomsCas` gave every compare-and-swap CAST.SPIN's result; nvdisasm names three forms by bit 87 (CAS clear, CAST set, CAST.SPIN with bit 88 too), and a plain CAS gives the old value (PTX `atom.cas`).
- **Fix:** INC (`old >= b ? 0 : old + 1`) and DEC (`old == 0 || old > b ? b : old - 1`) are a compare-and-swap loop in `AAtoms`; `AAtomsCas` gives the old value when bit 87 is clear, 1 / 0 for CAST and CAST.SPIN as before (Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's retry loop).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`SharedAtomics`: the three fail on `main`). A GPU cross-check of 400 LDS / STS / ATOMS from an nvdisasm-validated sm_86 corpus, run as one-thread compute shaders with the production options (debug and reduced on, MSL 2.3), against a reference: 84 mismatches (all INC, DEC and CAS) to none; the same on encodings from real compiled code.
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run. Also seen with `debug: false` (the AST optimizer, off by default): a shared store of 8 or 16 bits makes the translator throw, and an ATOMS whose result goes to RZ is dropped.

### FRND of a half (FRND.F16)

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed] NotSupportedException: Ampere 307 at …: FRND of a float that is not 32-bit`.
- **Cause:** the emitter took 32-bit floats only. `FRND.F16` rounds a half to an integer value: both sizes are 1 (bits 84..86 and 75..77), the rounding at 78..80 as for a float.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`AFrnd` in the vendored `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`). The half bits 60..62 select (as F2F's) is unpacked, rounded in 32 bits and packed into the low 16 bits. The pack is exact: every half from 2^10 up is an integer already, and an integer below 2^11 has at most 11 significant bits. That the half is selected as F2F's is taken from F2F, not from a disassembly of a high-half case (all of PRAGMATA's read the low half). A source and result of different sizes still fail.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: 65 shaders (47 compute, 11 vertex, 7 pixel), nearest and floor.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py -k Frnd`.

### Ampere forms met on PRAGMATA: TLD through a uniform register, I2F of an immediate, SUATOM.CAS

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed] NotSupportedException: Ampere instruction f66 … is not implemented`, `Ampere instruction 595 … is not implemented`, or `Ampere 906 …: I2F byte select of a 32-bit source`.
- **Cause:** three forms the emitters did not take. 0xf66 is TLD with its handle in a uniform register (40..46), the fetch beside 0xf60 (TEX), 0xf63 (TLD4), 0xf69 (TMML) and 0xf6c (TXD): its other fields are 0xb66's. That reading is the family's pattern (0xf60 + 3n beside 0xb60 + 3n), not a disassembly. 0x595 is SUATOM's compare-and-swap through a bound handle: Mesa's NAK encodes the bindless ones as 0x394 (atomic) and 0x396 (compare-and-swap, its data the pair compare value, new value), and 0x593 is 0x394 with the handle bound. I2F in its immediate form (0x906) has the value in 32..63, so 60..62 select no byte there; an immediate of -2 read as select 7.
- **Fix:** a chain change (vendored `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`: `ATex` takes 0xf66, `ASuatomCas`; `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`: `AI2f` reads no select from an immediate).
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: 17 shaders with 0xf66 (11 vertex, 6 pixel), compute shader `50fece0d891feba4` with 0x595, 3 compute shaders with the I2F. All build; none has been watched drawing.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py -k I2fImmediate`; the other two forms are checked by PRAGMATA's build only.

### IPA at an offset, and the barycentric coordinates

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed] NotSupportedException: Ampere 326 at …: IPA at an offset`; before the offset is reached, the build's log has `Attribute offset 0x3B4 is not valid` and the read gives 0.
- **Cause:** two things the attribute emitters did not have. IPA's sample location 2 (bits 76..78) interpolates at an offset from the pixel centre, the offset word in the second source register (32..40): x in bits 8..11 and y in bits 24..27, signed, in 1/16 pixel (Mesa NAK's `load_barycentric_offset` builds a signed 12-bit value in 1/4096 per half and clamps it to -0.5..0.4375, so only those four bits vary; GLSLC's code masks them with `0xf00` and `0x0f000000`). And `a[0x3a8..0x3b0]` and `a[0x3b4..0x3bc]` are the pixel's barycentric coordinates, screen-linear and perspective, three words each, Mesa's `NAK_ATTR_BARY_COORD_NO_PERSP_X..Z` and `NAK_ATTR_BARY_COORD_X..Z` (this entry first read the perspective group as two words with a third of 1 - x - y; Resident Evil 4's shaders read `a[0x3bc]`, and Mesa's `nak_private.h` has it there); the perspective pair is read like any perspective attribute, divided by w, and the shader multiplies by 1 / `a[0x7c]` read at the same place.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `AttributeMap.cs` maps both groups to new inputs (`IoVariable.BaryCoord`, `BaryCoordNoPersp`: SPIR-V `BaryCoordKHR` / `BaryCoordNoPerspKHR` with `SPV_KHR_fragment_shader_barycentric`, MSL `[[barycentric_coord]]`), and `EmitIpa` multiplies the perspective pair by `gl_FragCoord.w`. For the offset (`AIpa` passes the register, `EmitIpa`), what IPA.PASS reads (an attribute over w, 1 / w, a screen-linear attribute, the position) is linear over the screen within the primitive, so its value at the offset is the centre's plus the offset times its screen derivatives, which are exact for a linear function: `v + dFdx(v) * ox + dFdy(v) * oy`. That needs no `interpolateAtOffset`, which SPIRV-Cross can give a user attribute only (an `interpolant<>` member), not the built-ins this shader reads. A constant (flat) read and the integer attributes take no offset. Maxwell's IPA has the same location field, not met and not changed.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: pixel shader `4a9d4507eea6c837` reads 1 / w and the perspective barycentric pair at the position of the sample it runs for (the table byte at `c0 + 0x620`, shifted into the offset word); it builds; no draw with it has been watched. No other of its 36,779 shaders has either.
- **Open:** on a multisampled target SPIRV-Cross moves `gl_FragCoord.xy` to the sample, while IPA of `a[0x70]` / `a[0x74]` at the default location is the pixel centre: a shader that adds the sample offset itself (PRAGMATA's 154 sample position readers) would add it twice. Not met: the game's per-sample shaders have been seen on single-sample targets only. Derivatives are taken where the IPA stands; one inside non-uniform control flow has not been met.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py -k IpaAtOffset`.

### LDTRAM: an attribute at each vertex of the triangle

- **Symptom:** `[translation failed]` for Ampere opcode `3ad` in thousands of pixel shaders (Resident Evil 4: 7,246 of its 42,313 shaders did not build).
- **Cause:** 0x3ad is LDTRAM, which reads a user attribute as the triangle's vertices hold it, not interpolated: Mesa NAK's `OpLdTram`, which lowers `load_input_vertex` (fragment shader barycentrics). The attribute address in words is in bits 64..71; with bit 72 clear (`.AB`) Rd gets vertex 0's word and Rd + 1 vertex 1's, with it set (`.C`) Rd gets vertex 2's. The shader weighs the three itself with the barycentric coordinates (`a[0x3b8]`, `a[0x3bc]`, [above](#ipa-at-an-offset-and-the-barycentric-coordinates)). The translator had no emitter for it, and SPIRV-Cross has no MSL form of SPIR-V's `PerVertexKHR`.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs` `ALdTram` loads a new input, `IoVariable.UserDefinedPerVertex` (location = attribute × 3 + vertex, component = the word), which `Declarations.cs` declares as a flat `vec4` named `dm_pv<attribute>_<vertex>` at location 64 + that, past every attribute's own. `dmsh_rewrite.per_vertex` then makes the reads of attribute L one MSL 4.0 `vertex_value<float4>` at `user(locn<L>)`, fed by the vertex shader's output there (which `vs_pad` pads when the vertex shader does not write it), and each read that vertex's `get(vertex_index::first / second / third)`, the vertices `[[barycentric_coord]]` weighs. The Mac's deployment target is already MSL 4.0; `ios_metal.compile` compiles such a source with `-std=metal4.0`, which iOS 26 loads, and every other source as before. A shader that also interpolates the attribute is refused (`per-vertex input rewrite failed`); none is known.
- **Found on:** Resident Evil 4: 7,225 of its 18,248 pixel shaders read generic attribute 1, 2 or 3 this way, never one they also interpolate; most leave it out of their SPH input map.
- **Open:** an iPhone on iOS 18 to 25 cannot load those libraries. Vertex order is taken to match between NVN and Metal; not yet checked against a frame.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_per_vertex.py`.

### The shared compare-and-swap loop retries with the value it got

- **Symptom:** a shared-memory atomic built from a compare-and-swap loop (INC, DEC, the 64-bit and float operations) spins longer than it should or stores a value computed from stale data when other threads write the word.
- **Cause:** the loop `GenerateSharedAtomicCasLoop` (`HelperFunctionManager.cs`) writes reloaded the word at the head of every iteration instead of taking the value the failed exchange returned, which is the word's current value. The reload is a plain load between atomics, with no ordering against them.
- **Fix:** the old value is loaded once before the loop; each iteration computes from it, and a failed exchange's result becomes the next iteration's old value. Ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1796](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1796)).
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA, from PR #1796; not traced to a symptom here.

### Half-pair swizzle on a constant, and .F32

- **Symptom:** wrong values in the high lane of half-pair math that scales by one half of a uniform (`HMUL2 R37, R37.H0_H0, c[0x0] [0x168].H0_H0`), or a garbage operand where a half-pair operation reads a 32-bit float (`R20.F32`).
- **Cause:** `AHalfLanes` took a constant operand as is, ignoring its swizzle (bits 60..61 in the 32 slot, where a constant lives), so `.H0_H0` / `.H1_H1` on a constant gave the high lane the constant's other half. Swizzle value 1 was read as `.H1_H1`; nvdisasm (sm_86) names it `.F32` in all three slots (A 74..75, the 32 slot 60..61, the 64 slot 81..82), the operand's own 32-bit float. Only an immediate has no swizzle: those bits are its own.
- **Fix:** `AHalfLanes` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` applies the swizzle to constants and takes `.F32` as the float itself in both lanes (the lanes are computed in 32 bits already). MUFU.F16, whose bits 60..61 pick one half (`.H0` / `.H1`), keeps its own reading. `.F32`'s exact rounding on NVIDIA (whether the float is rounded to a half first) is not known; the float itself is the closer reading than its top 16 bits.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`HalfSwizzle`: the three fail on `main`), and `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`'s `MufuF16`. A GPU cross-check of 900 HADD2 / HMUL2 / HFMA2 from an nvdisasm-validated sm_86 corpus against an exact binary16 reference (one rounding, FMA included) went from 86 mismatches on constant swizzles to none; what is left is `.FMZ` (0 times infinity). The [half .RELU NaN correction](#hfma2mma-the-half-pair-fma-on-the-tensor-pipe) is shared by ordinary HFMA2 and HMUL2.
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run.

### HSETP2 / HSET2 .FTZ

- **Symptom:** a half comparison with `.FTZ` on a subnormal operand passes where NVIDIA's fails (or the reverse): `HSET2.BF.GT.FTZ R6, R19, RZ` gives 1.0 for a positive subnormal half, 0 on NVIDIA.
- **Cause:** bit 80 of HSETP2 and HSET2 was dropped. `AFtz` (FSETP / FSET) does not fit: the half lanes are unpacked to 32-bit floats, where a half's subnormals are normal, so `FlushDenormal`'s bound never flushes them.
- **Fix:** `AHalfFtz` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` flushes each lane under the half's own bound (2^-14) before the comparison. The arithmetic shares the signed flush described in [Half-pair .FTZ flushes f16 subnormals](#half-pair-ftz-flushes-f16-subnormals).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`HalfCompareFtz`: two fail before the comparison fix). The GPU cross-check with subnormal and zero inputs: 4 HSETP2 / HSET2 mismatches in 400, none with the fix. HMNMX2.FTZ now shares the source flush and has [synthetic boundary coverage](#half-pair-ftz-flushes-f16-subnormals).
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter (NAK's `OpHSetP2` gives the `.H_AND` reading the translator already has); no game run.

### I2F and F2F directed rounding

- **Symptom:** integer division results one off for some operands (the compiler's 32-bit division takes its reciprocal from `I2F.U32.RP`), or a half one ulp off (an overflow giving infinity instead of the largest finite value) after `F2F.F16.F32.RM` / `.RP` / `.RZ`.
- **Cause:** the rounding field (78..79: `.RM` 1, `.RP` 2, `.RZ` 3) was decoded but not applied: I2F and the pack of F2F round to nearest. For a 32-bit integer above 2^24, I2F rounded to nearest is one ulp off the directed result about half the time. A first fix of F2F also met the optimizer's `PropagatePack`, which takes an unpack of a pack for the packed float itself (dropping the half's rounding).
- **Fix:** `AI2f` (32-bit source, 32-bit float) and `AF2f` (F32 to F16) in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` compare the rounded result with the exact value (the integer converted back, exact below 2^31 / 2^32; the half read back through a mask) and step its bits to the neighbour `ADirected` picks. Narrow integer sources are exact; I2F to a half keeps rounding to nearest.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`DirectedRounding`: two fail on `main`). A GPU cross-check against an exact reference (one rounding, IEEE directed modes; NAK's hardware test for F2F.F16.F32.RZ's overflow): I2F 45 mismatches in 300, none with the fix; F2F 25-31 in 300, 6 left, all with results at or below 0x050d (half subnormals and the smallest normals, where the GPU's own conversion differs). Also left: FRND / F2I of a 32-bit subnormal (FLOOR of a tiny negative is -1 on NVIDIA; the GPU flushes it to zero).
- **Found on:** cross-checking the translator against nvdisasm and a reference interpreter; no game run.

### I2F from a 64-bit integer rounds once

- **Symptom:** `I2F` from a signed or unsigned 64-bit register pair to f32 can give the wrong float. A negative integer close to zero can lose its entire value during cancellation.
- **Cause:** `AI2f64` rounded the high and low words separately as floats before adding them, and discarded the rounding mode. The division sequence that introduced the emitter used a zero high word, where the old nearest-rounding calculation is exact.
- **Fix:** form the magnitude as two integer words, normalize to recover the 24 retained bits and the round and sticky bits, and round once in the encoded mode. Set the low bit of the leading-bit search operand to keep the shift counts valid for zero. Return positive zero for a zero input. This applies to the Ampere register-pair form with an f32 destination.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. Signed nearest-even changes from 16 differences to zero across 1,024 recorded Jetson Orin lanes. All 112 synthetic boundary lanes and a separate 36,244-lane exact-reference comparison match on the Mac for signed and unsigned inputs in RN, RM, RP and RZ. All eight synthetic shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile offline for iOS. The directed modes and unsigned conversion are covered by synthetic references. No games or iOS device ran.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2045](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2045). The opcode appears in the [Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave lighting path](#texture-handles-of-sdk-22s-ampere-code).

### F2FP packs BF16 and applies RELU and RZ

- **Symptom:** `F2FP.BF16.PACK_AB` returns a pair of F16 values, and `F2FP.RELU.PACK_AB` keeps negative values. A toward-zero pack can round away from zero or turn a finite value into infinity.
- **Cause:** `AF2fp` used the same nearest-even F16 pack for all `PACK_AB` instructions. It ignored the format, clamp and rounding bits.
- **Fix:** clamp negative inputs, including -0 and -infinity, to +0 for `.RELU`. Preserve NaNs through the clamp and emit canonical `0x7fff` in `.RELU` lanes. BF16 uses the upper 16 input bits, with a nearest-even bias when requested, and canonicalizes NaNs. F16 `.RZ` widens the rounded half's magnitude and steps it back one code when a finite input rounded away from zero. Plain F16 `.RN` and Pokopia's plain `.MERGE_C` keep their existing conversion.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. Recorded differences fall from 1,880 to zero for BF16 and from 913 to zero for F16 `.RELU`, each across 2,048 halves. Plain F16 `.RN` has the same 44 NaN-bit differences (Orin `0x7fff`, Metal `0x7e00`). Six synthetic forms cover BF16 ties, combined BF16 `.RELU` and F16 truncation. All 64 exact boundary halves pass with default and relaxed Mac math, and a separate sweep of 2,072,574 halves also matches its exact reference. Eight shaders compile offline for iOS. Plain F16 `.RN` and both plain Pokopia `.MERGE_C` examples emit identical MSL on main and the fix.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2046](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2046). The new combinations and `.RZ` are checked against synthetic references. No game or iOS device ran.

### IDP integer dot products

- **Symptom:** an Ampere shader using `dp4a` or `dp2a` fails translation with `Ampere instruction 226 ... is not implemented` for the register form.
- **Cause:** the Ampere decoder had no IDP entry for opcode `0x026` in the low nine bits.
- **Fix:** register `InstName.Idp` and use the shared ALU operand reader. `AIdp` extracts the four bytes of each source for DP4A or extracts two halfwords of A and B's low or high pair of bytes for DP2A. Signedness is independent for each source. Integer multiplies and adds accumulate the products into C with 32-bit wraparound. Before optimization the lowering emits 16 IR operations for DP4A and eight for DP2A.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. All five recorded register programs were refused on main and now match all 5,120 Orin lanes. Four synthetic shaders cover the B and C constant-buffer slots for DP4A and DP2A, with all 16 lanes matching using a nonzero Metal argument-buffer word at `c[3][5]`. Another 144 synthetic lanes cover independent signedness, DP2A low and high pairs, wraparound, immediate and uniform-register inputs. All 27 IDP shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile offline for iOS.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2052](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2052). Only register forms have recorded Orin outputs in this fixture. No affected game is documented. No games or iOS device ran.

### VABSDIFF, the absolute difference with an add

- **Symptom:** an Ampere shader using `sad.u32` fails translation with `Ampere instruction 214 ... is not implemented` for the register form.
- **Cause:** the GPU compiler emits `VABSDIFF.U32`, and the Ampere decoder had no entry for opcode `0x014` in the low nine bits.
- **Fix:** `AVabsdiff` uses the shared ALU operand reader and emits `max(a, b) - min(a, b) + c` with 32-bit wraparound. Bit 73 selects signed ordering. Bits 81–83 are the `Pu` predicate field. The recorded form uses `PT`, so other `Pu` values and unrecognized modifiers are rejected. The lowering emits four integer IR operations before optimization.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922. The previously refused unsigned register program now matches all 1,024 recorded lanes, including 416 wrapping additions. All 64 synthetic lanes match, including signed extrema, immediate and uniform-register sources, and 16 lanes using nonzero constants at `c[3][5]` in slots B and C. Separate probes confirm that a non-PT `Pu` and an unrecognized modifier fail translation. Nine supported shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile and link offline for iOS.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2053](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2053). Signed and non-register cases use synthetic references. No affected game is documented. No games or iOS device ran.

### A guarded load whose destination is its address

- **Symptom:** none in the log: everything drawn with an engine's scene data lands on the first instance's transform, or at nothing. In the MSL, a bounds-checked read has a constant index (`vp_s2._m0[0]`) just after a comparison of the register it should use.
- **Cause:** a uniform-form LDG (`0x981`) with a predicate is zero where the predicate is false, so the translator zeroed the destination first and then read the address. With `LDG.P R5, [R5 + UR4]` the address was already gone.
- **Fix:** `ALdg` reads the address into locals before anything is written.
- **Found on:** Yoshi and the Mysterious Book: Unreal Engine 5.4's GPU scene reads, every one of them; the first step away from a black scene.

### A deferred write under a false guard

- **Symptom:** the scene goes white with a yellow fringe: a few objects hold the largest value their target can, and bloom spreads it over the picture. Clamping a shader's terms one by one finds a value that is a register's stale contents. Or characters lit from an environment cube turn bright white in certain places only, and the cube's mean is far above its value elsewhere.
- **Cause:** an instruction with a write barrier [writes through a shadow register](#ampere-shader-code-switch-2), which starts as the register's value and is copied back at the first instruction that waits on the barrier. A guarded one whose guard is false writes nothing on hardware, but the copy still ran, and overwrote whatever had been written meanwhile. Here: `EXP2 R17` under P4, `R17 = R29 * R29` under !P4, then the wait; under !P4, R17 got back a squared distance from long before, and the light's falloff with it.
- **Fix:** `EmitOpsAmpere` keeps the guard as it was at issue in a shadow register of its own and commits only where it held. A predicated last instruction of a block no longer branches straight to the next block when writes are pending, which skipped their commits. A later guarded write no longer discards an earlier pending one, which may still be the one that lands.
- **Found on:** Yoshi and the Mysterious Book: the first light's falloff, 65,000 in R11G11B10 on leaves and eggs. Donkey Kong Bananza (GitHub issue 1100): DK and Pauline white between Hilltop's rocks. The terrain capture shader `7612419c02d496b9` picks its material with `@P0 IPA R8` then `@!P0 MOV R8` as a block's last instruction, and always got material 0, a placeholder with an emissive scale of 30, so the environment cube's red mean reached 53 against about 1.2 elsewhere. A hand patch of that one selection, on the old chain, brought it to 0.45 at the spot with DK normal. On chain 3dd02e581c0c the shader's MSL has the right selection; the spot itself was not revisited on that chain.

### Ampere forms with a handle in a register, and SURED's other operations

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction f63` (or `f6c`, `364`, `367`, `394`) `is not implemented`, or `SURED form 0310e000`, in the build.
- **Cause:** 0xf60 and 0x361 were known for TEX. The same pairs exist for the others: 0xf63 and 0xf6c are TLD4 and TXD through a uniform register at 40..46, 0x364 and 0x367 are TLD4 and TLD through a register, the first of the second source block, each otherwise laid out as its bound form. 0x364's meaning was inferred from these siblings, and its use agrees: Yoshi's single-layer water converts each of its four results as a device Z (`c4[66]`), which only a gather of a depth texture gives. SURED takes SUATOM's operation field (87..91): MAX, OR and MIN on 32 bits turn up.
- **Fix:** `ATex` maps them to the bound forms, `ASured` takes the operation. TMML has the same pair, 0xf69 and 0x36a (Kirby Air Riders' cube map LOD, 4,776 pixel shaders), with a binding of its own only when the handle is bound by its slot. SURED.MAX.U64 (33,550 uses, Nanite's visibility buffer) is now [a 64-bit texture atomic](#64-bit-image-max-and-min-suredmaxu64) on Apple GPU family 9; this game has not been rebuilt with it.
- **TXQ:** 0xf6f is TXQ (0xb6f) through a uniform register at 40..46, Ra the LOD (`ATxq`; PRAGMATA: 12 shaders); the size query reads the pools like any handle no slot holds. Check: `GuardedWrites.test_size_query_through_a_uniform_register_translates`.
- **Surfaces:** `Ampere instruction 394 … is not implemented` (`00321e00001ee014…7394`). Each surface instruction has a form with the handle in a register at 64..72 (Rc), 0x1ff below its bound form, which names a constant buffer word at 40..59: SULD 0x998 / 0x99a, SUST 0x99c / 0x99e, SUATOM 0x394, SUATOM.CAS 0x396 and SURED 0x3a0, the opcodes Mesa NAK encodes (its `sm70_encode.rs` `OpSuLd`, `OpSuSt`, `OpSuAtom`), with the other fields where the bound forms have them. `ASurfaceHandle` gives the register forms a bindless handle from Rc, and bindless elimination finds the constant buffer word the register was loaded from, as for TEX through a register. Orbitals's global compute shaders take Unreal's ShaderPrint counter with SUATOM.ADD.U32 on a 1D buffer image whose handle a MOV reads from `c[3][0]` (`45389d82b79dbf0c` +0x1850: `MOV R12, c[3][0]; MOV R20, R12; SUATOM R12, [R12], R24, R20`): 5 compute shaders stopped there. Only 0x394 is met in a game; the others follow from the same pairing (chain change). Check: `Suatom.test_handle_in_a_register`, `Suatom.test_reduction_with_its_handle_in_a_register`.
- **Found on:** Kirby Air Riders (TMML); Orbitals (SUATOM through a register); Yoshi and the Mysterious Book. Its shaders' jump tables also ran past their code on two compute shaders (`ArgumentOutOfRangeException`, in `GetCode`): an address past the code is now no code, which ends the table as the decoder expected.

### Ampere BMSK and the read of a covered pixel

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 21b at … is not implemented` (`…0405721b`) or `Ampere instruction 925 at 0x0 is not implemented` (`040e0200000e4000 …7925`) in the build.
- **Cause:** 0x21b, beside SGXT's 0x21a, is BMSK: a mask of b bits from bit a. Star Fox's `58046f286de779e1` takes it from two bytes of a word, ANDs it with the value shifted by the same a and hands the result to an atomic, a bit field written into a word. 0x925 has no name in the tables at hand (the part of CuAssembler's sm_80 table read so far; Mesa's NAK encoder could not be fetched); what it reads comes from its two shaders, quad efficiency views. They rebuild its value at the quad's three other pixels through SHFL and FSWZADD and add the four: one indexes a 4-entry palette with the sum minus 1, so a shaded pixel's sum is at least 1, and the other draws 0.03125 + 0.15625 sum in red. So it is 1 for a covered pixel and 0 for a helper invocation.
- **Fix:** `ABmsk` follows PTX's `bmsk` definition, its `.W` at bit 75 as SGXT's (only the clamping form has been seen). `APixelCovered` loads a new `IoVariable.HelperInvocation`, Metal's `simd_is_helper_thread()`, as an integer; any other form of 0x925 stays refused (chain change). `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` covers both.
- **Found on:** Star Fox: 1 compute and 2 pixel shaders, none asked for in play so far, so neither instruction has run. Neither opcode is in the shaders of Donkey Kong Bananza, Splatoon Raiders, Mario Tennis Fever or Pokopia.
- **Form 3, the sample index:** `[RED] … 0x925 form 3 outside a covered pixel's read` for 154 of PRAGMATA's pixel shaders, at their first instruction. The register indexes the driver's sample position table (`LDC c[0][R + 0x620]`, a byte of x and y nibbles), which is what GLSLC's Maxwell code does with PIXLD.MY_INDEX (numbered 5 there), so form 3 is the index of the sample the fragment runs for: `IoVariable.SampleId`, Metal's `[[sample_id]]`. Check: `PixelCovered.test_form_3_reads_the_sample_index`.

### The fragment's shading-rate size reads 1 x 1

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's water or another full-screen surface draws black (or as a flat dark colour under the fog) while its normals and depth are right; a texture dump of the target shows the colour channel at 0 where the surface is, and building the shader without fast maths changes nothing.
- **Cause:** S2UR of system register 0x84 (Mesa NAK's `NAK_SV_VARIABLE_RATE`): the fragment's size in pixels, X in bits 8..15 and Y in bits 16..23, which variable rate shading makes 2 or 4. The translator did not know the register and gave 0, the default for an unknown one. Nintendo Switch Sports Resort's sea shaders divide a blend weight by the fragment's area: 0, an infinite weight, and the colour came out NaN, which `blend_zero` (`dm_az`) writes as 0.
- **Fix:** `SReg.VariableRate` (132, `Decoders/InstDecoders.cs`) reads `0x10100` in `EmitSReg` (`Instructions/InstEmitMove.cs`): the host shades every pixel, so 1 x 1 (chain change).
- **Found on:** Nintendo Switch Sports Resort (`04008E001F098000`, v65536): 612 of its 11,059 shaders, all pixel shaders, read the register; its title screen's sea was black and draws blue with the change, on the Mac.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`ShadingRate`, with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`).

### VOTEU writes the uniform predicate

- **Symptom:** no wrong picture seen so far. In the MSL, a shader with a 0x886 vote has `if (false)` and `false ? a : b` where a uniform predicate guards a value or a branch, and a lane guard on the same number reads `simd_all` or `simd_any` of its own predicate.
- **Cause:** the destination at bits 81..84 of 0x886 is the uniform predicate UPd: nvdisasm prints `@!P1 VOTEU.ANY UP2, P2` for `0x000000000104010000000000003f9886` (CuAssembler's sm_86 records), and NAK encodes `OpVote` with a uniform destination as 0x886 and a lane one as 0x806, with its source and guard always lane predicates. The translator wrote the lane predicate of that number for both. An unwritten predicate reads false, so a uniform guard on the vote never held, and the lane predicate of that number became the subgroup's vote. Across Yoshi and the Mysterious Book, Pokémon Pokopia and The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time, 14,960 of the 17,889 votes that name a destination are next read as a uniform predicate, through a uniform guard, UPLOP3, USEL or PLOP3's uniform source (bit 67), and the 2,929 next read as a lane predicate all have an earlier lane writer. 0x806's next readers are all lane reads.
- **Fix:** `AVote` writes UPd for 0x886 (`dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py`, class `Vote`). No other instruction in CuAssembler's sm_86 tables has a uniform predicate destination without already being registered as uniform.
- **The same fault in a registered one:** UIMAD.WIDE (0x0a5, registered as uniform) wrote the carry out of its 64-bit addition (Pd at 81..84) to the lane predicate, while it read its carry in from the uniform one. `AImadWide` writes UPd for it now (class `ImadWideCarry`; found upstream, upstream nxnative 48c1154d, PR #1548). Ocarina of Time does not use that carry: its 320 UIMAD.WIDE and 140,829 IMAD.WIDE all write PT there, so its MSL does not change.
- **Reach:** every shader with such a vote changes: 7,560 translated of 9,231 in Yoshi (the rest stop at SURED, both ways), all 471 in Pokopia, 321 translated of 329 in Ocarina of Time; none in Mario Kart World, Fire Emblem: Fortune's Weave or Donkey Kong Bananza's 5,219 surveyed. Before, 1,815, 454 and 284 of them had a guard folded to a constant. In the shaders read, the vote is on a value that is one per draw, such as `c3[87].z != 11`, so the old code goes wrong only where that vote is true. Open: no played scene has shown such a vote. In Pokopia's first area the one changed pixel shader drawn, the full-screen `075fd7285c64b798`, has all 7 votes false and no mixed subgroup (a build that turns the frame red where one fires stayed untinted over a walk, and its control, forced on, was red all over), so the picture is unchanged there. In Yoshi's 1,124 keys asked in play, the one changed shader, `cbd80cb96ad38a61`, votes on a uniform buffer value and the uniform registers it guards are unused.
- **Found on:** Yoshi and the Mysterious Book, Pokémon Pokopia.

### TXD through a uniform register kept no handle

- **Symptom:** `[RED] <key>: the translator deleted N bindless accesses` in the build ([Losses a shader build reports](#losses-a-shader-build-reports); `Failed to find handle source for bindless access` in its reflection's notes), for a shader with TXD through a uniform register (0xf6c). The MSL has no gradient sample, and what it read comes back 0.
- **Cause:** a bindless read takes its handle as source 0. `ATex` adds it in every other form, but TXD has a branch of its own, which left it out. BindlessElimination then took the first coordinate for the handle, found no source for it and deleted the read.
- **Fix:** the TXD branch adds the handle first, as the others do.
- **Found on:** Yoshi and the Mysterious Book: in 14,079 of its 133,154 shaders, none of them among the 1,519 the playtests asked for, so no route has shown it in a picture. Found by [PR 1261](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1261).; Star Fox (281 pixel shaders, the characters' fur shells among them)
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Handles built under a guard the read's guard cannot be tied to

- **Symptom:** `Failed to find handle source for bindless access` in the build's translator notes, for a read whose handle is two words of the game's own uniform buffer, and what it read comes back 0. Or nothing in the log: the read binds `fp_t_cb3_<word>` or `fp_t_cb2_<word>`, a texture word with no sampler word, so its sampler is `samp_tex`'s guess, where the shader's other reads of that word bind both.
- **Cause:** the compiler builds a handle in a register under a guard (`R = word`; `R |= sampler word`) and reads through it under a guard of its own. The register's phi then merges the handle, its first word and what the register held before. `Utils.FindLastOperation` picks the phi's input only when both guards are the same operand in the same branch form, which misses `!UP0` with UP0 the vote of `P0` against a read under `!P0`, `P4` against a read under `P5 = P4 && !P1`, and `P6` against a read past `BRA.!P6`. `TryTableHandle` then took only c2 words, so a handle in another bank was deleted. In a register with no earlier value the first word's phi is (undefined, word): `FindLastOperation`'s rule that the one defined source of such a phi is its value was also applied to that phi inside the outer phi, so the first word came back as the whole handle.
- **Fix:** in the vendored translator. `TryTableHandle` also takes a texture word ORed with a sampler word of the same bank or a constant sampler from any constant buffer, including when only the OR's source is a phi and the handle is not. A word of a game's uniform buffer alone is no candidate, as it may be other data, so the first word and a float left in the register drop out without a look at the guards. A c2 word alone that an ORed candidate also takes is that handle on its way, not a second candidate, which also saves a read chosen at run time. `FindLastOperation` applies the undefined rule only to the phi it was asked about. Proving the guards equal instead would take a boolean prover over predicate logic and the vote's meaning. A chain change.
- **Found on:** Yoshi and the Mysterious Book: of 128,113 translated shaders, 130 reads in 78 were deleted (the pool's water surface on Chapter 1's page) and 189 reads in 152 bound the texture word alone. SPIR-V of every shader against the translator before: those 230 change there; 364 of 22,072 in DAVE THE DIVER and 2,633 of 69,924 in DRAGON QUEST I & II HD-2D Remake, of which 319 and 1,780 bound the texture word alone and the others lose a second read; 16 of 13,980 in OCTOPATH TRAVELER II, a second read. None of Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave's 16,888, nor of Blasphemous, NieR:Automata, Return of the Obra Dinn, Catherine: Full Body and FINAL FANTASY X/X-2. Only Yoshi's water was compared in a picture. FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE's 26 reads with [no c2 word](#bindless-reads-whose-handle-phi-also-carries-other-values) may be this form, not checked: its shaders are not split on disk.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (the three guard forms, an unguarded OR, and a c2 handle read once).

### Losses a shader build reports

- **Symptom:** in the build's log, `[RED] <key>: the translator deleted N bindless accesses (<type> N): their results read 0` or `[RED] <key>: its library builds only without its integer output, dual source entry points`, and `[RED] lost without failing: …` with the counts per kind (the NSP script repeats that one line). In `regress.py`, `NEW-FAIL: N shaders with more … than in the baseline`.
- **Cause:** both leave a shader that builds, so the build's status said ok and it exited 0. BindlessElimination deletes an access whose handle it cannot trace and writes 0 to its results (`Failed to find handle source`, kept in the reflection's notes). `finish` in `dmsh_build.py` rebuilds a library that does not compile with its extra entry points without any of them. Nothing compared these between builds, which is how [14,079 of Yoshi's shaders](#txd-through-a-uniform-register-kept-no-handle) and 38 of its vertex shaders (their layer entry points, which the chain no longer makes: [A shader the chain does not translate stops the split and the build](#a-shader-the-chain-does-not-translate-stops-the-split-and-the-build)) lost something unnoticed.
- **Fix:** the reflection holds both (`notes`, `entries_lost`), so a build that restores a shader from its cache reports it too. `dm/gfx/tools/shader_regressions.py` holds what counts as a loss (`losses`) and prints the lines (`report_losses`). `regress.py` compares each shader with the baseline (`loss_diff`): more accesses of a type or a new entry point lost in any shader is `NEW-FAIL`, however the totals move, and a shader new in the candidate starts from none. What the baseline has too is listed as known, and the game's note says what is known about it.
- **The build still exits 0:** the NSP script runs it for players, and one shader the game may never reach must not stop a pack. `regress.py` is the check for a change, so there is no stricter exit code. On a baseline from before `entries_lost` existed, every lost entry point counts as new.
- **Not counted yet:** the translator's other messages that lose something: `Shader instruction … is not implemented` and `Failed to find targets for BRX instruction`, which the build summary still counts among its translator lines.
- **Found on:** the 79 games with a library on the Mac (2026-11-05, those shaders rebuilt on the current chain): 1,984 deleted accesses in 964 shaders of 13 games, the most in Ghost Trick (1,030 in 616) and Super Monkey Ball Banana Rumble (490 in 245), and FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE has 26 by its note. No shader built without its extra entry points when rebuilt (65 of Sparks of Hope's, which look as if they did, could not be: its package is not on the Mac): the 25 compiles that failed in the logs were all Yoshi and the Mysterious Book's, before nxnative fixed the point size of their layer entry points. Each game's note lists its own. A listed loss is not proved harmless: none has been traced to a picture.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/tools/tests/test_shader_regressions.py Losses`, `python3 dm/tools/tests/test_regress.py Regress`.

### A guard band cull in every vertex shader (0x80d)

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 80d ... (000fc80003fe0000c43211110000780d) is not implemented` for every vertex shader of the game, so nothing draws.
- **Cause:** the compiler ends the position's computation with a test of its own. FSETP sets P0..P5 where x, y or z lies beyond +/- w times the system registers 30 and 31, which nothing else reads: a guard band. Two 0x80d follow, immediate 0xc4321111 with no predicate result, then immediate 0x80000000 with a result, and that predicate guards an EXIT before the shader writes its position and outputs.
