All 6,319 vertex shaders hold this once; no other game's do. What the instruction reads is not known: it is inferred from what surrounds it, and no other instruction reads its result.
- **Fix:** `AGuardCull` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`: the result is false, so no vertex leaves early. Every vertex writes its outputs and clipping does the rest, as for a shader without this; only the optimization is lost. A form with no predicate result at all (PT, as the first of the two) writes nothing and is skipped in any stage (PRAGMATA's mesh and pixel shaders hold it). Any other form of 0x80d stops the shader.
- **Found on:** Kirby Air Riders.
- **Open:** the instruction's real meaning. A vertex output at `a[0x5c]`, written by 4,771 vertex shaders (`Attribute offset 0x5C is not valid`), is dropped; it has no known meaning either.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Ampere instructions new in Pokémon Pokopia

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction <op> at 0x… is not implemented`, `F2FP.MERGE_C` or `F2I from a float that is not 32-bit` in the shader build; those shaders are left out and their draws and dispatches skipped.
- **Fix:**
  - F2FP.MERGE_C (bit 78): b converted into the low half, c's low half into the high one.
  - F2I from a half (source size 1): the half that bits 60..61 select.
  - ATOMS.POPC.INC (0xf8c): an add of 1 with no source register, also counted as using shared memory. The game's one encoding is accepted; any other stops the shader.
  - UP2UR (0x883): `URd = UP0 ? 1 : 0`. The game's one encoding (URZ, mask 1) is accepted; any other stops the shader.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Pokopia.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### Ordered not-equal becomes unordered in MSL

- **Symptom:** an ordered not-equal comparison is true with a NaN operand, so a NaN guard lets it through. An inverted result is wrong too; NVIDIA's compiler can implement unordered equal by inverting ordered not-equal. No game symptom has been identified.
- **Cause:** the ISA translator emits the correct SPIR-V `OpFOrdNotEqual`, but SPIRV-Cross's MSL emitter maps it to `!=`, which is unordered in MSL. The pinned upstream emitter has a TODO at that case. Writing `(a < b) || (a > b)` is insufficient under Metal fast math: the compiler can fold it back to `!=`.
- **Fix:** `spirv_cross_bin/ordered-ne.patch`, applied by its pinned `build.sh`, makes the MSL emitter write `!isunordered(a, b) && a != b` and preserve both operands' expression dependencies. The ISA translator remains unchanged. Logical and integer not-equal and unordered floating-point not-equal keep their existing lowering. This fixes the shared converter used by NVN, Vulkan and OpenGL.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_spirv_ordered_ne.py`: synthetic scalar and two-, three- and four-component f32/f16 modules, assembled and validated as SPIR-V, compiled with the chain's Mac flags and checked by GPU readback; the iOS compiler checks the same modules offline. Ordered, unordered, inverted, integer and Boolean results cover signed zeros, finite values, infinities and signed quiet/signaling NaNs. The old converter fails every width and type; changing only its emitter passes. PR [#2042](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2042)'s five targeted Orin-fixture operations also pass outside the fixture's separate denormal allowance (M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922).
- **Found on:** synthetic SPIR-V and a Jetson Orin's recorded instruction results (PR #2042); no game symptom identified. The Cuphead Mac route supplies regression coverage.

### Comparisons with .FTZ

- **Symptom:** single bright dots at thin far geometry (railings, track edges), changing frame to frame as the camera moves, already in the lit picture before anti-aliasing. Most of them come from another cause (below).
- **Cause:** a float comparison has an .FTZ flag (Ampere: FSET and FSETP bit 80, after the comparison at 76..80; Maxwell: FSETP bit 47, FSET bit 55, FCMP bit 47): on NVIDIA a denormal operand compares as zero. The translator decoded it and dropped it, and Apple's GPUs keep fp32 denormals. A guard like `sum != 0` before `1 / sum` then lets a denormal sum through: its reciprocal overflows, the products give NaN, and a `min(x, 1.0)` (`precise::`, see [min, max and clamp with a NaN operand](#min-max-and-clamp-with-a-nan-operand)) turns it into 1.0, a fully lit pixel.
- **Fix:** a comparison flushes each operand where the flag is set: `|x| < FLT_MIN ? 0 : x` (`FlushDenormal`, `InstEmitAluHelper.cs`, from `AFset` / `AFsetp` and from Maxwell's `EmitFset`, `EmitFsetp` and `EmitFcmp`, which flushes only the source it compares). An immediate or RZ needs no code.
- **Limit:** only the comparisons. The compilers set .FTZ on nearly every float instruction of every shader, so it is in effect a mode of the whole shader, and Metal has none (`MTLCompileOptions` has math modes, no denormal control). Flushing the arithmetic would be about 3.3 million more sites in Mario Kart World, so its FADD, FMUL and FFMA keep denormals. Four denormal weights can still add up to a normal sum, which NVIDIA sees as zero; it stays finite, so it makes no NaN.
- **Found on:** Mario Kart World: its deferred lighting `635061c90d75a8d1` normalises four depth-weighted half-resolution samples by `1 / sum` only where `FSETP.NEU.FTZ` says the sum is not zero. No Switch 1 game has shown the symptom; Maxwell's comparisons follow for the same semantics (the flag is on all of them in the 8 Switch 1 games counted).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` (`CompareFtz`, on the game's encoding) and `test_dmsh_maxwell_ftz.py`.

### Compute threads that return before a barrier

- **Symptom:** none on the Mac. Suspected as a cause of GPU hangs (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`) in compute, task and mesh shaders that return before a `threadgroup_barrier`.
- **Cause (checked, not a bug):** on NVIDIA's GPUs a thread that has exited counts as arrived at a barrier, and Switch compute shaders rely on it (`if (lid * 4 >= count) return;`, then a barrier). Metal's specification wants every thread of the threadgroup at each barrier, and the translator keeps the return in the MSL. On an Apple M6 a returned thread counts as arrived too. Seven shapes all complete with every remaining thread's value right: whole simdgroups returning, all but the last simdgroup returning, half the lanes of every simdgroup, returns between two barriers, a barrier loop after the returns, threads leaving a barrier loop at different iterations, half the lanes before a `simdgroup_barrier`. Each ran with 4,096 threadgroups of 1,024 threads, 280 dispatches in all with no hang.
- **Fix:** none: the translator keeps the return. A pass that kept a returned thread arriving at every later barrier (`BarrierExits.cs`, [PR #1774](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1774)) was not taken. It fixes nothing measured: PRAGMATA's hang went away only together with a cap on every shader loop. That hang was its cloth solver's loop, which never ends when the iteration count it reads is 0, and the cap hid it.
- **Limit:** measured on an M6 only. M1 to M5 have not run it. A failure of `t_barrier_exit` on one of them would bring the pass back for those GPUs.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA (no profile; 156 of its compute shaders return before a barrier) and Orbitals ([PR #1783](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1783)), as PR #1774 reports; neither game's files were run here. Not [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md)'s GPU hang: none of its 520 task and mesh shaders has a barrier in its body. Only 12 of its compute kernels return before a barrier, none of them in the pass that hangs.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> t_barrier_exit && dm/gfx/tests/t_barrier_exit`.

### MEMBAR is a fence, not a barrier

- **Symptom:** a GPU hang (`kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`, `[RED] the host has waited 10 s for GPU work`) in a compute shader whose threads poll a flag in a loop with no BAR.SYNC: a persistent work queue.
- **Cause:** MEMBAR (and the ERRBAR after it) orders memory and does not wait for other threads. The translator emits it as SPIR-V's `OpMemoryBarrier`, which SPIRV-Cross writes as `threadgroup_barrier(mem_flags::mem_device | mem_flags::mem_threadgroup | mem_flags::mem_texture)`: an execution barrier every thread of the threadgroup must reach. A thread that leaves the polling loop early never reaches it again, and the threads still in the loop wait for it for ever.
- **Fix:** SPIRV-Cross writes the chain's MSL at version 3.2 (`dmsh_chain.CROSS_MSL`), where `OpMemoryBarrier` becomes the fence it is, `atomic_thread_fence(…, memory_order_seq_cst, thread_scope_threadgroup)` for MEMBAR.CTA and `thread_scope_device` for MEMBAR.GL / .SYS and Ampere's MEMBAR, with no execution barrier. BAR.SYNC's `threadgroup_barrier(mem_flags::mem_threadgroup)` stays a barrier. First fixed by a text rewrite of that line (`membar_fence`, ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up, [PR #1407](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1407)), which made every MEMBAR a device fence and lost the BAR.SYNC after one (next entry).
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: its culling queue `12a52b683eec95c1` polls a done flag with `MEMBAR.SC.GPU; ERRBAR` at the end of the loop body.
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_membar_fence.py` (with `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1` the shaders also compile for the Mac and the iPhone; with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE` a Maxwell MEMBAR and BAR.SYNC go through the translator).

### Compute loops that outlive the process

- **Symptom:** the game exits but compute work keeps the Mac GPU busy, `kill -9` does not reclaim it, and WindowServer can stop until a reboot. PRAGMATA's reporters needed seven reboots, including after a game abort on main `9ccbd6b1` ([issue #2664](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2664)). Kirby Air Riders' particle submission hung twice in two races; the loop guard's author reports none in sixteen later sessions, whose builds also carried other changes ([PR #2254](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2254)).
- **Cause:** a loop whose exit condition never arrives in the translated execution. PRAGMATA's persistent queue kernels poll shared counters; why those counters disagree is **open**, as is Kirby's failing loop/input. Existing memory and fence fixes may affect the trigger, so a reproduction on current main is still needed. The platform failure is that committed GPU work is not reliably reclaimed at process exit. A CPU-raised abort was retracted: only 2 of 24 lone-kernel trials observed the store; the 876 ms bounded trial is evidence against that CPU approach, not a timing of the GPU approach below.
- **Fix:** declared host/chain containment, **not the root fix**. `dmsh_rewrite.compute_loops`, based on PRs #2254 and [#2709](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2709), adds a budget of 2^18 loop-condition checks per function invocation to compute shaders and their reachable helpers. Nested and consecutive non-barrier loops spend the same function budget. Fragment, vertex, geometry, task and mesh shaders are unchanged. The first capped thread raises a **GPU-written** abort word shared by the submission; other non-barrier loops check it every 1,024 checks. Helpers receive the state through their callers. A loop containing an execution barrier, directly or through helpers, gets a separate counter reset on each entry and never reads the abort word. Unequal work in earlier or nested non-barrier loops therefore cannot consume its barrier budget, and an asynchronous abort cannot split its threads. Every submission gets a fresh abort word.
- **Diagnostics:** each kernel in a submission has its own hit record, separate from the abort word. The host reads it in the submission's commit-feedback handler and logs `[RED] loop cap hit: submission N, kernel <hash>, loop site N`, once per loaded kernel. Only reaching the kernel's own budget records a hit; observing another kernel's abort does not. The site indexes `loop_sites` in `<hash>_b.json`, with its function, source line before containment and barrier status; the emitted MSL also marks each site; the logged site is checked against that count, and an out-of-range word is logged as such, not as a site. If multiple sites hit, the record names the lowest numbered one. This identifies capped work, not proof of the original runaway's cause. The hit word lives in the shared arena, and an arena block holding one is recyclable only once that feedback handler has run, not on `g_done` alone: the fast wake (`post_early`, on the GPU's event signal) can advance `g_done` before the handler runs, and a recycled word then reads as garbage — Ocarina of Time logged `1016326144` (`0x3c900000`, float bits of recycled arena data) for one of three kernels of one submission that way.
- **Limits:** this changes the result of capped or canceled work, including persistent state used in later frames. A legitimate long function can exhaust the budget: a 512 × 512 nested count produces 261,632 increments instead of 262,144 and reports its cap hit; a 256 × 256 count completes unchanged. No absence of hits on a smoke route proves all workloads fit. Helpers have independent budgets; barrier loops can still run to the full budget, and each queued submission must raise its own abort. Large grids, non-compute work and time spent before the first cap are not bounded in milliseconds. This is safer reproduction, not a guarantee against another reboot. #2664 stays open for the bad loop/input and its loosest correct binding.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 -m unittest dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_compute_loops.py`. Bounded GPU inputs exercise actual host dispatches and completion readback, a capped helper through a loop-free wrapper, propagation to later ordered dispatches, attribution without blaming canceled kernels, helper barriers following unequal per-thread work, once-per-kernel logging, next-submission isolation and valid/exhausted cumulative budgets in both encoding modes. Original inputs remain bounded if containment is broken. Checked on an Apple M6 with Metal compiler 32023.922; the authors' M4 Pro synthetic GPU measurements at a smaller 2^14 test cap were 59.8 → 8.6 ms for eight dispatches and 809.3 → 118.8 ms for one large dispatch, compared with the cap alone. Isolated containment overhead is unmeasured.
- **Validation:** Fast Fusion's race and Sports Resort's title route exercise guarded kernels. Static inspection accounts for every loop in both rebuilt compute libraries, including helpers. Such checks qualify those workloads, not every legitimate loop or a maximum completion time. The game comparisons used chain `968f7d1ea898` against `19d159c0b938`, before the landing rebase onto current main. The rebased chain `7d5d6482def7` passes all nine containment tests, including the GPU host fixtures in both encoding modes, and 63 documentation/chain/reflection checks. An additional game comparison after rebase was not run: an interactive PRAGMATA root-cause investigation held the shared GPU measurement lock.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA and Kirby Air Riders; the recycled-word read (the implausible site index) on [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md). Current-main trigger and gameplay retests on those titles remain open.

### A BAR.SYNC right after a MEMBAR lost

- **Symptom:** a GPU hang on the iPhone (`Caused GPU Hang Error`, `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang`, then `FATAL GPU access revoked`) in a scene that ran before chain d24f05cf9d78 (ccd834e0); the failed command buffer lists a compute shader with shared memory. Shared memory read before the other threads of the group wrote it can also give wrong values without a hang.
- **Cause:** below MSL 3.2 SPIRV-Cross writes `OpMemoryBarrier` as a `threadgroup_barrier` on every memory, an execution barrier, and so leaves out an `OpControlBarrier` that directly follows one as redundant (`spirv_msl.cpp`, `OpControlBarrier`). The translator emits MEMBAR then BAR.SYNC exactly so: `MEMBAR.CTA; BAR.SYNC` is how NVIDIA's compiler writes GLSL's `memoryBarrierShared(); barrier();`. The `membar_fence` rewrite then made that one line a fence (two such lines in a row one fence), so the group had no barrier left: its simdgroups (two in a 64-thread group) raced on shared memory. Both MEMBAR scopes printed the same line, so MEMBAR.CTA also became a device-scope fence, about 6 times the cost of a threadgroup-scope one in a kernel with one per iteration on an M6 (1.9 ms against 0.25 to 0.3 ms).
- **Fix:** MSL 3.2 for every shader of the chain (previous entry): SPIRV-Cross writes each barrier and each fence with its own scope, and `membar_fence` is gone. The per-shader MSL 3.1 for image atomics (`dmsh_atomic.cross_args`) went with it.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem: Three Houses on an iPhone 15 Pro (A17 Pro), compute shader `b0a3586f7a5ead00` (64 threads, 512 bytes of shared memory) in the failed command buffer ([issue #2786](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2786)); the hang has not been run again on the fixed chain.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_membar_fence.py` (`test_maxwell_membar_then_bar_sync`: a fence and a barrier for each pair).

### Barrier moves around warp shuffles

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction f55 at 0x… (000fe20000000000000000001a0f7f55) is not implemented` in the shader build; those shaders are left out.
- **Cause:** `0xf55` and `0xf56` (BMOV) save convergence barrier B15 into R26 before a block of warp shuffles (SHFL) and restore it after. The translator had no entry for either.
- **Fix:** both are no-ops, as the barriers they save are (BSSY, BSYNC, BREAK, WARPSYNC): per thread they do nothing. In the shaders found, no other instruction reads R26 after them, and the register beside the barrier field (R15) is read live by an FMUL, so neither writes a general register (`InstEmitAmpere.cs`).
- **Found on:** Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment: pixel shaders `02e49c189b769074` and `49241cf469c76830`, 16 pairs each (chain 95ba69b5403e).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`BarrierMoves`: the game's encodings leave the translated shader unchanged; it fails on the translator before).

### YIELD

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 946 at 0x… (000fde00038000000000000000007946) is not implemented` in the shader build; those shaders are left out.
- **Cause:** 0x946 is YIELD (CuAssembler's sm_86 records disassemble `…7946` as `YIELD ;`), which only lets the warp scheduler run another of the warp's paths. The translator had no entry for it.
- **Fix:** a no-op, beside the convergence barriers and ERRBAR in `InstEmitAmpere.cs`: per thread it does nothing.
- **Found on:** Resident Evil 4: 21 compute shaders (`68e3f47c80022855`, `b86773bfdc9f50e5`, ...), each with one YIELD after `MEMBAR; ERRBAR`, before a branch.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py Yield` (the translated shader is unchanged by it; it fails on the translator before).

### LDC with the bank in its address register (`.IS`)

- **Symptom:** `Ampere b82 at 0x…: LDC with a bindless bank or an address mode (…00008a00…7b82)` in the shader build; those shaders are left out.
- **Cause:** LDC's address mode 2 (`.IS`, bits 78..79; 3 is `.ISL`) takes the bank as the instruction's plus Ra's upper 16 bits, and the offset from Ra's lower 16 bits plus the instruction's, as Maxwell's LDC does. The compiler uses it for an indexed read of c1 with the bank folded into the address (`(index * stride) + 0x10000`). The Ampere path refused the mode, and the shared load picks a run-time bank only among the banks the accessor reports, none here, so even the Maxwell path reads 0 for it.
- **Fix:** `ALdc` lowers `.IS` / `.ISL` to that bank and offset, and the load selects among the banks the shader can reach: its own constant segment c1, the instruction's bank and every bank its other constant reads name, but not c0 (`ARuntimeBanks`, from a scan of the decoded program; `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`). The host fills the driver segment only at offsets known when the shader is built and does not load a shader that indexes it at run time (`dmsh_reflect.py` `unfillable`): with c0 among the candidates, the pixel shader below, which reads c0 elsewhere, translated but was not loaded. Maxwell shaders are unchanged. A run-time bank 0 in a compute shader is not remapped as a constant one is (`AMapC0`).
- **Found on:** Hyrule Warriors: Age of Imprisonment: vertex shader `cc679c7b4d3e703c` reads its quad corners (−1,1), (1,1), (0,0), (−1,−1), (1,−1) from c1 by vertex index % 5, and pixel shader `059a0a910ff87a26` reads c1 from 0x60 (chain 316fb82e2f22). The vertex shader is also Pokémon Pokopia's.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` (`LdcIndexedBank`; it fails on the translator before).

### LDC's 16-bit immediate: signed beside a register, the address alone

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_c15: 536839244 bytes read at fixed offsets` at load, and the draw is skipped; the shader's MSL reads `fp_c15._m0[33552452]`, a slot the game binds with a few words.
- **Cause:** Maxwell's LDC (`c[bank][Ra + imm]`, the immediate at bits 20..35) took the immediate as signed in every form (`InstEmitMemory.Ldc`, `Imm16ToSInt`). Added to a register that is right, and the sum is 32 bits: nvdisasm prints `0xef94003fffc7050b` as `LDC R11, c[0x3][R5+-0x4]`; Xenoblade Chronicles 2's pixel shaders read c6 at R − 8 to R − 128 (858 LDCs in 76 shaders); and to reach past 32 KiB the compiler adds 0x10000 to the register first, which a 16-bit wrap would make pointless (Yoshi and the Mysterious Book's pixel shader `461579dbf57658b4`: `IADD3 R40, R26, 0x10000` and `LDC.64 R24, c[6][R40 + 0xaa88]`, 438 shaders there and 290 in Orbitals, all on Ampere). Alone (Ra = RZ) the immediate is the address as it is: the compiler reads c[14][0x8448] with `LDC.U16 R49, c[14][0x8448]` beside a plain read of c14[0x844C]. Sign-extended, that read's byte address was 0xFFFF8448 and its word 0x3FFFE112, whose high bits ran into the operand's 5-bit slot field (`Operand.PackCbufInfo`): a read of c14 became c15 at word 33,552,452, which stretched c15's fixed extent (`NoteUniformRead`) to 512 MB.
- **Fix:** a lone immediate (Ra = RZ) is unsigned; added to a register it stays signed (`InstEmitMemory.Ldc`). Ampere's fixed forms, ULDC (0xab9) and LDC with RZ (0xb82, mode 0), take the immediate unsigned the same way (`InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`); no Switch 2 shader in the local builds has one at 0x8000 or above. A constant word past the 64 KiB bank reads 0, as a read past the size the game bound does, instead of being packed into the operand (`ConstantFolding`, the Load case), and `PackCbufInfo` throws for an offset or slot that does not fit rather than change the slot. Reading every immediate unsigned (the first proposal) moves XC2's reads 64 KiB away; wrapping every `Ra + imm` at 64 KiB agrees on all the shaders above but is not what the compiler assumes, and would put an AND in every indexed constant load whose `(Ra + imm) >> 2` shape `BindlessToArray` and the uniform handle matchers read.
- **Found on:** Mortal Kombat 11 (`0100F2200C984000`, v1703936, draft profile in [seedds/nxnative#2799](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2799); diagnosed in [seedds/nxnative#2800](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2800)): 27 rejected shader loads, 11 distinct keys, each with one `LDC.U16 c[14][0x8448]`. The signed form beside a register: Xenoblade Chronicles 2 (pixel shader `09030ba6b1d1283e`, `LDC.64 R4, c[6][R59 - 0x40]`), which the unsigned reading proposed first would have moved 64 KiB away. Corpus: the shader packages of 13 Switch 1 and 9 Switch 2 builds (chain 53cf95b181aa).
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ldc_offset.py` (the games' own encodings; 4 of 5 fail on the translator before, the signed register cases pass on both).

### IMAD.HI adds a register pair, and IMAD.X its carry

- **Symptom:** 64-bit arithmetic in a shader gives wild results: the 64-bit division behind an exposure average returned 893011 for 218, so the exposure stayed at the end of its range. With only the pair fixed it still gave 2 for 340 / 1.
- **Cause:** IMAD.HI is the high word of `a * b + {c + 1:c}`, the same 64-bit addition as IMAD.WIDE, with the carry out on the predicate at 81..84. The decoder took the high word of `a * b` and added `c` alone, losing the upper word of the pair and the low word's carry. The compiler's 64-bit multiply chains the two: `R9:R8 = R6 * R13 + {R9:R8}`, then `R8 = high(R7 * R11 + {R9:R8})`. Separately, all three IMAD forms have .X (bit 74, the carry predicate at 87..90), which the decoder dropped; as with IADD3.X, a negated addend is then its bitwise not.
- **Fix:** `AImadHi` and `AImadCarryIn` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs`.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave: the Lighting compute shaders' division, run line by line from its translated MSL on 208 known inputs, with dividends up to 2^44: 26 wrong with only the pair fixed, none with both. Other Switch 2 games share the decoder and have not been rebuilt with this.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### NVN2 compute reads its workgroup counts at c0 + 4

- **Symptom:** a scene lit by many small lights comes out too dark, with its clustered light lists empty for most clusters.
- **Cause:** a light culling shader computes its cluster as `(z * c0[8] + y) * c0[4] + x`. NVN2 compute keeps the dispatch's workgroup counts at the start of its driver segment, below the per-shader layout at 0x10, and the host wrote them only at c0 + 0x200, Maxwell's place. At zero, every cluster of a column wrote the same entry.
- **Fix:** with a Switch 2 `sdk` (`dm_sdk_ounce`), `encode_args_` also writes them at `DMSH_C0_DISPATCH_NVN2`, and an indirect dispatch copies its GPU-written counts there too. x and y are read by the shader; z at c0 + 12 follows their order and has not been seen read.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave: `35d12d6f98fa05e2`, read from its translated MSL. The picture's change has not been measured on its own.

### Texture handles of SDK 22's Ampere code

- **Symptom:** `Ampere instruction 361` (or `f60`, `370`, `38d` and others) refused, or hundreds of `Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "textureCube"` when the handle was taken from the wrong register: units drawn white, the rest of the scene right.
- **Cause:** the bindless form of a texture instruction is the bound opcode + 1 with 0x300: 0xb60 becomes 0x361 and 0xb6f becomes 0x370. 0x361 takes its handle first in the second source block (bit 59), 0xf60 in a uniform register at 40..46, and 0x370 (TXQ) in Ra with the level in Ra + 1. A census of all the game's instructions gave the position.
- **Fix:** `ATex` and `ATxq` in `InstEmitAmpereMem.cs`. The same bring-up adds, from their encodings in the game: 0x301 BREV and 0x2be its uniform form, 0x311 F2I.U64, 0x312 I2F of a 64-bit pair, 0x38c ATOMS, 0x38d ATOMS.CAS (Rd is 1 when it swapped, not the old value: the game's only use is a retry loop until Rd == 1), 0x992 MEMBAR, 0x9ab ERRBAR, and the carry out of IMAD.WIDE.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave: 902 shaders had the wrong register at first; all 16,888 translate now. Splatoon Raiders lists 0x2be, 0x301 and 0x38c among its refused instructions; it has not been rebuilt with this.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py`.

### NVN2 compute's separate handles in c[0]

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding cp_t_cb196610_10280074Smplr has no resolved slot number` at load, then `the dispatched program has no compute shader`; the shader's image has no slot either (`cp_i_tcb_AA_`).
- **Cause:** after the uniform and storage entries, NVN2 compute keeps four runs of 8-byte handles in `c[0]`: textures (control +0xb3), separate textures (+0xb4), separate samplers (+0xb5) and images (+0xb6). `AMapC0` knew textures and images, so everything past the textures landed in the image table and beyond. Star Fox's `1002069a331ceded` shows the order: 13 uniform entries, 4 textures, 32 separate textures, 13 separate samplers and 5 images make its 0x290 bytes (+0xba) exactly, and its handles fall on separate textures 22, 24 and 31, samplers 11 and 12 and image 4.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_build.py` passes the two counts (job `nvn2_cs`) and `AMapC0` maps them to NVN's fixed tables, separate texture r to `c[2]` 0x168 + 8 r and separate sampler r to 0x568 + 8 r (chain change). A shader with neither maps as before. `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` covers the image past both.
- **Found on:** Star Fox: one compute shader, dispatched in the prologue. Any NVN2 compute shader with separate handles reads them this way.

### NVN2 compute's second push block holds buffer entries

- **Symptom:** the GPU never finishes the first 3D frame: `[RED] the host has waited 10 s for GPU work`, with persistent compute shaders (a loop on a work counter, `threadgroup_barrier` inside) among the failed command buffer's shaders.
- **Cause:** `nvnCommandBufferPushComputeConstants(cmd, block, offset, size, data)` writes block 0 or block 1. NVN2 compute's `c[0]` lays out, after its 0x10-byte header, push block 0 (control +0xa9 words, rounded to 16 bytes), then push block 1 (+0xaa words), then the uniform entries (+0xb1), the storage entries and the handle tables of [the entry above](#nvn2-computes-separate-handles-in-c0) (the SDK's accessor at `sdk+0x5e6f20` reads byte +0xa9 + block). PRAGMATA pushes 16-byte `{address, size}` entries, 1 to 3 of them, into block 1 before its dispatches, and its shaders read buffers through those addresses (`ubo_ptr`-style global loads). The host kept one block, so block 1 overwrote block 0, and the translator placed block 1 after the uniform entries: the persistent shaders spun on a counter in the wrong buffer.
- **Fix:** the host keeps `DMSH_PUSH_BLOCKS` (2) blocks, block b at `b * DMSH_PUSH_SIZE` of `c[24]` (`push_const` in `hle_nvn.c`; a block past them is a red line). The translator maps `c[0]` reads in push block 1's range to `c[24] + 0x100` (`AMapC0`, `nvn2_cs` field 7 from +0xaa), and `GlobalToStorage` takes `c[24]` as an address bank like `c[0]`. A storage buffer whose address the game pushed binds through the bytes the replay holds (`sbuf_push`, `NVN_RB_SBUFQ`). Chain change.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: with it, the hang at frame ~1,300 is gone.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_nvn2_buffers.py`.

### NVN2 graphics stages' buffer banks (c[25] to c[29]) are push blocks

- **Symptom:** `Ampere … bank 25` (to 29) refused, or `stage N cbN has no uniform buffer bound` for graphics shaders that read buffers through an address. Then, with those banks taken for the uniform slots: `[RED] graphics push constants into block 5 (0 + 0x10): the host keeps 2, dropped`, `sbufp reg3 dummy` (to reg5) for a mesh shader in the pass dump, and everything a visibility buffer draws comes out black while the rest of the frame is lit.
- **Cause:** NVN2 graphics stage i (vertex 0, hull or mesh 1, domain 2, geometry 3, pixel 4) reads buffer entries from a bank of its own, `c[25 + i]`: 16-byte `{address, size}` entries, or 8-byte handles. They are not the uniform slots the game binds. They are what the game pushes with `nvnCommandBufferPushGraphicsConstants` into push block 1 + i, as compute reads its own entries from push block 1 (block 0 holds the constants). The translator mapped the banks onto the uniform slots, and the host kept two push blocks and dropped the rest. PRAGMATA pushes its mesh stage's six entries into block 2 and its pixel stage's five into block 5 before its visibility and material passes. Its mesh shader `5aab38bb5ba67ef7` binds uniform slots 0 to 2 only and read its rows 3 to 5 (the visible-cluster tables) from unbound slots, so the primitive IDs it writes into the visibility buffer were garbage. The material depth pass `93590d7bd6125d1c` took its cluster table from uniform slot 3, a 16-byte buffer, and every raw load it made missed. No material strip passed its depth test, and the G-buffer stayed black.
- **Fix:** a chain change, general to NVN2.
  - **Translator:** `InstEmitAmpere.cs` `AStageBlock` maps the stage's bank to its block of `c[24]`, at `(1 + i) * 0x100` (`AMapC0`, `AIndexedBank`). The addresses read there become `sbuf_push` bindings, which the host reads from its push blocks (`NVN_RB_SBUFQ`, handles decoded). `dmsh_build.py` passes the bank (job `stage_bank`, `NVN2_STAGE_INDEX`) for the stages the chain translates: vertex, mesh and pixel.
  - **ABI:** `DMSH_PUSH_BLOCKS` is 6.
  - **Host:** `push_const` keeps graphics blocks 0 to 5, and compute's 0 and 1.
  - **Removed:** the per-stage fill of `c[0] + 0x210` for graphics stages; only compute reads that table.
  - **Check:** `Nvn2Buffers.test_a_graphics_stage_bank_is_its_push_block` fails on the old translator.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: `c[25]` in 9,944 vertex shaders, `c[26]` in 538 mesh and 3 hull shaders, `c[27]` in 37 domain shaders, `c[28]` in 1 geometry shader, `c[29]` in 12,195 pixel shaders. In one intro frame it pushes block 1 157 times, block 2 51 times and block 5 92 times. A host-only test (each stage's rows filled from its block) brought back the ship's engines and the tower's lights at the moon base.

### Uniform words read through a buffer address (cx)

- **Symptom:** `Ampere … is not implemented` for ALU forms, ULDC (0xab9) or LDC (0x582) with bit 91 set.
- **Cause:** bit 91 makes a constant operand bindless: `cx[UR][offset]` reads a word of the buffer whose 64-bit GPU address is in the uniform register pair UR, UR + 1 (ALU forms: UR at bits 32–37, offset at 38–54; ULDC: UR at 24–29; 0x582 LDC adds Rb at 64–71). The address is a plain GPU address, not a handle: decoding it as one read through wrong pools and page-faulted the GPU.
- **Fix:** `ABindlessConstant` reads it as a global load at UR + offset, through the memory pool table the shader walks for raw global memory (`gmem_map`) (`AUldc`, `ALdcBindless`, `AGet`). Chain change.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: RE Engine's materials read their constants this way; its 0x36d (TXD with the handle leading the first block) and the plain form of [0x80d](#a-guard-band-cull-in-every-vertex-shader-0x80d) came up in the same shaders.

### A handle pair ORed sampler word first

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t_cb2_170 has no resolved slot number` at load (the draw is skipped), the build summary counting the binding under *slot was not decoded*; or, once the read's other inputs are known, `Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "texture2D"` and the read answers 0. `DMSH_BINDLESS_TRACE=1` on a `dmsh_xlate` job prints what a refused handle is made of.
- **Cause:** the compiler ORs the separate texture word and the separate sampler word of c[2] in either order. The bindless elimination took the OR's first operand as the texture: with the sampler word first (`c[2][0x5c0] | c[2][0x248]`, word 0x170 of the separate sampler table at 0x568), the sampler's word was named as the texture, a slot the host cannot decode. Through a phi (the OR predicated, the bare sampler word on the other path) the bare word was a candidate too, so the read was refused.
- **Fix:** `BindlessElimination.cs` orders a pair of c[2] words by the table they lie in: a word of the separate sampler table (`dmsh_abi.h` `DMSH_C2_SAMP`, 128 slots) is the sampler, the other the texture, in the plain OR and among a phi's candidates; a bare sampler word is no texture candidate (chain change).
- **Found on:** Star Fox (62 pixel shaders, 5 of them loaded at the prologue cinematic).

### A buffer texture with a two-word handle

- **Symptom:** menu labels and other text disappear while their backgrounds and icons render.
- **Cause:** a type-1 bindless handle ORs two uniform words, which may hold the sampler and texture in either order. Buffer-texture reflection discarded the second word, and the host bound the dummy buffer when the first word held only a sampler. Glyph vertices read zeros and collapsed.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_reflect.py` preserves `sreg` and `soff` for `tbuf_ubo`, `nvn_refl.m` retains them, and `encode_args` resolves both words with `ubo_bind_handle`, as for ordinary textures. This is a chain change, bound to the handle representation rather than a game or build.
- **Check:** Python reflection fixtures and `t_refl`; `t_tbuf_handle` reads distinct buffers on the GPU with both word orders, a second word at byte zero, a one-word control, a nonzero pool base and inline updates to either texture word.
- **Found on:** Sid Meier's Civilization VI, its glyph vertex fetches.

### Ampere HMMA through simdgroup_matrix

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's frame rate swings between 60 and 30 fps (Donkey Kong Bananza's own controller, `main+0x204a18`, drops to 30 after two frames of 20 ms from their start to the GPU's end, and comes back after six), and the GPU profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`) shows one compute dispatch of 2-6.5 ms a frame: agl's upscaler network (`agl_ndsr.pck`, shader `7629d857a0a93833`).
- **Cause:** its 144 HMMA.16816 a thread (F16 sources and accumulator) were emulated lane by lane: 32 subgroup shuffles and 64 FMAs a lane for each, 4,922 shuffles in the shader. Without its shuffles (each returning its own value; a wrong picture, measured only) the dispatch took 0.24-0.40 ms. On this GPU shuffles cost more than threadgroup memory: one HMMA a SIMD-group took 5.42 ns emulated, 1.08 ns through threadgroup memory and `simdgroup_matrix`, 0.65 ns with the tiles loaded straight from where LDSM reads them, and 2.73 ns moving the register fragments into `simdgroup_matrix` elements with shuffles (Apple's 8×8 layout gives each lane a pair of columns of one row too, but in another order). Metal's matrix types have no SPIR-V form spirv-cross converts (its cooperative matrix support is GLSL only).
- **Fix:** in a compute shader, HMMA.16816 with an F16 accumulator becomes `MatrixMultiplyAdd` (`AHmma`), then a call of the helper `dmsh_hmma16816_f16` (`TensorPass`, `HelperFunctionManager.GenerateHmma16816F16`), named in the SPIR-V. Each SIMD-group has 256 words of a shared memory of its own (`hmma_scratch`, 1 KB a SIMD-group): the lanes store their fragment words as they are, A row-major, B transposed and C row-major, and after a SIMD-group barrier (the new IR instruction `SubgroupBarrier`, Metal `simdgroup_barrier`) the helper's body computes D from them lane by lane. `dmsh_build.py` (`hmma_msl`) replaces that body in the Metal source with `simdgroup_load` (B with its transpose flag), four `simdgroup_multiply_accumulate` and a `simdgroup_store` into the same block; the name, the arguments and the block are the contract, and the translator's body stays correct where the rewrite does not apply. Other HMMA forms (m16n8k8, an F32 accumulator) keep the per-lane emulation. A chain change.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_hmma.py`: the rewrite of the helper as spirv-cross writes it, and on the Mac's GPU the rewritten body against a CPU reference of PTX `mma.m16n8k16` on random fragments (4 SIMD-groups a threadgroup; largest error 0.009 against a reference that rounds once: the matrix hardware rounds the F16 accumulator as it goes, as Ampere's does). The translator's body agreed with the same reference to 0.002.
- **Found on:** Donkey Kong Bananza: its 24 NDSR compute shaders. On one save and route, the upscaler took 1.1-1.6 ms a frame instead of 0.1-5.1 ms (2.5-4.5 typically), a frame's GPU span 8.9-19.4 ms instead of 13.8-23.3, and the game's 60 ↔ 30 switches fell from 87 to 68 in the same run length; the picture is unchanged.
- **Note:** three of four of these HMMAs start an output tile, with C = 0. A variant for those calls that neither stores nor loads C (`simdgroup_multiply` first) was measured and not kept: 0.767 against 0.820 ns a warp-HMMA (6 %) in an isolated loop of the build's two bodies on an M4 Pro, about 0.04 ms of the upscaler's 1.6-1.9 ms a frame, and no measurable change in the game.
- **Open:** LDSM and MOVM (27 and 72 a shader) are still emulated with shuffles (314 left in the upscaler, whose floor without them is about 0.3 ms); loading A and B straight from LDSM's source and keeping D as matrices between HMMAs (0.65 ns an HMMA) needs the translator to follow LDSM → MOVM → HMMA → HMMA chains. Done for accumulator chains and for A read by an LDSM: [HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group); B and MOVM remain.

### HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movie or upscaler network runs slow on the Mac and iPhone, and the GPU profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF` with each dispatch in an encoder of its own, then `DM_NVN_DISPATCH_OWN=1`, since removed) shows its compute dispatches, small grids of one to four SIMD-groups a threadgroup that loop over tiles, at 1-3 ms each; the dispatches' shaders call `dmsh_hmma16816_f16` hundreds of times ([Ampere HMMA through simdgroup_matrix](#ampere-hmma-through-simdgroup_matrix)).
- **Cause:** each call took every operand through the SIMD-group's scratch block: the lanes stored A (4 words), B (2) and C (2), the matrix hardware loaded them, and D went back to the registers through the block, three SIMD-group barriers a call. In these networks almost every HMMA continues an accumulation (C is the D of the call before it, which nothing else reads: chains of 9 to 36), four calls in a row share A, and A comes straight from an LDSM whose rows are a strided tile in shared memory. That LDSM's emulation cost four shuffles a lane on top of the round trip. Measured on an M4 (8 GPU cores) in Ocarina of Time's movie networks (14 dispatches a frame): without the helper's body the frame's compute fell from 26.9 to 15.4 ms; without the shuffles as well, to 11.0 (wrong pictures, measured only, over four frames).
- **Fix:** `TensorPass.Prepare` gives each HMMA flags, a constant argument after C, from the optimised IR of its block: an accumulator slot (8 a function) for a chain, whose D stays there as the next call's C (`FlagCSlot`, `FlagDSlot`); A that is the previous call's (`FlagAReuse`); C = 0 (`FlagCZero`); and A that is four matrices of one LDSM of the block with no shared memory write or barrier between (`FlagADirect`, `FlagATrans` for .MT88), with the LDSM's row address, each matrix's offset from lane 0's row and the row stride. That needs the address to be U + f(lane) with U the same in every lane and f known: a divergence analysis of the function (the lane and the thread index components a subgroup spans are divergent, a loop header's phis are not when every exit branches on a uniform condition and, for a loop with several back edges, every other branch in it too, phis where paths meet are) and an evaluation of f for the 32 lanes, whose rows must be 16-byte aligned and strided. An LDSM in a compute shader is now a `MatrixLoad` a matrix, lowered to its per-lane code after that. The translator's own body reads only the registers, so every flag is a promise about values it computes anyway. `dmsh_rewrite.py` `hmma_msl` declares the slots and A's matrices in each function that calls the helper and passes them by reference, so the inlined helper keeps them in registers, and passes the caller's shared memory array (the one threadgroup array besides the scratch; with none or several, A goes through the block): A loads with `simdgroup_load` at lane 0's row (`simd_broadcast`) plus its offset, which leaves the LDSM's per-lane code unused. A chain's middle call stores only B. A chain change.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_hmma.py`: the rewrite's arguments and callers, and on the GPU a run of four calls with those flags (A from shared memory plain and transposed, A kept, C = 0, accumulators in two slots), each given the registers the translator's body would read (a stale A where A comes from shared memory), against a CPU reference (64 SIMD-groups, largest error 0.014 for two HMMAs with an f16 accumulator between them). With `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`, the flags the translator gives two chained HMMAs on an LDSM's matrices. `DMSH_TENSOR_TRACE=1` on a `dmsh_xlate` job prints why an HMMA's A is not loaded where its LDSM read it.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate): its title and opening movies run eleven NDVE network shaders with 2,217 HMMAs, 1,890 of them continuing a chain. Measured on the title screen, the median over 321 profiled frames of the frame's compute: 26.65 ms before, 18.85 after on an M4 with 8 GPU cores (quartiles within 0.1 ms); on an M6 with 12, 14.78 before and 11.41 after (frames from 3,000 on, 1,057 and 851 profiled, quartiles within 0.6 ms), the GPU span 15.63 and 11.87 ms; the title and the opening movie unchanged to the eye. 1,789 of the HMMAs take no A through the scratch (540 load it where its LDSM read it, 1,249 keep the previous call's); of the other 428, 427 (most of `d15370a382630d4e`'s) have a shared memory store between their LDSM and the HMMA. agl's NDSR upscaler, in this game and in Donkey Kong Bananza (24 compute shaders, 5,328 calls: 2,664 keep A, 1,325 continue a chain, 3,996 start from C = 0), loads none of its A where its LDSM read it: its LDSM rows are addresses read from a table in shared memory, which the analysis cannot prove strided, so A goes through the scratch there. Bananza's opening and first play render as before.
- **Note:** what is left is B's trip through the block: with B loaded from shared memory too (a fake address, measured only), the frame took 12.6 ms. In these networks B is the weights, read by LDSM.MT88 before the tile loop and kept in registers (72 pairs in two of the shaders), while the shared memory is reused for activations, so it cannot be loaded where it was read. Turning B into matrices once where it is defined and keeping them in registers took 18.9 to 17.4 ms but slowed one shader (`0a56002cdc7e1fce`, four SIMD-groups a threadgroup, 144 matrices) by 13 %; not kept. Removing the barrier at the end of a chain's middle call changed nothing (18.84 against 18.88 ms).
- **B kept where it repeats** (`hmma_bslots`): two calls have the same B when their B arguments come, through copies (`x`, `as_type<T>(x)`, `int(x)`), from the same variables, each written once in the function and declared at its top level. Out arguments, a non-const reference or a taken address count as writes. The first such call at the function's top level keeps B's two matrices in a slot (`dm_bm`), and the calls of that B after it take them, with no store, load or barrier for B. It is a literal argument at each call, so the branches fold away when the helper is inlined. Ocarina of Time: 10,293 of 16,620 HMMAs in 56 of its 71 HMMA shaders take B; `fa3033d05c0a41d6` takes it in 118 of 176. Its largest shaders hold up to 168 slots, and they still gain: on an M6, a latency-bound chain (24 threadgroups of 4 SIMD-groups) with 10 to 160 weight pairs ran 16–28 % faster, with the same bits. In the game, standing on the Kokiri Forest deck, the 4-dispatch compute encoder that holds the upscaler's network went from 984 / 979 µs to 951 / 946 µs (two runs each in one session, pictures the same within noise). A contributor measured that network's largest dispatch on an M4 Pro at 779 → 655 µs (discussion #1789).
- **Ruled out: building the matrices from the lanes' registers.** Apple's matrix hardware fills a matrix quickly only through `simdgroup_load`. The 8x8 lane layout is fixed: on an M6 lane l holds row (l/16)·4 + (l/2)%4 and columns c, c+1 with c = 4·((l/8)%2) + 2·(l%2), the layout published for the M1. With it, each lane can set its elements through `thread_elements()` from SIMD shuffles, which gives the same bits. On a 64-call chain this took 1.716 ms with B built that way, against 1.448 through the block, and 8.9 ms with every operand built that way. So B's trip cannot get cheaper per call, and the slots leave it out where B repeats. With no trip at all the same chain took 1.147 ms. The exact Ampere rounding (f32 blocks, two conversions a lane) is the larger cost of a chain: 1.448 ms against 0.734 with f16 accumulation, which gives different bits.

### LDSM reads rows other lanes stored

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movie or upscaler network output has dotted streaks, every other pixel of a few rows wrong, in other places each run, while its input is clean. Through agl's NDSR upscaler a few such pixels a frame grow into white blotches over the whole picture: Ocarina of Time's Kokiri Forest on an M4 Pro on chain 844bf1a5ea8f, with the game's own upscaler (the default); with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` in its place, no blotches.
- **Cause:** a warp stores tiles to shared memory (STS) and reads them back with LDSM, often with no BAR between them. The Switch runs a warp's shared memory accesses in order. Metal orders another lane's threadgroup store and this lane's load only through a barrier, and its compiler moves them past each other. Since [HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group), a compute shader's LDSM is a `MatrixLoad` that `TensorPass` lowers late to its per-lane code, and NDSR's LDSMs all go that way: its picture went bad on that chain.
- **Fix:** `InstEmitAmpereTensor.ALdsm` puts a subgroup barrier (`simdgroup_barrier(mem_flags::mem_threadgroup)`) before an LDSM's reads and one after them, so the stores have landed and no lane's next store reaches the rows before every lane has read them; in a compute shader they bracket the `MatrixLoad`s and so the code that replaces them. LDSM is warp-collective, so the whole SIMD-group reaches both. `TensorPass` does not count a subgroup barrier as a shared memory write, so an HMMA still loads its A where its LDSM read it. A chain change.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` `Shared.test_ldsm_reads_between_subgroup_barriers` (fails on the translator before). Ocarina of Time on an M4 Pro, Kokiri Forest autosave, the game's upscaler: white blotches over the forest on chain 844bf1a5ea8f, none on fbd67412d49d at the bridge and on the village floor. GPU time through the title movie unchanged (median 12.7 and 13.6 ms a 300-frame window, both chains).
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): the movie library's NDVE networks first (five of them have no BAR between STS and LDSM; MetaIX, GitHub pull request #1376), then agl's NDSR in play. Not the cause of the dotted horizontal rows that remained in the scene with either upscaler (GitHub issue #1537): [An HMMA outside a chain overwrote the chain's accumulator](#an-hmma-outside-a-chain-overwrote-the-chains-accumulator).

### An HMMA outside a chain overwrote the chain's accumulator

- **Symptom:** dotted horizontal rows across the picture at a regular spacing, in fog and light, with the game's upscaler and with `DM_UPSCALER=metalfx` alike (MetalFX then replaced only `agl_ndsr.pck`'s shaders): Ocarina of Time in Kokiri Forest, Hyrule Field and the castle town since chain 23a1ea1db092. The rows are the tiles of agl's `agl_ndsr_vr.pck` / `agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck` networks (about 28.8 pixels of the 1536-wide input).
- **Cause:** [HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group) gives a chain an accumulator slot, `acc[k..k+1]` with k from the flags. The helper did all its work in `acc[k]`, and a call outside every chain has no slot bits, so it worked in slot 0: an unchained call between the two calls of a chain held in slot 0 overwrote that chain's accumulator. Each of those 30 networks has such calls (2,515 in all, of 7,558); their outputs came out wrong where the overwritten chains land in each tile.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_rewrite.py` `HMMA_BODY` computes C and D in matrices of its own; a slot is read only for a chain's C (`FlagCSlot`) and written only for its D (`FlagDSlot`). The flags are constants at each inlined call, so the copies cost nothing. A chain change.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_hmma.py` `HmmaOnGpu.test_calls_outside_a_chain_leave_its_slot_alone` (a chain in slot 0 around two calls outside it, on the GPU against a CPU reference): 8,090 outputs over tolerance (largest error 9.6) with the helper before, none after. Ocarina of Time on an M4 Pro, the same Kokiri Forest view: rows on chain 23a1ea1db092 and later; none on 0f510fd5's chain, with the helper's rewrite left out, or with this fix.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md) (GitHub issue #1537). Counted in the translated MSL (calls outside a chain while slot 0 is held): Ocarina of Time 3,700 in 52 of its 62 HMMA shaders (its NDSR upscaler and movie networks too); Donkey Kong Bananza's NDSR 7 in 5 of its 24, so a few of its outputs were wrong as well (not looked at in the game).

### HMMA rounds its f16 accumulator once per 8 products

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movie or upscaler network gives values a few f16 steps off the console's, in about half the outputs of each HMMA. Nothing looks broken; it is the network's arithmetic, compounded layer by layer.
- **Cause:** `HMMA_BODY` accumulated on `simdgroup_half8x8`, whose multiply-accumulate rounds the f16 accumulator after every product. Ampere's HMMA with an f16 accumulator adds 8 products to C and rounds once (Khattak and Mikaitis's A100 model, N_FMA 8; [MATLAB-tensor-core](https://github.com/north-numerical-computing/MATLAB-tensor-core)): an HMMA.16816 is k 0-7 rounded to f16, then k 8-15 on top, rounded again. Against the toolbox's 5,000 recorded A100 fp16 cases (C as f16), per-product rounding matches 2,420; summing the 8 products in f32 and rounding once matches all 5,000. On a Jetson Orin (sm_87, `mma.sync.aligned.m16n8k16` through PTX, 3 x 4,096 warps of random halves in [-1, 1]), per-product rounding matched 34.9 % of the outputs and the once-per-8 sum 99.97 % (fxd0h, PR #1897).
- **Fix:** `HMMA_BODY` accumulates each block of 8 into a `simdgroup_float8x8` (half operands and a half C, which `simdgroup_multiply_accumulate` takes) and rounds it to f16 per lane through `thread_elements()`. A multiply-accumulate with zero A and B into a half result rounds the same, but took NDSR's network twice as long. The host's NDVE network (`ndve_engine.metal` `hmma()`) rounds the same way, into a new matrix: written into its accumulator arrays' own `thread_elements()`, its layers ran 15 to 50 times slower on an M6. A chain change.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_hmma.py`'s GPU runs compare every output bit for bit with the two-block reference: main's body differs in 21,461 of them on an M5 Max and on an M6, the new one in none; `test_ndve_engine.py` keeps the host's network bit for bit with the helper. Isolated on an M5 Max, NDSR's `fa3033d05c0a41d6` takes 0.316 ms against 0.276 and `d99fbeeb53d38847` 0.446 against 0.393; their outputs change in 1.5 % and 0.8 % of halves. On an M6, a chain of 258 calls takes 2.56 ms against 1.69 for 2,048 threadgroups of 4 SIMD-groups, and the host network's seven layer kernels at the movie's sizes 2.94 ms against 2.85 (best of 100 each, summed). Not measured in a game.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): its NDVE movie network and agl's NDSR upscaler (PR #1897).

### SHFL takes its lane from the low 5 bits

- **Symptom:** a warp shuffle whose lane comes from a register with bits above bit 4 set returns the thread's own value instead of the requested lane's. Orin probes found 207 wrong IDX results, 196 BFLY results and 179 segmented IDX results, each out of 1,024 rows (PR #2044).
- **Cause:** SHFL uses only b[4:0] as its lane or offset in every mode ([PTX ISA](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/parallel-thread-execution/index.html#data-movement-and-conversion-instructions-shfl-sync)). The translator's `HelperFunctionManager.cs` `GenerateShuffle*` helpers consumed the whole register, so the high bits affected the source lane and validity predicate. Both Maxwell's `InstEmitWarp.Shfl` and Ampere's `AShfl` lower to these helpers; the c operand's clamp and segment mask were already decoded correctly.
- **Fix:** mask the lane argument to `& 31` in all four shared helpers (IDX, UP, DOWN, BFLY). General shader ISA semantics, with no game or build gate; at most one additional integer AND per shuffle. Rebuild the native translator and regenerate its lock and chain header.
- **Check:** on an M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1 (26B131), Metal 32023.922, the PR's recorded Orin SHFL programs through current main's chain reproduce 207/196/179 differences with the baseline native translator and zero with the rebuilt one. UP, DOWN and immediate BFLY still match. `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate DMSH_TEST_GPU=1 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_shfl.py` checks synthetic Maxwell and Ampere shaders on Metal: all four modes, raw high-bit operands, immediate lanes, result and predicate, and clamp/segment boundaries. UP uses clamp zero; its separate nonzero-clamp issue is unchanged. No game or iOS device run.
- **Found on:** no game; the Jetson Orin's sm_87 results (CUDA driver 12.6, PR #2044). The Orin fixture now runs in `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py`; IDX, BFLY and segmented IDX are no longer expected failures.

### Ampere instructions checked against an Ampere GPU

- **Symptom:** none that shows on its own: a translated instruction gives other bits than the console's GPU for some inputs (a NaN operand, a lane index past 31, a directed rounding), and the picture is off only where a shader depends on them. The translator tests check the MSL a few encodings give, not the values the shader computes.
- **Cause:** nothing compared the translated instructions' values with an Ampere GPU's, so a meaning read wrong from the encoding, or lost on the way through spirv-cross and Metal, went unseen until a game showed it.
- **Fix:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` runs about 190 operations, each the GPU compiler's own machine code for one PTX operation, through the whole chain: the translator with the build's options, spirv-cross with the chain's arguments, every MSL rewrite `dmsh_build.py`'s `finish()` makes (read from its source, so a new rewrite is included), and the chain's Metal flags. On this Mac's GPU each takes the 1,024 input rows of its fixture, and every output word is compared bit for bit with what a Jetson Orin (sm_87, CUDA 12.6 driver) computed from the same rows. The inputs mix special values (signed zeros, denormals, infinities, NaNs with several payloads, the limits of each integer type, ties for the roundings), random bits and ordinary values; warp operations run 32 threads to a group on both sides, so a lane's neighbours are the same. `dm/gfx/tools/ampere_orin/capture.py` records the fixture on any Linux machine with an Ampere GPU and NVIDIA's driver: the driver compiles each op's PTX itself (no CUDA toolkit, no compiler on either side), runs it, and the same machine code is rewritten for the translator, its global loads and stores becoming shared memory ones and the CUDA parameter reads and the addressing they feed becoming NOPs (`prog.py`), which the device's `nvdisasm` checks. `ampere_orin/ampere_orin.sass` lists each program as the translator gets it.
  - Allowed on every op: what Metal's f32 arithmetic itself gives, NaN bits (Ampere writes the canonical NaN, 0x7fffffff) and flushed f32 denormals (`compare.PLATFORM`).
  - `KNOWN` lists the ops that still differ, each with its reason ([the tracking issue](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2040)); `APPROX` the ones that run Metal's own approximation (MUFU, and the divisions built on it), which are reported and never fail. An op outside both lists that differs fails the test, and so does a `KNOWN` op that matches, so a fix takes its entry out.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` (about 5 s on an M5 Max); it prints every op's differing lanes by cause. `AMPERE_ORIN_ONLY=<ops or families>` runs some, `AMPERE_ORIN_REPORT=<file>` writes the results as JSON. To record again: `python3 dm/gfx/tools/ampere_orin/capture.py --host <user@machine> [--arch sm_86]`, with NVIDIA's `nvdisasm` (the `cuda_nvdisasm` redistributable) in `~/ampere_orin` on that machine.
- **Found on:** no game; a Jetson Orin's results. The HMMA comparison it extends is PR #1897's.

### Directed rounding in FADD, FMUL and FFMA

- **Symptom:** FADD, FMUL and FFMA with `.RM`, `.RP` or `.RZ` can return the round-to-nearest result, one ULP away from the result requested by the shader.
- **Cause:** the Ampere and Maxwell emitters discarded the directed modes. Metal's floating-point operations round to nearest, so these modes need a correction in the translator.
- **Fix:** the shared helpers use the sign of the rounding error to step the nearest result by one ULP when required. FADD orders the addends by magnitude before recovering the error, which prevents an intermediate overflow when the sum is finite. FMUL recovers the product error from the significands and scales the operand that keeps the intermediate in range. FFMA computes the error on scaled terms so a finite fused result survives an overflowing separate product. Directed `.FTZ` flushes inputs and results with their signs. The same FMUL rule applies to the `1/(2π)` literal used before MUFU.
- **Check:** the nine directed programs in `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` cover 9,216 recorded Orin lanes. On M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1 and Metal 32023.922, rounding differences fall from 2,857 to zero. The remaining differences are 486 NaN encodings (Orin `0x7fffffff`, Metal `0x7fc00000`), 179 with denormal inputs and 14 with denormal outputs. Another 48 synthetic lanes compare exact bits for finite overflow boundaries, signed FTZ, scaling, the trig literal and fused cancellation.
- **Coverage:** those 48 synthetic Ampere lanes and 45 synthetic Maxwell lanes pass with default and relaxed Mac math modes. Their 33 shaders validate as SPIR-V and compile offline for iOS. At the normal/subnormal boundary `FMUL.RP.FTZ` on `0x00800000 * 0x3f7fffff` still gives +0 on Metal, where exact arithmetic with round-up before flushing gives `0x00800000`. That case has no NVIDIA hardware readback. The approximate `MUFU.SIN` and `MUFU.COS` programs differ in 746 and 581 of 1,024 lanes, down from 780 and 606. No iOS device ran the shaders.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2051](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2051). Mac regression runs cover Fast Fusion's menus and Cuphead's map.

### FMNMX and HMNMX2 signed-zero ordering

- **Symptom:** minimum or maximum returns the wrong sign for a zero result. On the recorded Orin inputs, `max(+0, -0)` returns +0 while the translation can return -0. A subsequent reciprocal or sign test can distinguish the results.
- **Cause:** NVIDIA orders -0 below +0 for min/max in either operand order. Metal's min/max can choose either zero, including after the chain's rewrite to precise functions.
- **Fix:** the shared `FPMinMax` instruction helper checks for two zeros using their integer bits. For that case it selects the operands' bitwise OR for minimum and AND for maximum. Ampere FMNMX and HMNMX2 and Maxwell FMNMX use the helper after operand modifiers and half-input FTZ. The usual constant predicate adds four integer operations for minimum and five for maximum, per lane. Double min/max uses its existing path.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` runs recorded Orin cases and separate synthetic contracts. On an M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922, recorded fmax changes from one zero-sign error and one f32 denormal difference to only the denormal difference. Recorded hmin2 and hmax2 have 8 and 7 NaN-bit differences in 2,048 lanes each (Orin `0x7fff`, Metal `0x7e00`). Synthetic zero, numeric and NaN cases cover both directions, half FTZ, and Maxwell constant/register predicates. All pass in default and relaxed Mac math modes, and their nine shaders compile with the iOS Metal target. Maxwell results are synthetic contracts against the [documented NVIDIA semantics](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/parallel-thread-execution/index.html#floating-point-instructions-min). No Maxwell hardware or iOS device was used.
- **Found on:** the Jetson Orin sm_87 probes from [PR #2047](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2047). Fast Fusion's validation records the shader build and Mac menu-route comparison.

### Half-pair .FTZ flushes f16 subnormals

- **Symptom:** HADD2.FTZ, HMUL2.FTZ and HFMA2.FTZ keep half subnormals instead of flushing them. A subnormal times infinity consequently gives infinity instead of NaN.
- **Cause:** the translator dropped bit 80, as for f32 arithmetic, but Metal keeps half subnormals. The Orin reference shows that both inputs and the rounded half result flush to signed zero. Flushing only inputs or only results leaves 57 or 60 HMUL2.FTZ lanes wrong; dropping the zero's sign leaves at least 125.
- **Fix:** `AHalfFlush` in `InstEmitAmpereAlu.cs` flushes unpacked inputs below 2^-14 while preserving their sign. `AHalfResult` clears a packed result lane's mantissa when its exponent is zero, after rounding to half. A `.F32` result bypasses half-result flushing. HSETP2/HSET2 share the source helper; HMNMX2.FTZ uses it too. No game or build gate is needed. Non-FTZ forms emit the same code; FTZ forms add source comparisons/selects and integer result masking.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate AMPERE_ORIN_ONLY=f16x2 python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py`. On M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, Metal 32023.922 (metalfe-32023.922.4), the rebuilt native translator through current-main rewrites changes differing halves as follows: HADD2.FTZ 246 → 215; HMUL2.FTZ 449 → 234; ordinary HFMA2.FTZ 418 → 332. Every remaining difference is Orin `0x7fff` versus Metal `0x7e00`, both NaNs; differences beyond NaN bits fall from 31, 221 and 93 respectively to zero. These are not bit-exact matches. The JSON report records NaN patterns separately, and a classifier test keeps NaN-versus-infinity failures distinct from NaN-bit differences.
- **Coverage:** the hardware fixture uses HFMA2.MMA, translated by the [shared HFMA2 emitter](#hfma2mma-the-half-pair-fma-on-the-tensor-pipe); derived `hfma2_alu` / `hfma2_alu_ftz` copies change only opcode bit 2 to exercise ordinary HFMA2 with the recorded inputs/reference. Synthetic min/max FTZ cases cover both inputs, signed zeros and the smallest normal half (64 halves each); a synthetic `.F32` result case preserves ±2^-15 (32 floats). These synthetic checks are not new NVIDIA recordings. `.F32` source swizzles and HSETP2/HSET2.FTZ lack hardware-reference coverage. No game frame-time benefit or cost is established.
- **Found on:** no game symptom; [the Orin comparison](#ampere-instructions-checked-against-an-ampere-gpu). PR #2048 reports about 7,700 FTZ half instructions in Ocarina of Time, a static census rather than a timing measurement.

### HFMA2.MMA, the half-pair FMA on the tensor pipe

- **Symptom:** `NotSupportedException: Ampere instruction 235` or `435` prevents a shader from translating. Half `.RELU` arithmetic also turns NaN into zero instead of retaining NaN.
- **Cause:** the translator registered ordinary HFMA2 (low-nine-bit opcode 0x031) but omitted its tensor-pipe form 0x035. NVIDIA's compiler emits the latter for `fma.rn.f16x2` and for constant loads such as `-RZ * RZ + imm`. The result emitter used a plain maximum with zero for `.RELU`; unlike `.SAT`, [.RELU preserves a NaN result](https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/archive/12.0.1/parallel-thread-execution/index.html#half-precision-floating-point-instructions-fma).
- **Fix:** register 0x035 with `AHfma2` and `InstName.Hfma2`, preserving the same operand forms, predicates, constant-bank discovery and result modifiers. `AHalfResult` selects a NaN through before taking the maximum with zero on HFMA2, HFMA2.MMA and HMUL2 `.RELU` forms. The existing input/result `.FTZ` handling remains shared. This is general Ampere ISA behavior, with no game or build gate; ordinary non-RELU HFMA2/HMUL2 output is unchanged.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_xlate_bin/dmsh_xlate python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py`: all 200 recorded/derived/synthetic operations pass on M4 Pro, macOS 27.2.1 (26B131), Metal 32023.922 (metalfe-32023.922.4). Main rejects 17 programs at HFMA2.MMA; 13 now run, while four reach their separate FCHK, F2I.S64, DADD or DMUL refusal. Eight constant-loading conversion/integer programs are bit-exact. HFMA2, HFMA2.FTZ and HFMA2.RELU differ in 317, 332 and 314 of 2,048 halves respectively, all Orin `0x7fff` versus Metal `0x7e00` NaNs; saturation is bit-exact. Reciprocal remains a Metal approximation, not a bit-exact result.
- **Coverage:** derived ordinary-HFMA2.RELU changes only opcode bit 2 and retains the Orin inputs/reference: main has 314 NaN-versus-zero errors, the fix none beyond NaN bits. Synthetic HMUL2.RELU checks NaN inputs, zero times infinity, infinities, negative clamping and positive subnormals: main has 24 NaN-versus-zero errors in 64 halves, the fix 24 NaN-bit differences and no numerical errors. `HalfFmaAlias` in `test_dmsh_ampere_alu.py` checks identical emitted MSL and resource metadata across all seven operand forms, each plain or with SAT/F32/RELU/FTZ (35 comparisons). These translation checks do not provide new NVIDIA recordings for constant-bank/uniform forms. The #2049 fixtures have no triple that triggers the independent [half-FMA double-rounding error](#hfma2-single-rounding-to-half).
- **Found on:** synthetic Jetson Orin sm_87 programs (CUDA driver 12.6); the existing RELU path is present in PRAGMATA. No game symptom is attributed to it.

### HFMA2 single rounding to half

- **Symptom:** a packed HFMA2 result is one half ULP off near a rounding midpoint. For example, `-3136 × -8.125 + 0.000124693` produces 25488 on Orin and 25472 with the old translation.
- **Cause:** rounding the fused result to f32 can turn a value just beyond a half midpoint into a tie. Packing that f32 value then rounds the tie to the wrong half.
- **Fix:** the Ampere emitter selects a typed half FMA for HFMA2 and HFMA2.MMA when the sources and result are halves. SPIR-V widens the already rounded result exactly for the shared result handling, so even a later unpack sees the half-rounded value. Operand FTZ runs before FMA and result FTZ after half rounding. The shared .RELU still retains NaNs and clamps negative values, and .SAT still saturates. .F32 sources, .F32 destinations and FFMA use the f32 path.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_ampere_orin.py` runs the 4,570 input triples recorded for [PR #1902](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1902) through the actual translator. On an M4 Pro with macOS 27.2.1 and Metal 32023.922, 70 numeric differences in 9,140 lanes fall to zero. The 1,160 NaN encodings still differ (Orin `0x7fff`, Metal `0x7e00`). Separate cases check .RELU and .FTZ together, packed results read back as floats, .F32 operands and results, and float FMA followed by an explicit pack.
- **Found on:** Jetson Orin sm_87 probes. The issue was found in arithmetic comparisons.

### The NDVE movie network by the host

- **Symptom:** a Switch 2 game's movies and the title behind them run below 60 fps on a Mac with a smaller GPU, and the GPU profile (`DM_NVN_GPUPROF`) shows almost all of the frame in a dozen compute dispatches of `nn::ndve`'s movie network: Ocarina of Time's title at about 24 fps and 38 ms of GPU a frame on an M1 Pro with 16 GPU cores, 24.8 ms on an M1 Max ([issue 1375](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1375)), 18.9 ms of compute on an M4 with 8 ([HMMA chains keep their matrices in the SIMD-group](#hmma-chains-keep-their-matrices-in-the-simd-group)); 11-14 ms of the 16.7 on an M6, an M4 Pro or an M5 Max.
- **Cause:** `nn::ndve` (a Nintendo library linked into the game's `main`: `nn::ndve::RouleauArchiveIO`) runs the first network of its model, `Movie/Ndve/Ndve-Nvn2-vid_seq5.dat` ([`nn::ndve`'s movie enhancement file](#nnndves-movie-enhancement-file)): a Rouleau archive (footer `RLA1`: four entries, two networks and two small shaders) whose networks hold a table of their 9 compute shaders, `0x3c320` bytes of weights and the shaders, compiled for a 1920x1080 picture. The graph is in the library's code, not in the archive: a U-Net over the picture packed 2x2 at 960x540 (16, 32 and 64 channels), eleven HMMA.16816 convolution dispatches, the last convolution added to the picture (`5a7d69b0`), then a 4/3 resampler with a small network of its own (`4f41ccf2`, from the archive's NDSR pack). Its code is written for NVIDIA's register file: each convolution loads its weights before the tile loop (LDG.128, STS.128, LDSM.MT88.4) and keeps them in registers through it, up to 72 B pairs (144 registers a thread), each used once a row. Translated, that is register pressure and a trip through the SIMD-group's scratch for every HMMA's B. No general lowering found does better on an M4 Pro (best ms of the five heaviest dispatches standalone, chain `dedc9391c30b`: `e6be82d8` 1.34, `0a56002c` 1.69, `5a7d69b0` 0.97, `d15370a3` 0.80, `a8b4fb48` 0.54): B as `simdgroup_matrix` registers once before the loop gave 1.18 / 2.29 / - / - / 0.49 (worse where four SIMD-groups a threadgroup each hold their own 72 pairs); B loaded at every HMMA from its storage buffer, where `TensorPass` proved it came from (built and bit-exact), 1.75 for `e6be82d8` and 0.94 for `5a7d69b0`; B in threadgroup slots of their own, 2.16 for `e6be82d8` (the memory costs occupancy). A variant that read every B from one fixed place looked 35 % faster, but only because those 256 bytes were always in the cache.
- **Fix:** `nvn_mtl.m`'s dispatch hands the network to `nvn_ndve.inc`, which runs it as kernels of the host (`dm/gfx/msl/ndve_engine.metal`, built with the host's other shaders): 8x8 `simdgroup_matrix` tiles straight from the tensors, weights loaded once a tap, 3x3 inputs staged per threadgroup, the network's tensors in buffers of the host's with a zero border, one encoder for the 13 kernels. On by default; `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=0` runs the game's dispatches, `ab` alternates with `DM_AB`. It is bound to the library's model, not to a game: the keys are the hashes of the model's shaders and the weights' offsets follow from its layers (each layer's weights, then its biases, each padded to 256 bytes, to the archive's `0x3c320`), so any game that ships this model runs it. Recognition: the 14 keys in order, each dispatch's weights and biases at the offsets the layers give from the first's, the picture the last convolution adds being the one the first read, the sizes and formats, the resampler's constants. Anything else (another model, the archive's second network, other sizes) leaves the game's dispatches running, logged once; a mismatch after some dispatches were skipped turns the host's network off for the rest of the run. The colour conversion `49dff010` stays the game's. The kernels compute what the translated shaders compute, in their order: each HMMA as `HMMA_BODY` does it (two `simdgroup_multiply_accumulate` on `simdgroup_half8x8`, input channels 0-7 then 8-15, `simdgroup_multiply` for a chain's first call), a chain over the taps and then the 16-channel blocks, the epilogue's D + bias in f32, ReLU and one rounding; the picture's sum in the game's order under safe math.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_ndve_engine.py`: every kernel on random inputs and weights at the network's sizes, bit for bit against references that call `dmsh_hmma16816_f16` as `hmma_msl` rewrites it, in PTX's fragment layout with TensorPass's chain flags (all 10 identical on an M1 Pro, an M5 Max and an M4 Pro; with an f32 accumulator per HMMA instead, the 9 with HMMAs differ in 6-25 % of their outputs), and the weight offsets the layer table gives against those Ocarina of Time binds. Standalone on one title-movie frame on the M1 Pro (16 GPU cores): main's 13 translated dispatches 36.3 ms of GPU, the host's 13 kernels 10.8 ms (PR #1894). In the game, on an M4 Pro with 20 GPU cores (Ocarina of Time's `title-movie` route, `scripts/bench.py`, three runs each, the window kept in focus): the frame's GPU time 12.78 ms with `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=0`, 6.77 with the host's network (p50), both at 60 fps. `DM_NDVE_ENGINE=check` runs the game's dispatches and the host's network and resampler beside them into images of its own and compares them texel by texel (logged every 300 frames): over 1,500 title frames, the resampler identical in every frame, the network in all but 10 texels, each 1/1023 off, the same 10 with `DM_NVN_ENC_THREAD=1`. The translated shaders are built with Metal's fast math, which may contract and reorder those sums: `5a7d69b0` built with `-fno-fast-math` instead gives other bits in a few texels of random inputs; the host's kernels keep the game's order.
- **Note:** run standalone on fixed inputs, 4 times each, the 10 translated dispatches gave the same output every time on an M4 Pro, on chain `dedc9391c30b` and on `39417042cfe8` ([issue 1893](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1893) reported outputs that change from run to run on an M1 Pro, measured by another harness; not reproduced).
- **Note:** the host's network rounds each HMMA's f16 accumulator once per 8 products, as the translated helper does since [HMMA rounds its f16 accumulator once per 8 products](#hmma-rounds-its-f16-accumulator-once-per-8-products) (resolved; before that both rounded after every product, as `simdgroup_half8x8` does, and differed from the Switch in the low bits of most HMMAs). The host's network follows the helper, and `test_ndve_engine.py` fails when the two differ.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate): its title and opening movies ([issue 1375](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1375), PR #1894).

### agl's NDSR network on simdgroup matrices, in two passes

- **Symptom:** on an M1 or M2, the game's own upscaler takes about 5 ms of GPU a frame at docked resolution, most of it one compute dispatch: agl's NDSR network `fa3033d05c0a41d6` (`agl_ndsr_vr_light.pck`, 1920x1080 to 2560x1440). Ocarina of Time's Kakariko Village ran at 31-34.5 fps on an M2 Pro, 39 with that dispatch dropped (`DM_NVN_SKIP_CS`); its compute occupancy was 14 % (Metal System Trace, GPU counters).
- **Cause:** the translated kernel keeps NVIDIA's schedule. A threadgroup of 4 SIMD-groups covers 64x32 output pixels; each SIMD-group runs 8 patches of a three-block network (per block two groups of H = W1 . X, Y += H . W2, ReLU between blocks) over a 16x16 window of luma: 176 HMMA.16816 calls in straight-line code, the 12 weight matrices loaded as A fragments up front, every patch's tiles live at once (`hmma_msl` holds `dm_acc[16]`, `dm_am[4]`, `dm_bm[116]` in registers). On GPUs without Dynamic Caching that spills, and the pipeline allows 384 threads a threadgroup. Rewritten by hand on fixed inputs (a harness with the game's push constants and weights on the M2 Pro): no B cache gave nothing; weights read again for every use 4.7-5.5 ms; two patches at once 12.5 ms (spills); patch by patch, weights staged once per threadgroup in tile layout and per group H then Y at once (the fewest live tiles) 3.14 ms.
