That kernel alone, without the epilogue after the network, allows 1,024 threads and takes 1.99 ms: the epilogue's registers cap the whole kernel's occupancy.
- **Fix:** `dm/gfx/tools/dmsh_ndsr.py`, the last step of a compute shader in `dmsh_build.py`. It reads the translated MSL and proves that its HMMA calls compute this network: the addresses each call reads are evaluated for every thread of the 128 (the window's words at the lane's base + 12 bytes a patch, the weights' in PTX fragment order, 512 bytes a matrix), and each call's operands become terms through the helper's flags (A reused, a chain's accumulator slot, the cached B), up to the words the epilogue reads, which must be the 8 patches' last D words of the expected graph. Then it replaces the calls (and only them: the prologue, the values the epilogue takes from the core and the epilogue stay the shader's) with the network on `simdgroup_matrix` tiles: weights staged once per threadgroup (6 KB of threadgroup memory), per patch and block the helper's arithmetic (each 8 products summed in f32 and rounded once to f16, k 0-7 then 8-15), block 1 in transposed orientation (the same products in the same k order for each element), ReLU as translated, the last D word taken from the lane that holds it. And it splits the kernel by a bool function constant, `DMSH_SPLIT_FC` (218): undefined, one pass; false, pass 0, the prologue and the network, which writes each thread's 8 words to the buffer at compute slot `DMSH_SPLIT_SLOT` (29) and returns; true, pass 1, which fills no window, reads the words and runs the epilogue. The reflection says so (`"split": {"fc": 218, "buffer": 29, "words": 8}`); a kernel with HMMA calls the pass does not take keeps its translation, and its reflection says why (`ndsr_refused`), which the build's report counts. The host (`nvn_dispatch.inc` `split_pipes`, `split_dispatch`; the reflection's words in `nvn_refl.m`, which refuses another function constant or buffer) builds the two passes' pipelines beside the common one (`fn_off` masks `FN_SPLIT0` / `FN_SPLIT1`; the prewarm builds them with it, and `pso_offline.py` puts all three in the offline archive) and, for a direct dispatch, encodes pass 0 and pass 1 on the same grid in the same encoder, a dispatch barrier between and the words in frame scratch (7.0 MB a frame here). An indirect dispatch, a pass that does not build, or `DM_NVN_SPLIT=0` runs the one pass. Of Ocarina of Time's 71 shaders with HMMA calls, 58 reach the pass and `fa3033d05c0a41d6` alone is this network (the others have 46 to 576 calls, or another local size).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ndsr.py`: a synthetic kernel of the same graph in the translator's shape (no game code), recognised under plain operands and under TensorPass's chain slots, A reuse and interleaved patches; each change of the graph refused (a weight matrix, a window offset, the ReLU, the groups' order, a final word, a call, the lane), the source left as it was; on this Mac's GPU, the translated calls against the rewrite in one pass and in two, bit for bit. On the game's kernel in the harness (M2 Pro, random 1920x1080 input): the translation with the barrier of [Threadgroup stores in warp-synchronous code](#threadgroup-stores-in-warp-synchronous-code) 4.78 ms, the rewrite in one pass 3.15 ms, in two 2.42 ms, both identical to it in all 3,686,400 output pixels.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate): its docked upscaler.

### The game's movie kernels compiled although the host's engine runs them

- **Symptom:** a development build (`--platform mac`, no offline pipeline bundle) never shows the title movie: the run stops at the movie's first frame with the submit thread waiting in `newComputePipelineStateWithFunction`, and `sample` shows `MTLCompilerService` in LLVM's `InstructionCombiningPass` for minutes on end. A packed app does not show it: its bundle holds the pipelines.
- **Cause:** `dispatch_` made a compute shader's pipeline before asking whether the host draws the dispatch itself ([The NDVE movie network by the host](#the-ndve-movie-network-by-the-host), the game's upscaler through MetalFX). The 12 kernels of `nn::ndve`'s network were compiled at their first dispatch although the engine replaces every one, and Apple's compiler takes minutes on each on an M2 Pro. The engine was also made at the first colour conversion, in the background, so the network's first frame, one dispatch later, found it not ready and ran the game's kernels for that frame.
- **Fix:** general, in the host. A dispatch the host may take (`dispatch_host_may_take`: the network's kernels and the resampler with the engine on, the upscaler chain's shaders, the upscaler MetalFX draws) gets its pipeline only after the host has left it to the shader (`dispatch_cps`); other dispatches are unchanged. The network's first frame waits for the engine being made (`ndve_engine_dispatch`, bounded at 5 s; the wait is logged, 10.6 and 18.8 ms on an M2 Pro), so the game's kernels run only when the engine fails or is off.
- **Check:** Ocarina of Time on an M2 Pro with no bundle and an empty archive (`DM_PSO_BUNDLE=off`): the title movie and the file select play, the save loads, `NDVE engine: first movie frame by the engine (11 dispatches skipped)`, no `[RED]` line; before, the run never left the movie's first frame. With the app's bundle: the same pictures as before at 60 fps.
- **Open:** the prewarm lists still name the network's kernels (`C` rows), which a start without a bundle compiles in the background.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#frame-rate).

### Driver-segment reads outside fragment shaders

- **Symptom:** a vertex or compute shader reads zeros from NVN's driver segment `c0`.
- **Cause:** `dmsh_reflect.py`'s `drv_reads` scan matches only fragment-shader names (`fp_c0` / `fp_c2`), so vertex and compute shaders that read `c0` were never marked, and the host passed them an all-zero `c0`; these reads are not listed in the JSON.
- **Fix:** the host treats a declared driver-segment binding as read (`nvn_refl.m`). The words the host fills are in the entries below.
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening, Super Mario Odyssey, Pokémon Legends: Z-A.

### Compute `c0`: dispatch size and uniform buffer table

- **Symptom:** flickering hard-edged white tiles (tiled lighting with every tile row getting row 0's or other rows' lights).
- **Cause:** a compute shader that computes its tile index as workgroup y × `c0[0x200]` + workgroup x reads the dispatch's workgroup count from the driver segment, which the host left zero.
- **Fix:** for compute, `c0` carries the dispatch size at 0x200 (x, y, z) and the uniform buffer table at 0x210 + 16 r (address, size) in front of the storage buffer table at 0x310 (`encode_args_`).
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Link's Awakening (Mac and iPhone): the step of Marin and Tarin's doorway. The room shaders use tiled forward lighting: the fragment position picks a 64×64-pixel tile (`trunc(y / 64) × ceil(width / 64) + trunc(x / 64)`), whose light count and light indices come from two storage buffers that a compute shader (`light_culling_compute.bfsha`, one thread per workgroup, one workgroup per tile) fills every frame. Every row's workgroups wrote row 0's entries, the last writer won (a different one each frame), and the bottom tile row, where the step is, got the lights of other rows; a dump of the tile lists showed only the first of 17 rows filled. After the fix every tile row has its lights and the doorway shows the game's soft light from outside.
- **Check:** Breath of the Wild (101 compute and 4 vertex shaders read `c0`) and Super Mario 3D World (11 and 1) render the same as before on the Mac.

### Storage-buffer write flag in translation metadata

- **Symptom:** a storage buffer the shader stores to or uses atomically has `rw: false` in its generated reflection; replay does not record its GPU writes for dependent buffer reads.
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's `BufferUsageFlags.Write` is bit 0 (`1`), and `Program.cs` serializes that enum unchanged. The builder tested bit 1 (`2`), incorrectly marking every such descriptor read-only. Native reflection honors the explicit `rw` field, so its older fallback to the MSL pointer qualifier could not correct it.
- **Fix:** `reflect` tests bit 0 for both driver-table storage buffers and addresses carried in guest uniform buffers. Replay then uses its existing write-access and pending-write tracking paths. Rebuild shaders to replace the old metadata.
- **Found on:** NieR:Automata The End of YoRHa Edition: its geometry counter atomic and output stream both emitted translator flag `1` but reached reflection as read-only.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_reflect.py` exercises writable and read-only translator descriptors, including an address in a guest uniform.

### Graphics `c0 + 0x610`: the largest point size

- **Symptom:** point sprites of size 0 (Odyssey's Cap Kingdom fog never parted around Mario).
- **Cause:** a vertex shader that writes `gl_PointSize = min(size, c0[0x610])` read 0, because the host had nothing at that offset of the driver segment.
- **Fix:** `c0 + 0x610` holds the largest point size, and the host writes 2047 there for the graphics stages. Writing the `SetPointSize` value instead (always 1 in Odyssey) left one-pixel splats whose disc weight came out 0 (the fields held `-0.0`).
- **Found on:** Super Mario Odyssey (reported on an iPhone; fix checked on the Mac): on the Switch the ground fog opens around Mario as he walks through it. The fog's shading reads velocity fields that a small fluid simulation updates every frame: two 256×256 and two 128×128 RG16F targets, with a two-pass blur and a blended composite. Its only input is a point-sprite splat per mover, drawn with `alPointSpriteShader`: one vertex per mover, its size in the vertex data (Mario's main splat asks for about 18 px, others 3–5 px) and a soft disc in the fragment shader (`clamp(1 - 4r², 0, 1)` × velocity). The simulation fields stayed exactly zero (raw dumps with `DM_NVN_TEXDUMP` + `DM_NVN_SNAPRAW`; the 8-bit dump shows velocities as black either way). After the fix the fields hold velocities up to ±44, and the fog clears around Mario.

### Graphics `c0 + 0x644`: the draw's base instance

- **Symptom:** occlusion queries that always return 0 samples (Z-A: characters' legs folded back from the hips once they walked); every instanced draw's transform taken for instance 0.
- **Cause:** a shader that picks its transform with (instance index − base instance) + `c0[0x644]`: Maxwell's instance ID leaves the base instance out, and the NVN driver keeps it in its constant segment, where GLSLC reads it for `gl_InstanceID + gl_BaseInstance`. The host left `0x644` at 0.
- **Fix:** `encode_args` writes the draw's base instance there for graphics stages and does not reuse a stage's argument block across draws with different base instances. The word before it, `0x640`, is added to the vertex ID by 58 of Z-A's shaders and stays 0 until what the driver puts there is known.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Legends: Z-A: from the first step on, the player's legs stuck straight out behind her at hip height, and the rival's forward in a later cutscene; standing still beforehand looked right. The bone palette the game computes on the CPU was already bent. The cause was six occlusion queries a frame, one 36-vertex box each (`a793f436dcabd350`, base instance 61, 62, …), drawn against mip level 2 of the depth pyramid: every box was box 0, every query came back with 0 samples, and the game took its characters for hidden and posed their legs that way. Forcing every result to visible fixed the legs, and depth testing with ALWAYS did not. 793 of the game's vertex shaders read the word.
- **Note:** how it was found: the raycasts were logged with scratch observation gates (all correct), then `DM_HLECOUNT` showed nothing walking-specific, then the counter reports.

### The base instance of indirect draws comes from Metal

- **Symptom:** flat grey or background-coloured triangles with straight edges across part of the scene, changing shape from frame to frame while the camera stands still; dropping the colour pass's fragment shaders in groups (`DM_NVN_SKIP_FS`) did not remove them, as the instances are misplaced in the depth pre-pass too, whose depth the colour pass tests for equality.
- **Cause:** for an indirect draw (`DrawElementsIndirect` / `DrawArraysIndirect`) the base instance is in the argument record, which the game's GPU culling writes earlier in the same submission; `encode_args` could only write the command's base instance into `c0 + 0x644`, 0 for an indirect draw. A vertex shader whose instance is (instance index − base instance) + `c0[0x644]` then read its transforms from the first records for every indirect draw.
- **Fix:** the translation reads the word from Metal's `[[base_instance]]`, which holds every draw's base instance, direct, indirect or masked (`dmsh_entry.py` `vs_base_instance`: in vertex functions `c0[100].y` becomes the base instance's bits, and `main0` gets the parameter when it lacks it). `encode_args` still writes the word for the other stages.
- **Found on:** Super Mario Party Jamboree: every board. Its culling (`2b5485a2a731a231`, 62 points with rasterization off) writes 80-byte transforms per visible instance into a 30 KB buffer and counts them, `276504dc5c987ecf` writes nine indirect records from the count, and the instanced draws (`5a805bbb08421b86` in the depth pre-pass, `4b05556737e9535f` and `eaa3a44ba28f0744` in the colour pass) draws them, both reading record = instance + `c0[0x644]`. 5,249 of its vertex shaders read the word.

### Pixel `c0 + 0x620`: the sample index and GLSLC's sample position table

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: reads driver-bank locations at run time that the replay layer cannot fill: c0 dynamic index c0+0x630` (or `c0 dynamic index` alone), and that shader's draws are missing (`[RED] shader … (pixel) failed to load`); the build notes `Shader instruction Pixld is not implemented` for pixel shaders; `[RED] NVN unimplemented function called: nvnProgramSetSampleShading`.
- **Cause:** GLSLC reads the sample index with `PIXLD.MYINDEX` (RZ, offset 0; nouveau reads `gl_SampleID` the same way), which Ryujinx's translator left unwritten, so the register read as 0. For `gl_SamplePosition` (or interpolation at a sample) it then indexes a byte table the NVN driver keeps at `c0 + 0x620`: index = ((((y & w) << z) + (x & z)) << log2 samples) + (sample & mask), with {mask, log2 samples, z, w} at `c0 + 0x630` and x, y the pixel (`gl_FragCoord` over the render scale); each byte holds the sample's x (low nibble) and y in 1/16 pixel. The replay filled neither row, and the build refused any computed index into `c0`.
- **Fix:** the translator writes the sample index for `PIXLD.MYINDEX` (`IoVariable.SampleId`, SPIR-V `SampleId` with `SampleRateShading`; MSL `[[sample_id]]`, so the fragment function runs per sample on a multisampled target, and SPIRV-Cross moves `gl_FragCoord` to the sample); the other modes still log. `encode_args` fills both rows for the pixel stage from the pass's sample count (`c0_sample_rows`: Metal's default positions, the same for every pixel, so the grid masks are 0; one sample is 0x88, the pixel centre), and `dmsh_build.py` counts them as filled for pixel shaders (`SAMPLE_ROWS`), with a computed `c0` index accepted when the shader reads the 0x630 row, or when its range is proven (`index_range`: an integer expression of literals and the sample index, below 16, through single-assignment temporaries) to lie in the filled rows. `nvnProgramSetSampleShading` returns true: Metal has no switch to force per-sample shading, which a shader gets by reading the sample index.
- **Found on:** Metroid Prime Remastered: 438 of the 562 pixel shaders of `MaterialArchive.arc` read the sample index; in 30 the translated shader keeps it, and there it reaches the table, whose byte the G-buffer shaders pack into an integer target (`04023899305e93e1`). Its targets are single-sample (1600×900), so sample 0 was right and only the table mattered: 0 there put every sample in the pixel's corner. No other game here reads either row (58 games' libraries checked). The nibble order for several samples is the hardware's, not seen in a game. **NVN2's table is signed from the pixel centre:** PRAGMATA's 154 readers compute `(byte ^ 0x88)` nibbles / 16 - 0.5 and add it to the pixel centre, and `4a9d4507eea6c837` passes the nibbles unchanged to IPA's offset, which is signed from the centre ([IPA at an offset](#ipa-at-an-offset-and-the-barycentric-coordinates)): both are right only when the byte holds each coordinate minus 8, so one sample at the centre is 0x00 there, not 0x88. With the corner form every sample position read half a pixel up and left. `c0_sample_rows` now writes that form when the sdk is the Switch 2's (`dm_sdk_ounce`). Whether NVN1's table is the same was not checked (this game only stores the byte, and its files are not at hand): open. PRAGMATA: 156 pixel shaders read the byte at `0x620 + sample` without the grid and without the 0x630 row (`a689530d00090ebf`: `_m0[(sample + 1568) >> 4]`, row 98), and were refused (`c0 dynamic index`) until the build bounded the index; all 156 load, the other 6,753 of its shaders that read `c0` or `c2` keep their result. Check: `DriverReads.test_sample_byte_by_the_sample_index_is_filled_in_a_pixel_shader`.

### Texture and sampler pools with overlapping IDs

- **Symptom:** a menu's labels drawn as plain bars, other menus empty except for the cursor (one pool's textures overwritten by another's); a black screen when IDs past a fixed table read as empty slots.
- **Cause:** a game that binds two texture pools and two sampler pools whose IDs overlap; the replay had one ID table.
- **Fix:** each pool has a base in the table, in creation order; `RegisterTexture` / `RegisterSampler` add it, and binds recorded on a command buffer add the base of the pool that command buffer last set (`bind_handles` in `hle_nvn.c`, the base kept in `cmd_t`, [next entry](#bases-of-bound-handles-kept-in-the-command-buffer); log line `pool #2 (…): numbered from N`). Handles read from uniform buffers take the base of the pool bound at replay (DARK SOULS: REMASTERED, `ubo_tex_id` / `ubo_samp_id`, [Handles from uniform buffers take the bound pools' bases](#handles-from-uniform-buffers-take-the-bound-pools-bases)). Deferred binds are not remapped (red line; [Deferred texture binds read at execution](#deferred-texture-binds-read-at-execution)). The table's capacity is the sum of the pools.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem: Three Houses: the scene binds a 12,544-entry texture pool and a 3,856-entry sampler pool, the 2D UI's command buffer binds 4,096-entry ones, and both fill IDs from 256. The scene's default 8×8 white texture overwrote the UI's textures (the title menu's labels drew as plain bars). The scene registers each draw's textures into fresh IDs of a ring (8,729 and up a few seconds in), which used to read as empty slots and left the screen black.

### Bases of bound handles kept in the command buffer

- **Symptom:** red lines from the list of command buffers that bound a non-first pool (6,734 in one DARK SOULS: REMASTERED run).
- **Cause:** that list had 64 entries and was never cleared, and the game binds a non-first pool in more than 64.
- **Fix:** originally the bases lived in each command buffer, removing the fixed-size lookup. That was incomplete for inherited pools; [execution-relative descriptor handles](#descriptor-handles-use-the-execution-pools) now keep pool selection only in queue execution state.
- **Found on:** DARK SOULS: REMASTERED.

### Descriptor handles use the execution pools

- **Symptom:** terrain is magenta, trees are white and colour conversion reads uninitialized data; the compute pass dump shows `W ... tid=360 dummy` despite a registered writable 3D texture.
- **Cause:** explicit binds added host descriptor-table bases at recording, using the pools that particular command buffer had bound. Civilization VI records its colour-LUT dispatch in a list that inherits a different pool at execution: relative image index 360 should resolve to host ID 30824, not 360. The dispatch wrote a dummy image, leaving the sampled 32³ LUT unwritten. A pool change after a bind had the same error, and deferred binds did not apply any base.
- **Fix:** all recorded handles and binding state remain the hardware's relative 32-bit words. `bound_handle`, `bound_tex` and `bound_img` resolve them against the queue's active texture and sampler pools when consumed: sampled textures, buffer textures, images, separate and constant samplers, driver-table arrays, feedback/dependency checks and `DrawTexture`. Uniform-memory handles already follow that rule. Pool changes invalidate argument blocks. A single bind still puts its patchable handle 16 bytes into the command, always in the short record. The capture record layout changes to version 2; captures with the old absolute IDs must be recorded again.
- **Check:** `t_descriptor_pools` records real NVN binds without a pool, inherits a non-first pool, changes pools without rebinding, changes deferred handles after recording and writes two distinct 3D images through the same image slot. Both this test and `t_tbuf_handle` run under Metal validation. `t_nvn` checks compact/canonical records and patchable single binds; `t_capture` rejects the old record layout. Binding of Isaac and Xenoblade Chronicles 2 quick regressions pass.
- **Found on:** Sid Meier's Civilization VI, colour-LUT compute `06932ac941e93119` and final colour conversion `65381f87fa4f1382`.

### Handles from uniform buffers take the bound pools' bases

- **Symptom:** distant walls and floors smeared sideways (DARK SOULS: REMASTERED): tiling textures took a clamp-to-edge sampler and repeated their edge column.
- **Cause:** the map materials read their combined texture / sampler handles from a uniform buffer (`fp_t_cb13_*`, resolved at replay by `ubo_handle`), and the game keeps several sampler pools, each with its own samplers under the same IDs, one per command buffer that records the scene. The replay resolved uniform-buffer handles in the first pool.
- **Fix:** the replay keeps the texture and sampler pools bound (`OP_SET_TEX_POOL` / `OP_SET_SAMP_POOL`, which it used to skip; a command buffer that binds none keeps the queue's, as on the GPU) and adds their bases to handles read from uniform buffers (`ubo_tex_id` / `ubo_samp_id` in `nvn_args.inc`).
- **Found on:** DARK SOULS: REMASTERED: the map materials `FRPG_Phn_*Lit*`; 12 sampler pools of 2,304 entries (ID 261 is clamp-to-edge with LOD 0..0 in one pool, repeat with mips in others); the walls' tiling base textures (u ≈ −3.45).
- **Check:** found by highlighting the six most common fragment shaders of the scene pass (`DM_NVN_HILITE`), then the next six, and reading the wall draw's binding lines (`DM_NVN_PASSDUMP_ALL`).

### Deferred texture binds read at execution

- **Symptom:** the 3D scene black, only particles and the HUD drawn; pass dump `B` lines all `tid=0 dummy`.
- **Cause:** `nvnCommandBufferBindTexturesDeferred` passes a pointer to the handle, and "deferred" means the GPU reads the handle from that address *when the command executes*. A game that records its command blocks once, submits them every frame and writes the handles afterwards got the handles the host copied at recording, mostly zeros, so draws read the dummy texture 0.
- **Fix:** the bind record keeps the source address (`p_bind_handles.defer / src`) and every replay-time reader refreshes the handles first (`bh_fresh` in `nvn_descpool.inc`).
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild: the engine binds almost every texture this way (`main+0xec2e64`, one handle, the pointer being the handle field inside the texture-sampler object); the deferred-lighting composite (one full-screen triangle per material class, blending into the HDR target) read texture 0 for all of its inputs.
- **Note:** how it was found: a frame data-flow table built from `DM_NVN_PASSDUMP_ALL` (per pass: attachments, shader keys, sampled serials) showed the half-resolution lighting outputs never being read, and the composite pass's `B` lines were all `tid=0 dummy`. Without `_ALL` the dump lists only the first draw of each pass, which hid the composite. Deferred binds are not remapped by pool base ([Texture and sampler pools with overlapping IDs](#texture-and-sampler-pools-with-overlapping-ids)).

### Handles in uniform buffers written earlier in the same submission

- **Symptom:** bindless handles read from a uniform buffer name the wrong texture when the game writes them earlier in the same submission.
- **Cause:** the replay reads handles on the CPU, and a write queued earlier in the submission has not landed yet.
- **Fix:** inline uniform updates keep where their data sits, so the handle is read from the newest one covering it (`pend_read`). The queued writes are indexed by granule in one hash, newest first in each chain: a write goes in at the finest granule size (4 KB, then each eight times the last: `pend_shift`) over which it spans at most 8 granules, and a lookup walks the chains of the granules it touches at each size the submission used (`g_pend_lv`). Nothing caps the count: the old limit of 256 writes per submission went first (Monster Hunter Rise), then writes over more than 8 pages, which had a list of their own that stopped at 256 and that every lookup scanned. Past it the submission counted as a write anywhere (`g_pend_over`), and every handle in it was read from memory, under updates still queued (`RED 47`, `more writes than the host tracks`). Merely growing that list would have made each lookup cost about 0.25 µs per thousand such writes (a CPU-only benchmark of the tracker, Apple M4 Pro); with the index a lookup takes 7–19 ns from 256 to 20,000 of them.
- **Found on:** Monster Hunter Rise (it queues more than 256 writes per submission); Mortal Kombat 11 (`0100F2200C984000`, not yet in the games table: GitHub PR #2801), whose fragment shaders read handles from slot 3 after more than 256 large writes in a submission.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=<id> check_t_pend_index` (CPU only, with AddressSanitizer: 9,000 large writes, 10,002 over one handle, writes from 16 bytes to 1 GB at their edges, and 20,000 mixed writes and lookups against a scan of every entry; 8 of its checks fail with the old list).

### Storage buffer addresses in the game's own uniform buffer

- **Symptom:** a storage buffer reported with `cb_slot` 3, which had no NVN slot.
- **Cause:** the shader reads a storage buffer's address from the game's own uniform buffer (c3 + 0x38), which Ryujinx reports with `cb_slot` 3.
- **Fix:** the build marks it `sbuf_ubo` (uniform slot `cb_slot - 3`, byte offset), and the replay reads the 64-bit address there at bind time, as it reads bindless handles.
- **Found on:** Monster Hunter Rise (about 30 compute shaders).

### Global memory at an address loaded from memory

- **Symptom:** whatever a shader writes through a pointer it read from memory never happens. The build summary counted `Failed to find storage buffer for global memory operation "Store"`, and the shader's MSL had a store function with an empty body. In Donkey Kong Bananza the tunnel's walls and floor were black and only the loose rocks drew.
- **Cause:** Ryujinx turns a global memory access into a storage buffer access by tracing its address back to a constant buffer entry (`GlobalToStorage`). An address the shader loaded from memory traces to none, and Ryujinx dropped the access: a store did nothing, a load read 0. Donkey Kong Bananza's gsys vertex shader `c9a56f796ea4a4cd` draws 96 points every frame. Each point loads one indirect draw record's 64-bit address from its storage buffer and writes {0, 1, 0, 0, 0} there (count 0, one instance) before the terrain's culling geometry shader adds its triangles to the count. Without those stores the records kept instance count 0, and their counts grew frame after frame. NVN2 draws instance count 0 as nothing. Its indirect draw (`sdk+0x6049b0`) calls macro 27 (driver constants at 0x640) and macro 40 with the record's five words. Macro 40, decoded with Mesa's MME decoder after running the driver's macro upload (`sdk+0x60fd40`) in an emulator, skips its draw loop when the instance count is 0 and never writes the record.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain. Accesses traced to one or more storage buffers translate as before; only those traced to none change:
  - **Translator** (`GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateRawStorageOp`): reserves the host's pool table as a storage buffer under a sentinel constant buffer slot (`DMSH_KIND_GMEM_MAP`, `dmsh_abi.h` `DMSH_GMEM_MAP_*`). It finds the pool holding the address by the entry it chose last ([Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry](#raw-global-memory-lookups-that-start-from-their-last-entry)), else a tagged hint for its 2 MiB chunk, else a branchless binary search over the table's starts, adds that pool's delta and accesses the result through a physical storage buffer pointer. In MSL that is `reinterpret_cast<device uint*>(ulong)`.
  - **Why a delta:** a no-copy Metal buffer's `gpuAddress` is not its CPU address, so the guest address has to move by the pool's delta (GPU address minus guest address).
  - **Host table** (`nvn_args.inc` `gmem_map`): built from the memory pools whenever they change, 8,191 ranges at most (a pool past that is not reached, with a red line). It lists only the pools something gave a GPU address in, and those are made resident while such a shader is in use ([Memory pools nothing gave an address to stay out of the raw pool table](memory.md#memory-pools-nothing-gave-an-address-to-stay-out-of-the-raw-pool-table)). Each 2 MiB chunk wholly inside an entry gets a hint in entry (chunk & 8191): the chunk as a tag, and the index of the entry holding it. A colliding chunk or a smaller pool searches.
  - **Write log:** where a store through a loaded pointer lands is known only once the GPU has run it. Counting it as a write anywhere refused every uniform shadow of the submission: Donkey Kong Bananza's tunnel ran at about 70 ms a frame, with 4,533 passes ended to land deferred updates. So each store or atomic also sets the bit of its table entry in a write log the host hands each draw (`DMSH_KIND_GMEM_LOG`, `gml_take`), and the completion handler reads the bits against the same table. The word is read first: a bit is set once, so nearly every store costs a load, not an atomic ([A shader that writes a whole frame through raw global memory](frame-rate.md#a-shader-that-writes-a-whole-frame-through-raw-global-memory)). A bitmap cannot run out of slots, as the hash set of pages it replaced did.
  - **Learned pools:** the host takes each logged entry's range. The stores count as a write anywhere until the learned pools have stayed the same over 8 logs, then as writes over those pools. A later log that shows another range adds it, without going back to a write anywhere: a write anywhere made every later geometry draw of the submission wait for the GPU in nxnative (`geo_cpu_hazard`; the CPU readers it holds back here are `wl_cpu_hazard` and `mdi_live`), and going back for 8 logs after each miss gave Donkey Kong Bananza streaks of 300-2,000 ms frames (38 such frames in 5,400, 6 after the change, the geometry wait in one 25 s sample 3,490 → 264 ms). The host then checks whether the miss could have mattered: every uniform shadow taken from that submission on (its own and those of the later submissions encoded while it ran, each recorded with its fence) is compared with the ranges the stores wrote outside the prediction, whole pools now, so this check can report a shadow elsewhere in the pool. Only an overlap, or a record that has wrapped before the check, prints a `[RED]` line; a miss no shadow saw is logged with the pools' ages.
  - **Why pools, not pages:** the game allocates new records as the level streams in, in the same pools. Learned page by page, the tunnel still met a new page 213 logs in.
  - **Result in Donkey Kong Bananza:** 4 pools learned over 9 logs at first, 60 fps (p50 16.64 ms), and no pass ended to land deferred updates. Further into the level the stores keep reaching pools the set does not hold yet: 12 in a 17,700-frame session, 10 in a scripted 13,500-frame run. Those pools are not new: they were added 3.5 to 294 s before the miss (the game streams records into pools it made earlier), so a new pool would not have announced them. None of the 7 misses checked had a page under a shadow (3 to 5 shadows each).
  - **Still dropped:** an address in no pool. Atomics on 8- and 16-bit values are not implemented.
- **A full table loses geometry silently:** a pool past the table's end is not reached, so a load from it reads 0. Ocarina of Time holds about 4,170 ranges, and at the former 4,095 entries its foliage and moss were missing: black shapes where moss should cover them, and the sky through the ground (restored at 8,191 entries, and back again with the host capped at 4,095). The only sign was one red line (`raw global memory: N more memory pools than the … entries`), so treat that line as a picture fault.
- **Open:** the prediction is checked, not proven: a miss that does reach a shadow is reported (`[RED]`) after the frame, not prevented. The records' pointers come from memory, so the pools they will reach are not known ahead of the draw.
- **Found on:** Donkey Kong Bananza: its terrain, and agl's compute shader `49bc2268925e25b6` (one load); rebuilding the chain changed only these two of Donkey Kong Bananza's shaders. Vertex shader `588e242a52f5f7aa` (five stores) has one too, the same shader in The Legend of Zelda: Tears of the Kingdom and Super Mario Bros. Wonder, so likely one the SDK ships; neither drew it in its regression route.

### Global memory at a storage buffer's address on some paths only

- **Symptom:** GPU-driven geometry never draws: its indirect draw records keep count 0 and its visibility buffer stays clear, although the culling compute runs every frame. A persistent culling kernel takes its work items (its work counter passes the item count) and writes nothing. Its build notes do not say `traced to no storage buffer`: the loads are traced, to a storage buffer the address is in on only some paths. In PRAGMATA the title's Earth and Moon and the intro's ship and cockpit were black.
- **Cause:** for an address that is a phi, `GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateMultiTargetStorageOp` collects the storage buffers its sources trace to and makes a helper that range-checks each one. A source it cannot trace (an address the shader loaded from memory) was skipped without a word, and the helper returned 0 when the address was in none of the buffers. That case is exactly the untraced source. PRAGMATA's cluster culling `251426e9d44fd558` reads each instance's mesh header at an offset of its push+0x170 buffer on one path and at an address from its table at push+0x110 on the other, in other pools. Every header read gave 0, every instance 0 clusters, so the chain after it (`3c5dde027a045df7`, `1b16be7a9ee2c490`, which fills the mesh draws' records) had nothing to count. Found with counters added to the kernel's MSL (a copy laid over the library from a folder beside its image): 59,000 items reached the header test, 0 reached the header read, 0 reached either output store.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain (`GlobalToStorage.cs`). A source that is neither traced nor another phi marks the access. Its helper keeps the range checks, and on a miss calls the pool table helper (`TryGenerateRawStorageOp`, [Global memory at an address loaded from memory](#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)) instead of returning 0. A tag after the buffers keeps it apart from a helper whose sources are all traced. An access the table cannot serve (an atomic on an 8- or 16-bit value) keeps the 0. The build notes say `traced to a storage buffer on some paths only`.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA (`0400C76024710000`): the title's 3D and the intro's mesh-shader geometry.

### Bindless constant buffer handles in the hardware's layout

- **Symptom:** an NVN2 shader that reads a constant buffer through a bindless handle (`cx[UR][offset]`) gets a dummy buffer or zeros, with `[RED] shader <key>: the buffer address the game pushed at c[24] + 0x… is 0x1800830776a28, in no memory pool: bound to a dummy buffer` (an "address" of 49 bits, with a small number in its top bits).
- **Cause:** the handle is not an address. Its layout is the hardware's (as NVK's `nvk_bindless_cbuf`): bits 0..44 hold the address >> 4 and bits 45..63 the size >> 4. The translator (`InstEmitAmpere.cs` `ABindlessConstant`) used the handle as the address, and that worked only for the handles the host writes itself (compute's uniform entries in c[0]), because the host wrote plain addresses there. A game that builds its own handles reached no pool. PRAGMATA pushes them: its SDF compute shader `b12078fe3bce1273` takes three at push block 1, `0x5a0083055d124` = 32 bytes at `0x83055d1240`, the other two 192 bytes each. Its pixel shaders also load such handles with `LDG.64` from the game's own buffers. An earlier attempt to decode the handle in the shader alone met the host's plain addresses: wild reads, a GPU timeout and page faults at the first 3D frame.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain and the host.
  - **Translator:** `ABindlessConstant` decodes the handle: the address is `(handle & (2^45 - 1)) << 4`.
  - **Storage pass** (`GlobalToStorage.cs` `TryUnwrapHandle`): traces that address (handle << 4) back to the handle's constant buffer words as it traces an address, and marks the target (`SearchResult.Handle`, bit 15 of a packed target). A multi-target helper compares the decoded address and size.
  - **Host** (`nvn_mtl.m`): writes NVN2's uniform entries in the hardware's layout (`ucb_handle`; NVN1 shaders read the plain address there, Link's Awakening's `c0[0x280]`). The readers of a pushed or a uniform-buffer word (`NVN_RB_SBUFQ`, `NVN_RB_SBUFU`) decode a handle (`ucb_handle_addr`): a handle of any size has a bit at 45 or above, which no guest address has, so a word with a plain address is read as before.
  - **Traces unchanged:** a 312-shader sample of PRAGMATA's library (every stage, with uniform, pushed and raw bindings) built with the same bindings and raw-access notes as before.
- **Open:** a read past the handle's size returns what lies there; the hardware returns 0.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA (`0400C76024710000`).

### A uniform word << 4 taken for a bindless handle where it is the offset

- **Symptom:** skinned meshes lose parts or explode: players without heads in the lineup cutscene, and with the uniform test rewrite on, giant triangles over the pitch at pauses and in cutscenes. No red line; the shaders build. In a shader's Metal source, a storage buffer read's word index is `c[x][y] >> 2` where the previous chain had `(c[x][y] << 4) >> 2`.
- **Cause:** `GlobalToStorage.FindUniqueBaseAddressCb`, for an address that adds two sources, replaced each source that `TryUnwrapHandle` matched (a constant buffer word << 4, [the address of a bindless handle](#bindless-constant-buffer-handles-in-the-hardwares-layout)) by the word itself, before choosing which source is the base. When the other source was a buffer's address (c[0], or a pushed entry in c[24]), the shifted word was the offset, and the access went to the unshifted word: element y / 16 of a 16-byte array instead of element y. The match also ran on Maxwell code, which has no bindless constant buffers, so on NVN1 it was always wrong. Ampere code meets the same offset through `LEA` (index << 4 plus an address).
- **Fix:** general, in the chain (`GlobalToStorage.cs`). `TryUnwrapHandle` matches only in Ampere code (`TranslationFlags.Ampere`, through `TransformContext.Ampere`), and there the handle's words stand for a source only as the base; the source chosen as the offset keeps its shifted value.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_storage_offset.py`: a Maxwell compute shader (c[0] + 0x110 plus c[3][4] << 4) and an Ampere one (a pushed entry plus c[3][0x10] << 4 by `LEA`); both fail on chain ff7c2e3ba2b4. On the PR author's build (PR #1129), 23 vertex and 8 compute shaders of EA SPORTS FC 27's 7,875 had lost the shift, and all 31 matched chain 2afd39090b76 (before the handle pass) again with the base-only rule.
- **Found on:** EA SPORTS FC 27 (`0100C49025D3E000`): its skinning vertex shaders (`0a82d49f3c969ed1` among them) and compute shaders.

### Raw global memory through a pointer read when the draw is bound

- **Symptom:** a raw load reads 0 when its pointer names a pool omitted from the raw table. Ocarina of Time's eight culling task shaders then compute `(min(end - start, 256) - 1) >> 5` from two zero range ends: 134,217,727 iterations and an isolated GPU hang.
- **Cause:** [the address filter](memory.md#memory-pools-nothing-gave-an-address-to-stay-out-of-the-raw-pool-table) lists pools whose GPU addresses NVN handed out. A pointer derived by the game can reach another pool. Refreshing the table at commit handles pools created after encoding, but does not discover these existing, unaddressed targets.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator, reflection and host. `GlobalToStorage.FindPointerSite` traces fixed storage-buffer words contributing a raw load's address, including across mixed traced/loaded pointer paths. `dmsh_reflect.gptr_sites` records up to 64 supported `[NVN storage slot, byte offset]` sources on the existing `gmem_map` binding. Before argument-block reuse as well as a fresh encode, `gmem_discover` reads each complete pointer word, including a covering queued inline update. It checks the binding's size, the source pool and sparse mapping before a CPU read. A valid target admits its logical pool or mapped run through `mtl_pool_addressed`; the existing commit-time table refresh and write-log pairing then apply. Texture-only pools with no discovered pointer remain excluded. There is no extra GPU binding, site check, or change to scalar/vector raw helpers.
- **Limits:** a pointer generated later by the GPU, a dynamically indexed source, or an unsupported source descriptor still relies on the addressed pool table. Discovery does not establish a fix for the separate whole-mesh GPU hang. The memory line reports pointer words read and newly addressed logical ranges; those bytes are not a measurement of additional wired memory.
- **Checks:** `test_dmsh_raw_pointers.py` translates scalar, 64-bit, 128-bit and mixed-path loads; `test_dmsh_reflect.py` and `t_refl` check metadata. `t_gmem_words` checks an omitted target, commit refresh, changed pointer data without rebinding, bounds and queued inline updates; `t_vpool_first_map` exercises an unmapped sparse source and a word crossing a mapped run's end. The existing raw-helper GPU tests cover scalar/vector reads and misses. [PR #1585](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1585) supplies the original diagnosis: on an M3 each of the eight task shaders hung with its pool omitted (about 22 ms GPU time) and completed with its target supplied; its original site-table implementation filled 1,091,044 sites over 2,700 frames on an M2 Pro 16 GB. Those are contributor results for that implementation, not measurements of this table-population revision.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry

- **Symptom:** a pass whose shaders read memory through loaded addresses takes several milliseconds on the GPU for little work. `[gpuprof]` names the pass, and its shaders' build notes say `Global memory operation "Load" traced to no storage buffer`. Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh shader `73dc0b90c4082e43` (`Foliage.Product.110`, 135 such reads) took 10.9 ms of a 683x384 frame; 2,241 of the game's shaders (vertex, compute, fragment and mesh) have such a lookup, some hundreds of them.
- **Cause:** each 32-bit word found its pool on its own ([Global memory at an address loaded from memory](#global-memory-at-an-address-loaded-from-memory)): the hint of its 2 MiB chunk, and on a miss the 13-step search of the pool table. A shader reads a structure word after word from one pool, so all but the first lookup of a run chose the entry the first one chose.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator (`GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateRawStorageOp`). An invocation keeps the pool entry its raw accesses chose last in private words that all its helpers share (`ResourceManager.GetRawCacheMemory`: the start's low and high word, the span, the delta's low and high word, and the entry's number when the shader stores or runs atomics, for the write log; zero at the start through a SPIR-V null initializer, `MemoryDefinition.ZeroInit`; spirv-cross declares them `uint _N = 0u;` in `main0` and passes them to the helpers). An address whose high word is the start's and whose offset from the start is below the span takes that entry without reading the table. Otherwise `dmsh_raw_find` takes the chunk's hint or the search, checks the entry's exact 64-bit end and returns the entry, whose words the helper caches, or 0 for an address in no pool, which leaves the cache as it was. The host stores a 32-bit span only for a range within one 4 GiB window, so a zero span (the start, a range across a 4 GiB boundary) always goes to `dmsh_raw_find`. `dmsh_raw_find` takes no reference parameters, so Metal keeps the cache in registers across its call. Loads, stores and atomics all do it, and a store's write log bit is the chosen entry's. It holds because the table's ranges are disjoint (`nvn_args.inc` `gmem_map`) and the host binds one table for a whole draw or dispatch. `dmsh_meshstage.py` keeps thread variables declared ahead of the stage memory in `dm_stage_body`: until then Ocarina of Time's 98 mesh and 11 task shaders with such a lookup failed the mesh stage (`no kernel main0 starting with its stage memory`).
- **The range in registers** (upstream nxnative c4c63f58): until then each helper kept only the entry's number and read five of its words from the table at every hit. Upstream measured on Cyberpunk 2077 on an M6 (171 shaders with such a lookup laid over its library): GPU time p50 36.15 → 32.98 ms while its dynamic resolution rose, and at a matched 1.104 MP of rendering 22.78 → 18.91 ms (−17 %). In Ocarina of Time 2,299 shaders call `dmsh_raw_find` (804 vertex, 605 pixel, 731 compute, 148 mesh, 11 task; the 1.0.0 library of chain `14495341b2c9`). On this game's M2 Pro (macOS 27.2, 1.0.0, headless, standing in the Spirit Temple, 2026-11-28; chain `401e0eebe72f` with the range in registers against `14495341b2c9`, the rest of each host the same) it is neutral: GPU p50 20.85 / 20.92 / 20.90 ms against 21.12 / 21.08 ms in the scene's first part, about 31.0 against 30.65 ms in its heavier part; the screenshots match.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py`: the translated helper's shape (one shared cache of five words, six with a write; no table read before the call to `dmsh_raw_find`, which takes no reference), and on the GPU its reads over runs of addresses that stay in, leave and come back to pools (adjacent pools, a gap, a hinted chunk above 4 GiB, a range straddling a 4 GiB boundary, a miss with the same low word in another window), and a load and atomics sharing a cache: a hit, a pool across 4 GiB and an address in no pool, with the pools' bits in the write log. `test_dmsh_meshstage.py` for the thread variables. The entry's number alone, on Ocarina of Time on an M6 Mac (macOS 27.2), main's host, the 2,241 shaders of the new chain laid over main's libraries (the other 2,592 shaders built with them came out with the same MSL), the same scripted walk from `save_ref` with warmed pipeline archives, two runs a side: at the spawn point right after loading (the same view, about 2,500 draws a frame) the GPU span p50 went 16.53 / 16.41 → 14.11 / 14.57 ms, and over the walk 15.7 → 14.0 ms. Screenshots at the spawn point match, and so do the red lines.
- **Open:** over that walk the frame time p95 went 19 → 26 ms. The runs ended in different places (the route is timed and the game moves at its frame rate), the new side's render target loads were higher (the game's dynamic resolution taking the headroom), and the Mac had 8 GB of swap in use, so this is not settled. Keeping the range itself in registers (PR #1464's form), tried on an M4 Pro (2026-11-08) as an overlay of Ocarina's eleven hot task and mesh shaders, changed nothing (GPU p50 16.45 → 16.61 ms, two runs each): then the time went to the words of each 128-bit load being read one by one ([128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time](#128-bit-raw-global-loads-read-one-word-at-a-time)), since fixed. The range in registers on this game's whole library is to measure.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md); the diagnosis, the cache and the first measurements in [PR 1464](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1464) and [PR 1405](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1405#issuecomment-6678379920).

### Raw global lookups built once for every call

- **Symptom:** a pipeline whose shader reads memory through loaded addresses takes seconds to compile on a cold Metal cache, and the GPU waits at its first draw ([Native mesh pipelines compiled at their first draw](#native-mesh-pipelines-compiled-at-their-first-draw)); its library is large for its MSL. Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh shader `3439aaa071d868ae` makes 141 raw accesses; its pipeline took 1.9 s on an M2 Air, and the route's 54 task / mesh pipelines 25 s at the first start.
- **Cause:** the pool table lookup of a raw access ([Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry](#raw-global-memory-lookups-that-start-from-their-last-entry)) is a helper function, and spirv-cross marks every function `static inline __attribute__((always_inline))` (it ignores SPIR-V's `DontInline`). Metal's compiler built a copy of the last-entry check, the chunk hint, the binary search and the physical-pointer access at each access.
- **Fix:** general, in the chain. The translator puts the chunk hint and the binary search in one function per shader, `int dmsh_raw_find(address low, address high)`, which returns the entry (`GlobalToStorage.cs` `GetRawFindFunction`; `SpirvGenerator` keeps `dmsh_` names), and the access helpers call it past the last entry's check. The build (`dmsh_rewrite.py` `raw_noinline`, called from `dmsh_build.py` `finish` for every stage) writes that function `static __attribute__((noinline))` outside task and mesh functions: one copy the misses branch to. Task and mesh functions keep the inline search since chain 43187e203d02 ([A noinline call in a task or mesh function locks up an M2's GPU](#a-noinline-call-in-a-task-or-mesh-function-locks-up-an-m2s-gpu)), mesh functions also as a [compiler workaround](#a-noinline-raw-lookup-crashes-the-m1-and-m2-mesh-compiler), so the mesh pipeline times below are those of chains bd23f9ff1f64 to 9eecb8412308. The last entry's check, the entry's end, the delta and the access stay inline at each access, so a hit runs as before. Writing the whole access helper `noinline` ([PR 2769](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2769)'s first form) compiled faster still but put the last entry and the vector words behind a call at every access: on an M4 Pro, Ocarina's still Kokiri Forest scene took 17.4 / 17.7 ms of GPU time a frame against 15.0 / 15.2 on main, in every pass with raw accesses.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), on main 74181da8 (2026-11-16). Offline Metal 4 pipeline compiles (`metal-tt`, 5 runs each in turn, on an M4 Pro), main → this fix: `3439aaa071d868ae`'s mesh pipeline with pixel shader `bd09ab5fc4fd7680` 1.67 → 0.66 s for the M2 (`applegpu_g14g`; whole helper `noinline`: 0.48 s), 1.81 → 0.70 s for the M4 Pro (`applegpu_g16s`), its archive 874 → 425 KB; the vertex pipeline of `ffc2a130a97593c3` (216 accesses) with pixel shader `9ef3ef25d67ef099`, 1.38 → 0.73 s for the M2, 828 → 449 KB; the compute kernel `d15370a382630d4e` (384 accesses) 132 → 109 s (median of 3; most of its time is elsewhere), 3.2 → 1.6 MB. The 938 playtested shaders with raw accesses have main libraries of 233 → 109 MB; the playtested build compiled in 14 instead of 19 minutes. GPU time on the M4 Pro, the still Kokiri Forest route (`kokiri-still`, 1920x1080, warm pipelines, steady frames): 15.2 / 15.2 / 15.3 ms a frame on main, 14.8 / 15.0 with the fix. Earlier, with the whole helper `noinline` on an older chain (c39ed5ebd458) and a 16 GB M2 Air, the pipeline compiled cold in 1.92 → 0.46 s and the Kokiri Forest walk's frame time stayed within its runs' noise at 683x384.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_noinline.py`: `dmsh_raw_find` marked, the access helpers and a function of the same shape without the name left inline, the pass idempotent, and the result compiles with `xcrun metal`. `test_dmsh_raw_last.py` (with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`) runs the translator's scalar and 128-bit lookups on the GPU with the search inline and `noinline`.

### A noinline call in a task or mesh function locks up an M2's GPU

- **Symptom:** on an M2 Pro the picture flickers once, or freezes for a moment, and after a few of those the game quits (`launcher: the host exited with 70`). The log has `Caused GPU Hang Error (00000003:kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorHang)` on a command buffer whose shader list ([Naming the shaders of a GPU fault](rendering.md#naming-the-shaders-of-a-gpu-fault)) holds task and mesh shaders, then `Ignored (for causing prior/excessive GPU errors)` on every submission ([A GPU whose access macOS revoked stops the game](rendering.md#a-gpu-whose-access-macos-revoked-stops-the-game)). macOS writes one `/Library/Logs/DiagnosticReports/gpuEvent-dmhost-*.ips` per restart, with `"restart_reason_desc":"firmware-detected lockup"` and `fw_ta_state` busy (the geometry stage: vertex, task and mesh functions). It comes at some camera cuts and area loads and at moments with no pattern; standing or walking in the same place does not show it.
- **Cause:** not found, and it lies in Apple's compiler or firmware for that GPU. Since [Raw global lookups built once for every call](#raw-global-lookups-built-once-for-every-call) the pool table search of a raw global access, `dmsh_raw_find`, was written `static __attribute__((noinline))` in every stage, so a task or mesh function with such an access held a real call. With that call an M2 Pro (`applegpu_g14s`) locks up in its geometry stage; with the same function `always_inline`, as spirv-cross writes it and as every chain before had it, it does not. The source is not at fault as far as it can be read: the search has no loop (the hint, then 13 fixed steps), the inline and the called form read the same, and the loops of the two task shaders in most failed buffers are bounded (`a5288d0a6c4971fd`'s Hi-Z rectangle by `c3[193]`, `f75708d176bb6591`'s batches by `min(count, 256) / 32`; both clamp the mesh grid to 1,024). `a5288d0a6c4971fd` holds no raw access and its library did not change; it is in every failed buffer because it is in every pass with models. Open: what in the call the GPU stops on, whether the task or the mesh function alone is enough, and whether other Apple GPUs do the same (the change was measured on an M4 Pro, with no hang reported).
- **Fix:** a workaround, general, in the chain: `dmsh_rewrite.py` `raw_noinline` keeps the search `always_inline` in a task or mesh function (stage `ts` or `ms`: `dmsh_build.py` `finish` passes the shader's `mesh_stage.kind`), so those two stages build the search at every access again and their pipelines compile as slowly as before that entry (`3439aaa071d868ae`'s 1.67 s against 0.66 s for the M2, offline). Vertex, pixel and compute functions keep the call: the vertex functions that have it ran in the same passes through both runs without a hang, which shows no fault of theirs, not that they have none.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), 16 GB M2 Pro, macOS 27.2.1, the app of cddbc623 (chain 9eecb8412308), 2026-11-16: three sessions at Lon Lon Ranch ended with GPU access revoked, 16 `gpuEvent` reports in 40 minutes and none in the seven days of reports before that chain. 157 of the game's 520 task and mesh libraries held the call. The first failed command buffer of five of the seven listed episodes was the shadow pass (pixel `ea1bf96b72e72476`, tasks `a5288d0a6c4971fd` and `f75708d176bb6591`, the latter and mesh `e6df2f14035e7be7` with the call). The player's own runs from one save, into Talon's house and through his cucco game to the cut to his dialogue (one run each; the game is random, so no script reaches it): the app's libraries hung there at frame 3,942; with the previous chain's (cd731f39944d) 157 task and mesh libraries laid over the app's, no hang in 6,300 frames; with the app chain's own 157 sources compiled with `dmsh_raw_find` `always_inline` again, no hang in 7,500 frames. Headless runs of the app's host on a copy of that save, 4 minutes standing and 7 walking at the ranch, did not show it.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_noinline.py` `test_mesh_keeps_the_search_inline` (task and mesh stages, sources already rewritten `noinline` included). No test shows the lockup: it needs that GPU and has no small case yet.

### A noinline raw lookup crashes the M1 and M2 mesh compiler

- **Symptom:** Ocarina of Time logs `task / mesh pipeline prewarm failed` with `XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED`; the compiler backend dies with SIGBUS in `llvm::MachineOperand::setReg` while compiling a mesh program. Other compiles interrupted by the same service death can fail too. The isolated pipeline can compile on an M4 Pro even when an offline compile for an older GPU fails.
- **Cause:** `43e816d8` introduced the separate `dmsh_raw_find` search and marked it `noinline` ([Raw global lookups built once for every call](#raw-global-lookups-built-once-for-every-call)). This call shape triggers an external mesh-compiler defect: changing only the search's attribute back to `always_inline` removes it without changing the lookup. The controlled inputs, exact targets and compiler identity are recorded in the linked game's validation. Offline evidence and a successful local-device compile do not establish the affected system compiler's runtime result.
- **Fix:** a general chain workaround: `raw_noinline` keeps the search inline in final Metal mesh code (and in task code, for [the M2's lockup](#a-noinline-call-in-a-task-or-mesh-function-locks-up-an-m2s-gpu)), and retains one search in other stages. Native task/mesh shaders reach the pass as compute shaders, so `dmsh_build.py` passes their `mesh_stage.kind`. No title, build or GPU-family branch is added; the library preserves the same lookup semantics on every target. Apple's compiler defect remains open.
- **Cost:** estimate: mesh pipelines with raw accesses can take longer to compile and have larger binaries because the search is duplicated again. Earlier measurements in the linked entry concern one different foliage pipeline and the optimization across all stages; they do not measure this workaround's aggregate startup or mesh-only GPU cost.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_raw_noinline.py` covers inline mesh code, already-rewritten inputs used by composed mesh libraries, unchanged non-mesh stages, idempotence and a real Metal compile. Offline verification exports every full manifest row through the production host, retaining differing descriptors even when rows share a shader triple; the linked game note records its coverage and results.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#mesh-compiler-workaround-validation), the later M1/M2 reports in [#2825](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2825) and [#2911](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2911).
- **Remaining:** affected-device retests are recorded in the game note. [Recovery of archives poisoned before the host fix](rendering.md#an-archive-period-written-after-the-compiler-service-died) remains separate; this workaround changes no host recovery or serialization.

### A shuffle of a constant crashes Apple's M2 mesh compiler

- **Symptom:** on an M2 Pro (`applegpu_g14s`), `[RED] task / mesh pipeline prewarm failed: task a7f22b4a795ff4d1_b mesh ac2f3feb11427426_b fragment eddc12907cefc9af_b: Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED` at every start, with `[RED] pipeline archive: the compiler service died under a compile`; `MTLCompilerService` dies with SIGBUS in `llvm::MachineOperand::setReg` under `AGCModule<AGX::Impl::MeshProgramKey>::compileLLVMObject`. The archive period the crash falls in is not written, so its pipelines are captured again at every start, other compiles under way then fail with the same error, and objects the pipeline draws stay white. This is the crash that remained on that Mac with the raw search inline ([A noinline raw lookup crashes the M1 and M2 mesh compiler](#a-noinline-raw-lookup-crashes-the-m1-and-m2-mesh-compiler)).
- **Cause:** the mesh shader counts what the lanes before its own put out with a prefix sum over the SIMD group: 32 `spvSubgroupShuffle(value, lane)` calls, one per lane, each added when the ballot has the lane. In six of the game's mesh shaders the translator's constant propagation left the value as the literal 2, so the code shuffles a constant 32 times. Apple's mesh compiler for this GPU dies on that in one of the six. Measured on the failing shader's source, changing nothing else: all 32 shuffles of the literal crash, the first 31, 30 or 24 crash, the first 28 or 16 do not, none does not, and 32 shuffles of a value that differs by lane do not; a value the optimizer folds to the constant crashes like the literal. The other five shaders compile with their 32. What the compiler does wrong is not known (Apple's defect, open).
- **Fix:** general, in the chain (`dmsh_rewrite.py` `shuffle_literal`, every stage): a subgroup shuffle of a literal is replaced by the literal. Every lane holds the same value, so the lane read gives it back; a lane that is not active gives an undefined value on NVIDIA's GPU too, and the shaders mask those with the ballot. No shader math changes, and the compiler has no shuffle of a constant left to trip on. A shuffle of a value stays.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#mesh-compiler-workaround-validation), on an M2 Pro with macOS 27.2.1 (26B131). Each of the 534 task / mesh rows of the profile's prewarm list and of an end-of-game player list was compiled alone by the host's prebuild tool: this pipeline alone failed, in 0.5 s; the failures the logs showed beside it (the same task with mesh `f85bb2e8d78b864c`, task `a5288d0a6c4971fd`, compute pipelines) were compiles the dying service interrupted.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_shuffle_literal.py`: a literal folded whatever the lane argument, a shuffle of a value and spirv-cross's helper definitions left alone, the pass idempotent, and the folded mesh function compiled by Metal.
- **Not taken: out-of-line subgroup helpers.** Upstream nxnative fixed the same crash another way (`32fe34ef`: SPIRV-Cross's mesh helpers with `gl_Subgroup*` parameters made `noinline`), which met a defect of Apple's compiler: a builtin such as `gl_SubgroupInvocationID` passed by reference (`thread uint&`) on from one out-of-line helper to another reads as one value for the whole SIMD group, so every lane of this game's exclusive scan got the same prefix sum, the mesh groups wrote outside their output ranges, and the GPU hung after a save loaded (at `-O1` to `-O3`, minimum 26.6 and 27.0, in compute and mesh kernels; not at `-O0`, inlined or by value). Upstream's `6dc27632` passes those parameters by value. Here the helpers stay inline, so neither applies; a change that moves subgroup helpers out of line must pass their builtins by value.

### 128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time

- **Symptom:** a pass of mesh, vertex or compute shaders whose build notes say `Global memory operation "Load" traced to no storage buffer` takes milliseconds of its stage's time for little drawing, at any resolution. Metal System Trace's Shader Timeline shows no samples for task and mesh shaders, so such a pass looks idle there; the encoder's vertex-stage time and an overlay A/B tell. Ocarina of Time's foliage mesh pass took 4.6 ms of vertex-stage time a frame at 683x384, 0.2 ms of fragment.
- **Cause:** the emitters split an `LDG.64` / `LDG.128` into 32-bit loads (`InstEmitAmpereMem.ALdg`, Maxwell's `InstEmitMemory.EmitLdg`), each with its own 64-bit address. Traced to no storage buffer, each word then went through the pool table on its own ([Raw global memory lookups that start from their last entry](#raw-global-memory-lookups-that-start-from-their-last-entry)) and was read through a pointer of its own, built from two 32-bit halves. Metal's compiler cannot see that four such pointers are consecutive, so one 128-bit read became four word loads, each waiting on its own address. Ocarina's foliage mesh shader `eed1add7c89f5d34` read 464 of its 506 raw words as pieces of 116 such loads. Measured on an M4 Pro: the lookups' table reads cost nothing (kept in registers, no change), and the reads were the time (replaced by a value made from the address, the frame's GPU time fell 16.8 → 13.0 ms).
- **Fix:** general, in the translator. The emitters mark the words of one access (`Operation.GlobalVectorCount`, `GlobalVectorWord`). For such an access whose address goes through the pool table, `GlobalToStorage` (`ReplaceGlobalMemoryWithStorage`, `GenerateRawVectorLoad`) calls a vector helper once (`…RawV2` / `…RawV4`, `TryGenerateRawStorageOp`): the first word's lookup, then one 64- or 128-bit load through a physical pointer (`StorageKind.GlobalMemoryVector`, the SPIR-V backend's `GenerateRawVectorLoad`, aligned as a word like the scalar loads). Word 0 is returned and the others go to the shader's `gmem_vec` local memory, which the loads of those words read right after the call (they become copies, so the pass does not take them for global accesses again). The first word's entry holds the whole access: an `LDG.64` / `LDG.128` is aligned to its size on the GPU, and every pool table entry starts and ends on a pool's 4 KiB boundary (`nvn_args.inc gmem_map`). Ocarina's `eed1add7c89f5d34` makes 122 vector calls and 22 word calls instead of 506 word calls, and its MSL shrinks from 7,949 to 5,656 lines.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md): the Kokiri Forest route (`bench.tsv` `kokiri-deck`, its partial shader set rebuilt, with [occlusion conditions read once landed](rendering.md#occlusion-conditions-read-once-landed)) on a 20-core M4 Pro went from GPU p50 16.3 / p95 18.6 ms at 34.7 fps (a 30 fps frame step) to 12.6 / 13.6 ms at 51.8 fps, frame p50 16.69 ms (60 fps most of the time), two runs each, picture unchanged.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py` `RawVector` (with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`): an `LDG.E.128` through an untraced address translates to one call of a helper with one `uint4` load and no word call, and on this Mac's GPU each of its four words is the pool's.

### A 64- or 128-bit raw global load in no pool reads undefined upper words

- **Symptom:** a 64- or 128-bit load through an address in no pool (a null or stale pointer, a gap between pools) reads 0 in its first word and whatever the invocation's `gmem_vec` held in the others: the words of the previous vector load, or undefined data on the first. Before the change in [128-bit raw global loads read one word at a time](#128-bit-raw-global-loads-read-one-word-at-a-time), every word of such a load read 0. A shader that tests a loaded record's first word for 0 is unaffected; one that uses the other words (a bounding box, a count, a second pointer) gets garbage.
- **Cause:** the vector helper (`…RawV2` / `…RawV4`, `GlobalToStorage.TryGenerateRawStorageOp`) returned 0 on a miss but wrote nothing to `gmem_vec`, which spirv-cross declares in the entry point without a value and the caller reads right after the call.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator: before its miss return, the vector helper stores 0 to each of the other words, as the word loads it replaced read on a miss.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py` `RawVector.test_a_miss_zeroes_the_other_words` and `test_a_64_bit_miss_zeroes_its_other_word` (the 128- and 64-bit helpers' miss paths store 0 to `gmem_vec[0..2]` / `gmem_vec[0]`) and `test_reads_on_gpu`, whose kernel now sets the three words to `0xdead` before each call: a read in no pool must leave all four 0. All three fail with the previous translator (on the GPU, 256 of its 1,024 reads: every miss).
- **Found on:** found by reading the translated vector helper; no game's wrong picture is traced to it yet.

### A word of one 128-bit load read from another's vector

- **Symptom:** wrong data, no error: of two `LDG.64` / `LDG.128` in one block, a word of the second reads a word of the first load's memory, and the second load's own memory is never read.
- **Cause:** `GenerateRawVectorLoad`'s follower scan took any later load with the same word count and a word above 0 as a word of its call. When the first load's later words are unused (so the scan does not end at its last word) and the second load's word 0 is unused too (so it has no call of its own), a used later word of the second load became a read of the first call's `gmem_vec`.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator: the emitters give each access's words a group of their own (`Operation.GlobalVectorGroup`, counted by `EmitterContext.GlobalVectorGroups`), and the scan ends at a word of another group. That word then takes a lookup of its own.
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_raw_last.py` `RawVectorPair` (two adjacent unpredicated `LDG.128`, the first using word 0, the second word 2): one vector call, no `gmem_vec` read, one scalar lookup for the second load. It fails with the previous translator, which read `gmem_vec[1]`.
- **Found on:** reported by lunks on PR #1813 from reading the code; none of Ocarina of Time's 2,055 shaders that call a vector helper has the pattern.

### Bindless handles through a loop phi

- **Symptom:** `no resolved slot number`, the shader does not load; the translation has a 32-entry texture-pool array (`fp_t2d_cb0_40000000`, handle type Direct).
- **Cause:** the handle is `c2[0x1A0] | 0x10500000` (separate texture slot 7, constant sampler 0x105), kept in a loop variable. Ryujinx's bindless elimination compared only the phi's direct inputs, gave up, and turned the access into an index into a 32-entry texture-pool array, which the replay cannot bind.
- **Fix:** the vendored translator now follows the phis that feed a phi, and treats two `constant buffer | constant` handles as the same value ([third_party/ryujinx/README.md](../../dm/third_party/ryujinx/README.md)). Those accesses become ordinary type-3 handles.
- **Found on:** Monster Hunter Rise (218 pixel shaders).

### Bindless reads whose handle phi also carries other values

- **Symptom:** none in the log: texture reads come back 0. Particle meshes collapse to a point (their scale reads 0), so effects are missing or show as dark patches. The translator's own message (`Failed to find handle source for bindless access of type "textureBuffer"`) used to be dropped, because `dmsh_xlate`'s accessor had no `Log`; its messages now go into the shader's notes, marked `translator: `, and the build summary counts them apart from unanswered queries.
- **Cause:** the handle reaches the read through a phi. Some inputs are c2 handle words, the others are not handles: undefined, or a value the compiler kept in the same register on a path where the predicated read does not run (float arithmetic, a texture result, the absolute value of a uniform word). Some phis merge two or more different c2 handles too (a float and a half-precision buffer). `TryConvertBindless` cannot trace one handle, and `GenerateBindlessAccess` refuses a phi with such an input, so bindless elimination deleted the read and wrote 0 to its results.
- **Fix:** only for a phi `GenerateBindlessAccess` refuses (`TryTableHandle` in the vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`, marked `nxnative:`), the read takes the c2 handles among the phi's inputs (a word, or a texture word ORed with a sampler word or a constant sampler). With one handle, it takes the phi's place. With several, one read is made per handle and the result is the read whose handle equals the phi's value at run time: the host fills c2 with the bound handles, or with slot indices for `tex_dyn_slots`. A candidate that other reads of the shader use as a texture of another shape is left out, so those reads are converted in a second sweep once every other read has registered its texture (`ResourceManager.GetTextureTypes`). An image store or atomic with several candidates is left alone, since one per candidate would write each of them. A phi made only of handle words keeps `GenerateBindlessAccess` (the dynamic arrays of [Texture slots picked at run time](#texture-slots-picked-at-run-time)), so no other shader changes: in FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE, the SPIR-V of the 69,449 other shaders is byte for byte what it was. A chain change.
- **Found on:** FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE: 58,122 reads removed (57,861 of them texture-buffer reads, mostly in Unreal's Niagara particle vertex factories), of which 58,096 in 3,197 shaders are now resolved: 21,380 with one handle, the rest choosing among 2 to 5 at run time. 26 reads in 14 shaders, whose handle comes from no c2 word, are still removed. Reported in GitHub issue #168.

### Texture slots picked at run time

- **Symptom:** the shaders' bindless arrays have no slot and used not to load; the translator falls back to a 32-entry array such as `array<texture_buffer<float>, 32> vp_tb_cb0_40000000`.
- **Cause:** the shader reads one of several c2 texture-table words, chosen by branches. With separate textures (Octopath Traveler 0) it ORs the word with a sampler-table word; with combined ones (Paper Mario) the handle is read from the combined texture table (c2 `0x20 + 8 × slot`, constant offsets only). The translator cannot trace that to one slot and falls back to Ryujinx's bindless arrays, `array<texture_buffer<float>, 32> vp_tb_cb0_40000000` and `array<sampler, 32> vp_s_cb0_40000000`, indexed by the handle's texture ID (low 20 bits) and sampler ID (high 12), capped at 31; the build leaves them without a slot.
- **Fix:** for buffer textures (and images). A sampled texture of another kind indexes the whole texture pool instead, whatever the handle's source ([Texture handles from game memory index the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools)), which took over Paper Mario's cube and 2D arrays. With the profile key `tex_dyn_slots` the reflection reads them as `NVN_RB_TEXD` / `NVN_RB_SAMPD` arrays (`nvn_refl.m`), the replay writes slot indices into that shader's c2 tables (texture slot r → r, sampler slot r → r << 20; in the combined table `r | r << 20` in slot r) and binds slot k at element k, so the shader's own arithmetic lands on the binding: the handle names element r, which holds the texture bound in slot r. The array's other elements hold whatever the other slots do, mostly 2D textures where the array is of cubes; an array element bound to a 2D texture gets the zero cube without a type mismatch report (`tex_fit_elem`), since the shader does not pick it there. The host logs each such shader's first encode with the slots that held textures. Host only: the chain version does not change.
- **Check:** check first that the handles come only from c2's tables: in Octopath Traveler 0 every integer read of c2 in those shaders is a table slot (texture slots 0-10, samplers 0-4).
- **Found on:** Octopath Traveler 0: 61 Unreal vertex shaders, particles reading their data from buffer textures (separate textures and samplers). FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE: 2,077 particle vertex shaders. Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door: 2,386 pixel shaders (5,476 cube samples, 487 2D), slots 0, 7, 8, 13 and 28 (combined table), until the pools took them over.

### A texture chosen at run time between two uniform-buffer handles

- **Symptom:** Ryujinx's fallback arrays (`fp_t2d_cb0_40000000`) in pixel shaders whose handle comes from the game's own uniform buffers; the shaders do not load and their draws are skipped.
- **Cause:** the handle is one of two words of the game's own uniform buffers, chosen by a flag, packed as texture ID | sampler ID << 20. `tex_dyn_slots` covers only handles from the driver's c2 tables ([Texture slots picked at run time](#texture-slots-picked-at-run-time)).
- **Fix:** the pools: whichever word the flag picks, its texture ID indexes the texture pool ([Texture handles from game memory index the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools)). Not checked on Big Brain Academy, whose files are not on the Mac the change was made on.
- **Found on:** Big Brain Academy: Brain vs. Brain: ten pixel shaders (six in `03GamePerception.bnbshpk`, two PBR, two in `main`); in `e10f24835bbaa5f6` the handle is c3 +0x130 or c12 +0x1d0, chosen by a flag in c12. None of the activities checked uses them; a long run of activities met one once.

### Texture handles from game memory index the pools

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: a texture handle the translator could not trace to one slot (profile key tex_dyn_slots off)`, then `failed to load`, and the draws are skipped: in Mario Party Superstars every character and NPC in a lit scene is a flat grey silhouette (the depth pre-pass draws, the colour pass does not), and the opening scene's pipe is grey.
- **Cause:** a lit pixel shader loops over the lights, reading each light's shadow map, cube map and sampler handles from the game's storage buffer (`fp_s1`) at a computed offset. Ryujinx cannot trace such a handle to a slot and falls back to arrays indexed by the handle's IDs (`array<texturecube<float>, n> fp_tcube_cb0_40000000`, `array<sampler, n> fp_s_cb0_40000000`, texture ID in bits 0–19, sampler ID from bit 20), clamped to `n − 1`. The translator's accessor gave `n` = 32 and the replay had no pool to bind there: only `tex_dyn_slots` arrays, whose handles must come from c2.
- **Fix:** a chain change. The accessor sizes these arrays to the whole ID ranges (`Program.cs` `QueryTextureArrayLengthFromPool` 2²⁰, `QuerySamplerArrayLengthFromPool` 2¹²) for sampled textures other than buffer textures; buffer textures and images keep 32-entry slot arrays (vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`, `NvnSlotArrayLength`). `dmsh_reflect.py` `pool_arrays` turns each such array into a pointer to `struct spvPool_<name> { uint n; array<element, 1> e; }`, read as `(&e[0])[min(index, n)]`, and renumbers the argument IDs (a pointer takes one).
