The struct declares one element because Metal's argument encoder walks every element of a declared array (`AGX::IndirectArgumentLayoutGen2::parseArray`): with 2²⁰ textures each pool shader's encoder took 57 ms and 66 MB, and the prewarm of 733 pipelines left the Mac host at 12.5 GB resident (9.7 GB of large mallocs); the elements are 8-byte resource IDs, so the pointer reaches the others (checked on the GPU with a test kernel); the reflection names them `tex_pool` (with the element type) and `samp_pool`. The host keeps one buffer per element type and pool base (`nvn_pooltab.inc` `pool_gpu`): the bound n, then from `DMSH_POOL_HEAD` the resource ID of each texture (sampler) ID of the pool bound at the draw, the dummy where an ID holds nothing or a texture of another type and at n. It is rebuilt when a registration or a texture's storage changes (`g_reg_gen`, `g_ser_gen`), the old buffer released after its submissions, and an argument block holding a pool address is not reused past a rebuild. Handles read from game memory are the bound pool's IDs, so the pool takes that pool's base (as `ubo_tex_id`), and a pool shader's c2 holds the relative bound handles as the game wrote them; ordinary slot consumers resolve them through [the execution pools](#descriptor-handles-use-the-execution-pools). Every texture is already in the residency set. A walk over every ID also reaches IDs no draw binds, which a game leaves registered after it is done with them: the first walk once crashed in `nvn_sparse_protect` making such a texture (a garbage `pool->vp`; the ID was not caught). So a texture no draw has made is made only while its storage exists (`tex_storage_live`: the memory pool object still holds its magic, and an ordinary pool's storage is in a pool the host maps), and an ID whose memory pool the game finalized is skipped until it is registered again (`treg_t.gone`, set in `treg_drop_pool_views`). Every other route is covered too: [A texture read after its memory pool object was freed](nvn-objects.md#a-texture-read-after-its-memory-pool-object-was-freed).
- **Found on:** Mario Party Superstars: 6,370 of its 82,790 shaders, every character and NPC material (`_chara/pc/*.bnbshpk`, `_chara/npc/*.bnbshpk`) and some board variants (`hsmn_bg.bnbshpk`); a 2,304-entry texture pool (1,286–1,314 2D textures, 65 cubes) and a 512-entry sampler pool, about 0.1 ms per rebuild once the textures exist.

### Handles a shader computes index the pools

- **Symptom:** `[RED] <key>: the translator deleted N bindless accesses (<type> N): their results read 0` for thousands of shaders, and `lost without failing: …` in the build; textures read as black (PRAGMATA: 65,241 reads in 8,630 shaders, its intro a saturated cyan).
- **Cause:** the handle is no word of any buffer but a value the shader computes: RE Engine's materials read a heap index from game memory (`cx[UR][off]`) or from a uniform buffer at a computed offset, clamp it to the heap (0x100–0x7fff), and OR it with a sampler ID (`| 0x10800000`, or with a separate sampler word of c2). `GenerateBindlessAccess` read such a handle through [the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools) only when it was loaded straight from a constant buffer, an input or a storage buffer (Ryujinx's `IsBindlessAccessAllowed`, kept there to spare its pool walks), so every other read was deleted.
- **Fix:** a chain change. A sampled texture other than a buffer texture indexes the pools whatever made its handle (vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`): its texture ID indexes the texture pool and its sampler ID the sampler pool, as on the GPU. Images and buffer textures keep the old rule, since their slot arrays are bound only for handles from c2's tables.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: no access is deleted after the change. Any game with deleted sampled reads in its build log ([Losses a shader build reports](#losses-a-shader-build-reports)) changes with it.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py GuardedWrites.test_a_computed_handle_reads_the_pools`.

### A handle loaded from memory on one path only

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding … has no resolved slot number` at load, then `the dispatched program has no compute shader` each time the game dispatches it; the build log has `Bindless handle taken from the one constant buffer handle among its phi inputs` for the shader.
- **Cause:** the handle is a texture ID ORed with a constant sampler, and the ID's register holds a word loaded from memory on the path that reads the texture and something else on the other (a pushed buffer address's high word, `c[24] + 0x114`). `TryTableHandle` walks the OR's phi inputs for words of a uniform buffer, did not count the loaded word (a global load `GlobalToStorage` served is a copy of its helper's result, not a `Load`), and took the stray word alone as the handle: a binding the host has no slot for.
- **Fix:** a chain change (vendored `BindlessElimination.cs`). For a read that [indexes the pools](#handles-a-shader-computes-index-the-pools), a handle with a loaded word among its phi inputs (`IsLoaded`: a load, a call, or a copy that keeps a storage kind), alone or as a source of the OR, is left to `GenerateBindlessAccess`: the read takes the texture its ID names, whatever path made it. Images and buffer textures keep their candidates.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: compute shader `02e1292114121308`, which reads a material's texture by an ID from a material record.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_nvn2_buffers.py -k test_a_texture_id_loaded_on_one_path_indexes_the_pools`.

### One handle read as a texture and as an array

- **Symptom:** `[metal failed]` in the build, the compiler's message `vector component access exceeds type 'float2'` on a line that samples a `texture2d_array` with a two-component coordinate and `uint(rint(coord.z))` as its layer; the shader and its alpha test entry points do not build.
- **Cause:** a handle has one binding, declared with one type, but Ryujinx gave the declaration the type of the last instruction that read it and the descriptor the first accurate one, while each instruction built its coordinates for its own type. A 2D read (`TEX` 2D at level 0) of a binding another instruction read as `ARRAY_2D` (`TXD`) gave no layer, which is invalid SPIR-V that SPIRV-Cross wrote out as it was.
- **Fix:** a chain change (`ResourceManager.UnifyArrayTypes`, run once every function is translated). A 1D or 2D binding that some instructions read as an array is an array for all of them, in the declaration and in the descriptor the host binds by; an instruction that gives no layer reads layer 0, which is what the GPU reads through a non-array instruction. Level-of-detail queries take no layer either way. Cube and 3D bindings and arrays of handles are left as they were.
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: 24 pixel shaders (`21ba8fea5a5eb03c` is one), each reading `cb65542` handle `0x3041000E` both ways.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_nvn2_buffers.py -k test_a_handle_read_as_2d_and_as_a_2d_array_is_an_array`.

### An array of handles in a uniform buffer

- **Symptom:** the host stops at the shader's first load: `unexpected argument buffer layout: encodedLength=640 maxAid=48`.
- **Cause:** a loop reads one handle per light from the game's uniform buffer at a computed offset; Ryujinx's texture array from a buffer indexes it by the handle's byte offset ÷ 8 (an NVN handle is 64-bit, the shader computes `(word >> 2) << 1 | (word & 3) >> 1`). The build reads the name as one bindless texture (`tex_ubo`, offset 0), so the argument buffer was 32 entries longer than the reflection said.
- **Fix:** the host's reflection reader takes the array length from the MSL type, and the replay fills element k from the handle at byte 8k of that uniform slot (`NVN_RB_TEXU` / `SAMPU` with `n`); elements whose bytes are not a registered texture or sampler ID read the dummy, silently. No chain change: the build already wrote the array type. Sampled textures have since moved to [a table the replay builds](#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array); this form remains for images and buffer textures.
- **Found on:** Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door: the reflection shaders (`reflection.bfsha`: `array<texture2d<float>, 32> fp_t2d_cb14_0` and its samplers), c14 (uniform slot 11). The first run showed elements 0–3, 6 and 8–12 holding textures, among them the shader's fixed handles at +8, +16 and +24 and the lights' from +64.

### A table of handles longer than the array

- **Symptom:** characters and other moving objects have almost no ambient light: lit only from where the sun reaches, black underneath, while the scenery around them is right. Nothing is red. On Kirby Air Riders a dark red Kirby on a bright course, where the game's own tutorial movie shows him bright pink.
- **Cause:** an [array of handles in a uniform buffer](#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer) was declared with 32 elements and its index clamped to the last, but the game's table is longer. Kirby Air Riders' compute shader `f59db619b5c0be33` traces each object's eight light probe values from atlases named in c[4], and its index was 390 for the player's objects: clamped to element 31, which held nothing, so the eight values were black and the vertex shaders that read them lit the character with nothing. Objects whose probes happened to sit under element 32 were lit. The table has 197 handles up to element 392, in a buffer of 4,096, and nothing in the shader tells how long it is.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `Program.cs` `QueryTextureArrayLengthFromBuffer` gives a game's uniform buffer 8,192 elements (64 KB of 8-byte handles), `dmsh_reflect.py` `pool_arrays` turns such an array and its samplers into pointers with a run-time bound, and the replay builds the table from the buffer (`nvn_args.inc` `htab_gpu`), as long as the last handle, looking again in each pass and keeping the buffer when the elements are the same. Images and buffer textures stay arrays of 32, bound element by element. Reading these handles through [the pools](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools) instead gave the same picture and cost too much: the walk over 65,824 IDs made all 34,000 of the game's registered textures, 16 GB resident against 9.7, and 9 to 25 fps against 60.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_pool_arrays.py` checks the rewrite and the reflection.
- **Found on:** Kirby Air Riders: most of its lit materials and light probes.

### Reads of a handle table marked through a null address

- **Symptom:** a pass that samples through [a table of a uniform buffer's handles](#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array) takes hundreds of milliseconds on the GPU; nothing is red and the picture is right. In Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave on an M1 Pro, the lighting pass at Alcineus Bridge took 196.7 ms and the scene ran at 4 fps.
- **Cause:** `dmsh_reflect.py` `pool_arrays` gave the table's texture reads the same mark as [the pool's](#texture-handles-from-game-memory-index-the-pools) (`dm_pool_mark`, an atomic load, and an OR when the bit is clear, at the bitmap address in the buffer's header). `htab_gpu` never set that address, so every read went to address 0. The mark had no use there anyway: a table's index is its element, not a texture ID, and the replay has made every texture the table names before the draw.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `pool_arrays` marks only reads of the texture pool (`_cb0_40000000`); a table is read plainly, and a fragment shader with no other mark keeps its depth test where Metal puts it. The header keeps its layout, with the address 0 in a table. PR #1738 (HammerChain), which found the null address, pointed it at a premarked bitmap in the host instead; with that the bridge ran at 56.1 fps and the lighting pass took 2.35 ms (M1 Pro, 1080p output from 960x540). The chain change takes the mark away as well, which leaves every table read a plain load.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_pool_arrays.py` (`test_table_of_uniform_handles`).
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave at Alcineus Bridge.

### A handle table kept past a write to its handles

- **Symptom:** a draw or dispatch that reads [a table of a uniform buffer's handles](#a-table-of-handles-longer-than-the-array) samples the texture or sampler of an earlier draw: the one before an inline uniform update (`nvnCommandBufferUpdateUniformBuffer`) in the same pass, or, in a submission with compute work only, the one the previous submission read. Nothing is red.
- **Cause:** `nvn_args.inc` `htab_gpu` kept a table without reading its handles again while the pass serial, the registrations and the size were the same. Neither covers a write queued in the submission over the handles (an inline update an open pass defers, a copy or a fill), and a compute-only submission keeps the last render pass's serial, so the guest memory read in the submission before was taken for this one's.
- **Fix:** a table is kept only in the same pass and submission and when no write queued since its last look lands on its bytes (`htab_written`: `pend_since` scans the writes queued since then, or any when the host lost count). Otherwise its handles are read again as the GPU will see them (`ubo_read` with the queued inline updates); changed handles go into a new buffer, as commands already encoded hold the old one, and the same handles keep it. A write elsewhere keeps the table without reading it, as games update other uniform blocks before nearly every draw; the table log line counts the reads. Handles written by a buffer copy, a fill or a shader are still read as the CPU sees them (see [Handles in uniform buffers written earlier in the same submission](#handles-in-uniform-buffers-written-earlier-in-the-same-submission)).
- **Check:** `dm/gfx/tests/t_htab_update.m` encodes real draws and dispatches through `encode_args` with red and green textures and clamp and repeat samplers: full and partial inline updates between two draws in an open pass (the first draw's pixels unchanged), a write beside the table (kept without a read), two dispatches around an update, and a new compute-only submission. Main before the fix failed 8 of its checks.
- **Found on:** source review in PR #2228 (akratch); no game's symptom is attributed to it yet. It reaches the games that read handle tables: Kirby Air Riders, Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave and Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door.

### An array of uniform-buffer handles with its sampler word in c2

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t2ds_cb196614_10570000 has no resolved slot number`, then `failed to load`; those draws are skipped, and the scene is dark or black where it is lit by them.
- **Cause:** handle type 1 on [an array of handles in a uniform buffer](#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer): the texture words in the game's c[6], one separate sampler word in the driver table (slots 0x30006: the high half is bank + 1, so bank 2; word 0x15c, c2 byte 0x570). `decode_ubo_tex` took only a uniform buffer for that word.
- **Fix:** `decode_ubo_tex` answers ('c2', byte), the reflection gives the sampler `sreg` `DMSH_SREG_C2`, and the replay takes every element's sampler from that c2 word (`sampu_id`), whose IDs are moved already, as those of `NVN_RB_SAMPA`.
- **Found on:** Kirby Air Riders: about 5,700 pixel and compute shaders, its shadows and lights.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_reflect.py` (`UniformArraysWithDriverSampler`).

### A separate sampler ID in a uniform buffer (handle type 2)

- **Symptom:** `reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t_cb720898_2000005ASmplr has no resolved slot number`, then `failed to load`; those draws are skipped. In The Witcher 3 245 of 602 shaders failed this way, its domain shaders among them, and the screen stayed black.
- **Cause:** handle type 2, Ryujinx's `SeparateSamplerId`: the texture word is in the driver table c2 (the low half of the slots, 2), and the second word is a sampler ID itself, not a handle with the ID in its high 12 bits, in one of the game's uniform buffers (the high half less one: slots 0xb0002, bank 10; `(handle >> 14) & 0x3fff` its word). `decode_ubo_tex` decodes a uniform buffer only for the texture half, so the sampler had no slot. `decode_tex` also tried both words for the texture, so a sampler word at a byte offset that is a combined texture slot's in c2 (0x20 to 0x11f) would be named as the texture.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `dmsh_reflect.py` `decode_ubo_samp_id` gives a single sampler of this form `samp_ubo` with the uniform slot and the word's byte offset and `raw_id`, and `decode_tex` takes only the first word as the texture for type 2, as for type 3. The replay reads the word and shifts it into the handle's sampler field before the pool lookup (`nvn_refl.h` `raw_id`, `nvn_args.inc` `sampu_id`). Arrays of such samplers are not covered; none is known.
- **Found on:** The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt — Complete Edition: `fp_t_cb720898_2000005ASmplr` in 181 of its shaders, `fp_t_cb720898_2000405CSmplr` in 34.
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_reflect.py` (`SeparateSamplerIds`) and `t_refl` (the `raw_id` key).

### A table of handles past the array's length

- **Symptom:** characters shaded black; a shader indexes a table of cube maps, and every element it reads clamps to the array's last one, which holds no handle.
- **Cause:** the shader reads `c[14][8 * i + 0x340]`. `BindlessToArray` starts an array of handles at the buffer's start, so with a base past the array's length (32) each index ran off its end.
- **Fix:** `TryArrayBase` finds an index plus a constant base; when the base is past the length, the array starts at the base and the index is the handle's place in the table. `nvn_mtl.m` fills element k from the array's offset + 8k (`NVN_RB_TEXU`, `NVN_RB_SAMPU`). A base inside the array keeps the array at 0, as before.
- **Found on:** Fire Emblem Fortune's Weave: `e496014da6497fad` and the character shaders.

### An array of handles from the driver table c2

- **Symptom:** `shaders with a binding whose slot was not decoded 588`; shadow cascades read through `array<texture2d_array<float>, 32> fp_t2das_cb2_0`.
- **Cause:** Ryujinx's texture array from a buffer, as Paper Mario's uniform-buffer arrays ([An array of handles in a uniform buffer](#an-array-of-handles-in-a-uniform-buffer)), but over the driver table c2, indexed by (0x80 + a cascade offset) ÷ 8.
- **Fix:** `dmsh_reflect.py` `c2_array` names it `tex_c2a` / `samp_c2a` with the c2 byte offset of element 0; the replay fills element k from the slot whose c2 word is at that offset + 8k (combined, else separate), and the dummy elsewhere (`NVN_RB_TEXA` / `NVN_RB_SAMPA`, `c2_handle`).
- **Found on:** Luigi's Mansion 3 (586 pixel shaders, the shadow cascades).

### Arrays of c2 handles cover the whole table, with a separate sampler word

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: B binding fp_t2d_cb196610_10570000 has no resolved slot number`, then, with that resolved, `… reads driver-bank locations at run time that the replay layer cannot fill: c2 dynamic index`; the shader does not load and its draws are skipped.
- **Cause:** two gaps in [An array of handles from the driver table c2](#an-array-of-handles-from-the-driver-table-c2). The translator sized every texture array from a buffer at 32 elements (`Program.cs` `QueryTextureArrayLengthFromBuffer`) and clamps the index to the last one, but over c2 the index is any handle word's byte offset ÷ 8: EA SPORTS FC 27's pixel shader `dc446d7f52c14ea6` loops over separate texture slots i = 1, 2, …, reads each handle word at c2 byte 0x168 + 8i and samples element 45 + i, so every element it picked was clamped to 31 (a combined slot). And the handle is type 1: `BindlessToArray` ORs one fixed separate sampler word (c2 byte 0x570, separate sampler slot 1) into every element's handle, which `c2_array` did not decode (it took only `_cb2_` with type 0), so the texture array had no slot and its sampler array took that one slot.
- **Fix:** a chain change. `Program.cs` gives an array over c2 one element per handle word up to the end of the separate sampler table (`C2HandleWords`, 301; other buffers keep 32), `c2_array` decodes type 1 with the sampler word in c2 too (`soff` in the reflection), and the replay ORs that word into each element's handle for the samplers (`NVN_RB_SAMPA`; `c2_handle` now also answers the separate sampler table). `nvn_rbind.n` is 16 bits and `NVN_REFL_MAXARG` 768 (such a pair takes 602 argument entries). The same loop reads each handle word itself at a computed c2 index (`fp_c2._m0[int(_237)]`, to test the light's handle), which `unfillable` refused for any bank; the replay fills c2's handle tables whole (`DRV2` in `encode_args`), so a computed index into c2 is accepted, and one into c0, filled only in its tables, still refused. Luigi's Mansion 3's cascade arrays become 301 long as well; the host fills the elements past the handles with the dummy.
- **Found on:** EA SPORTS FC 27 (its lights in a match).
- **Check:** `test_dmsh_reflect.py` (`DriverTableArrays`, `DriverReads`) and `test_dmsh_abi.py` (the translator's table length agrees with the header).

### A texture slot that shares its c2 word with an image slot

- **Symptom:** characters and props glowing white, about 10⁴ too bright, in DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined's opening cutscene only, while walls and gameplay look right.
- **Cause:** a size query (`GetDimensions` on a `RWTexture3D`) is a TXQ on the image's handle, c2 `0x120` (image slot 0): the build numbers it combined texture slot 32, the slot that shares that word, and with no type in the facts table the translator read it as 2D. The host bound the empty texture slot 32, and the 1×1 dummy's size passed only thread (0, 0, 0).
- **Fix:** two general fixes: a texture binding of slot 32–39 reads the image bound in slot 0–7 when there is one, as c2 holds it (`nvn_mtl.m` `tex_slot_h`; Hades II's texture slot 34 without an image still reads the texture), and a query on an image handle the facts do not type is translated again with the type of that image from the first translation (`dmsh_xlate` `ImageTypesOf`).
- **Found on:** DRAGON QUEST VII Reimagined: the per-pixel volumetric lightmap read brick (0, 0, 0) of the brick atlas for almost every cell. Unreal merges each streamed level's lightmap into one atlas on the GPU: brick copies (`0a7ff4171bd1a82a`), then a patch of the shared 16³ `RGBA8_UINT` indirection texture (`7b958a7b21e72801`) whose bounds come from `GetDimensions` on the very `RWTexture3D` it writes. Only cell (0, 0, 0) was written, every other cell kept size 0, and the shader fell back to the atlas origin: the first daytime level's bricks, baked about 2¹⁵ brighter (the same bricks in gameplay hold 0.5–1.4). Monster Hunter Rise has four such size queries (`cp_t_tcb_48` / `_4A`).
- **Check:** how it was found: the raw scene colour (`texraw`) put the characters at 1,000–2,000 and the walls at 0.05; a debug build of one character shader that wrote its terms in alternating 8-pixel columns isolated the volumetric lightmap ambient term; dumps of the atlas (`texlist` / `texraw`), of the uploaded level bricks (the 3D upload dump) and of the indirection texture, with the cell computed from the primitive's origin and View `VolumetricLightmapWorldToUVScale / Add` (View 220 / 221), gave the rest.

### A size query on a 3D texture gets a 2D slot

- **Symptom:** `[RED] texture type mismatch: shader … declares slot 128 as Metal type 2, texture ID … is type 7 (NVN target 2, …); bound as is`, for a shader whose MSL has a `texture3d` and a `texture2d` on the same register and only calls `get_width` / `get_height` on the `texture2d`.
- **Cause:** a size query (TXQ) carries no texture type, and a table built without the game's PC shaders does not answer one, so `QuerySamplerType` defaults to 2D. A handle ORs a texture word and a sampler word, and the query's has another sampler word than the sample's, so Ryujinx's merge by handle (`MergeTextureMeta`) keeps them apart. The shader then has a second binding of the slot, typed 2D, beside the sample's own `texture3d`, and the host has no 2D view of a 3D texture (`tex_fit`). In Princess Peach: Showtime!, 1,490 slots in 1,463 of 56,594 shaders had that pair. The same pair with a cube (27 slots, `get_num_mip_levels`) or a 2D array raises no red line, since those have a 2D view.
- **Fix:** general, in the translator: when a query's guess meets another type on its texture slot, `dmsh_xlate` translates again with that type (`TextureTypesOf`), as `ImageTypesOf` does for images. Types go by slot, as the answer table does; the table comes first, a query with nothing else on its slot stays 2D, and a slot two instructions give different shapes keeps the guess. The values these shaders read do not change: every query of the 1,490 3D slots reads only the width, or the mip count on the 27 cube slots, and an Apple M6 returned the right width through the old binding for all six sizes tried. What changes is that Metal now defines the binding.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_txq_type.py` (synthetic shaders; the fix's tests fail on the translator before it). Princess Peach: Showtime! has no such pair left, and its route into the ninja play went from 15 red lines to none, with the same picture at the same spot. Of the 69 local games' shader folders scanned, only two others hold the pattern: Ghost Trick (2 shaders) and EA SPORTS FC 27 (1), each with a 2D array.
- **Found on:** Princess Peach: Showtime!: up to 19 red lines per run, with no visible fault over the first play.

### More than 64 bindings in one shader

- **Symptom:** `[RED] shader … reflection conversion failed: 66 bindings, over the limit 64`, then `failed to load`, and the draws with it are dropped (a black map).
- **Cause:** the reflection is converted into fixed arrays of `NVN_REFL_MAXB` bindings (`nvn_refl.h`), which was 64. NVN gives a stage 32 textures and 32 samplers beside its uniform and storage buffers and images, so a shader can have more.
- **Fix:** `NVN_REFL_MAXB` is 128; the argument buffer entries keep their own limit (`NVN_REFL_MAXARG`, 768, see [Arrays of c2 handles cover the whole table](#arrays-of-c2-handles-cover-the-whole-table-with-a-separate-sampler-word)). It was raised from 96 when every sampled sampler got a twin binding ([Border colours Metal has no sampler state for](rendering.md#border-colours-metal-has-no-sampler-state-for)): that shader then has 97 bindings.
- **Found on:** OCTOPATH TRAVELER II: the 3D world map of the traveler selection (`UiMM_WorldMap3D`, pixel shader `f7c8373b7323687d`: 30 textures, 30 samplers, 3 uniform buffers and the c1 / support / driver segments).

### Argument buffer members with attributes after their id

- **Symptom:** the host stops at a shader's first load: `FATAL shader … has an unexpected argument buffer layout: encodedLength=88 maxAid=8` (crash in `c1_store` / `pull_load`), with a storage buffer or image missing from its reflection json.
- **Cause:** a pixel shader that writes a storage buffer and images inside a fragment interlock (the translator's FSI, `PixelInterlockOrderedEXT`) gets those members as `[[id(n), raster_order_group(0)]]` from SPIRV-Cross. `dmsh_reflect.py` `reflect` matched only `[[id(n)]]`, so it left them out, and the host's argument buffer came out longer than the reflection.
- **Fix:** `reflect` reads attributes after the id, and a member it still cannot read fails that shader's build (`reflection failed`) instead of loading with a short reflection. A chain change.
- **Found on:** Mario Party Superstars: `light_primitive_point_cluster.bnsh` (`56071a5cf62dccc3`, the engine's clustered point lights), first drawn on the way into a party.

### Image atomics

- **Symptom:** the replay put a texture resource ID where the shader wanted an address; with the old 64 KB dummy buffer, pointers in the game's objects and in the host's program objects got their upper 4 bytes replaced and the game or the replay crashed, or `kIOGPUCommandBufferCallbackErrorPageFault`, after which macOS ignores the process's command buffers (GitHub issue #101). Later, `[RED] image atomics on a block-linear texture … not followed, the atomics go to a scratch buffer`, and compute shaders with atomics on a 3D image did not build (`array subscript is not an integer`).
- **Cause:** below MSL 3.1, SPIRV-Cross emulates an image atomic with a raw device pointer beside the texture (`texture_buffer<uint> cp_i_cb2_4C_r32ui` and `device atomic_uint* cp_i_cb2_4C_r32ui_atomic`), indexed by the bound image's width (`spvImage2DAtomicCoord`), so the host had to bind memory in that packed layout. A buffer image's pool memory has it. A block-linear image's Metal texture is a deswizzled copy whose pool memory has another layout, so its atomics went to a scratch buffer that nothing read, and a pointer shorter than the image (or an invalid coordinate) wrote over other GPU memory. For a 3D image SPIRV-Cross indexes the pointer with the `int3` coordinate itself.
- **Fix:** image atomics are Metal's native texture atomics (MSL 3.1: `texture3d<uint, access::read_write>::atomic_fetch_max`). `dmsh_atomic.py` has SPIRV-Cross write shaders with image atomics (an `OpImageTexelPointer`) at MSL 3.1, and every other shader at MSL 2.3 as before, so their MSL is unchanged and their libraries come from the compile cache; a shader whose MSL still has the pointer form fails the build. The atomics act on the texture every other access of the image uses (a buffer image's view of its pool, a linear image's pool memory, a block-linear image's Metal texture), for buffer, 2D, 2D-array and 3D images. Metal runs texture atomics only on an R32Uint / R32Sint texture made with `MTLTextureUsageShaderAtomic`: NVN images of those formats take that usage when they are made (`nvn_texture.inc` `img_usage`; a view keeps working through its base), the build marks the slots a shader uses atomically (`"atomic"` in the reflection, `nvn_rbind.atom`), and the host binds an atomic-capable write sink, logged red, in place of any other texture there (`dummy_img_atomic`). The translator's coordinate guard stays ([Bounds of image atomics](#bounds-of-image-atomics)).
- **Found on:** Octopath Traveler 0: 2,084 shaders with atomics on buffer images (Unreal's GPU particles; three ran in the first 100 seconds) and 16 on a 3D image. Pokémon Legends: Z-A: an atomic max into a 480x270 r32ui block-linear 2D array (fragment shader `2bd5037003980716`, the scene after the story opening's first cutscene), which ran about 1.4 MB past the 64 KB dummy buffer into zero-copy pools over the game's heap and into unmapped addresses (GitHub issue #101), then went to a scratch buffer. FINAL FANTASY RESONANCE (8 shaders) and Ghost Trick (5, one of them a compare-and-swap): atomics on 3D images.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_image_atomic_bounds.py` with the rebuilt translator, `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1 DMSH_TEST_GPU=1`: synthetic Maxwell atomics on buffer, 2D, 2D-array and 3D images, compare-and-swaps among them, run on native atomic textures of a Mac GPU under the Metal validation layer (`t_atomic_return.m`). `t_refl.m` checks the atomic flag of a reflected image.

### Buffer image reflection keeps its texture type

- **Symptom:** `image atomics: unsupported or overflowing IMG extent` at the first dispatch, despite a valid four-byte counter buffer.
- **Cause:** `nvn_refl.m` classified `texture_buffer<uint>` as the default 2D texture. The atomic admission check of the pointer emulation then rejected the actual Metal buffer texture against that 2D descriptor.
- **Fix:** the shared reflection type decoder recognizes `texture_buffer` as `MTLTextureTypeTextureBuffer`, so a buffer image keeps its buffer-image shape. `t_refl.m` checks the reflection converter with a buffer image.
- **Found on:** SHIN MEGAMI TENSEI V: Vengeance, compute shader `2d2093c1f1d3a7e0`, image slot 2 (one R32Uint texel).

### Image atomics on a 32-bit colour texture

- **Symptom:** `[RED] image atomics on a texture Metal cannot run them on`, and whatever the shader computes through them is lost: its writes go to a sink.
- **Cause:** Metal runs texture atomics only on R32Uint and R32Sint textures and views. NVN lets a shader run them through an R32 view of any 32-bit colour texture: agl's anti-aliasing (`d17eefaff4f0bc23`) ORs its blend weights into RGBA8 that way, then reads it as RGBA8.
- **Fix:** `nvn_texture.inc` `tex_upgrade`, `tex_atomic_alias`: at the first such dispatch the texture moves to R32 storage holding the same bytes, and its own format becomes a view of that storage for sampling, rendering and copies. Packaged, multisampled, 3D and buffer-backed textures keep their storage. The design is [PR 1312](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1312)'s.
- **Check:** `make -C dm/gfx/tests GAME=0400486024DEC000 check_t_image_alias`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md).

### Bounds of image atomics

- **Symptom:** Metal shader validation reports invalid image-atomic stores even with an image-sized scratch buffer; without validation, GPU page faults can freeze rendering.
- **Cause:** the translator lost the surface instruction's `IGN` bounds mode before lowering an image atomic, so a coordinate outside the image reached memory. Neither a pointer nor a native texture atomic outside its image is defined on Metal.
- **Fix:** the translator (`TranslationFlags.MetalImageAtomics`, Metal only; other backends keep their behaviour) keeps the bounds mode and whether the result is used, queries the bound image's size and tests every original coordinate, the layer included, before the atomic. Negative coordinates fail an unsigned comparison; coordinates are not clamped or flattened. Invalid coordinates leave memory unchanged and return zero ([ARB_shader_image_load_store, issue 30](https://registry.khronos.org/OpenGL/extensions/ARB/ARB_shader_image_load_store.txt)): a returning `IGN` atomic joins the old texel from the valid branch and zero from the other with an SPIR-V phi. A compare-and-swap (`SUATOM.CAS`: compare with Rb, swap in Rb+1) is Metal's texture compare-exchange behind the same guard; SPIRV-Cross loops `atomic_compare_exchange_weak` until it succeeds or the texel differs. Predicate-returning, `TRAP` and unknown modes fail translation, as do 64-bit, floating-point and half2 sources (checked before the descriptor format can erase their size), descriptors that are not R32 integers, bindless handles that do not resolve statically, and indexed or multisample images. Source 1D and 1D-array surfaces keep the translator's one-row normalization to 2D.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Legends: Z-A ([PR #178](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/178)); SHIN MEGAMI TENSEI V: Vengeance, whose two buffer-image counter shaders dispatched at boot consume their returned values.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_image_atomic_bounds.py`. Portable tests check the MSL version choice and the rejection of the pointer form. With `DMSH_TEST_XLATE` set to a rebuilt translator (and `spirv-val` on `PATH`; spirv-cross is the in-repo build) the synthetic shaders are translated, the guard's dataflow is checked in the SPIR-V (the size query and comparison dominate the atomic, the phi carries zero on the invalid edge), and the MSL is produced; `DMSH_TEST_METAL=1` compiles and links it (`DMSH_TEST_METAL_SDKS=macosx,iphoneos` for both SDKs) and `DMSH_TEST_GPU=1` runs the returning fixtures (above). Changing this path changes the chain version: rebuild the translator and update the lock before building shaders.

### 64-bit image max and min (SURED.MAX.U64)

- **Symptom:** `NotSupportedException: Ampere 59f at …: SURED form 0110e400` in the shader build, then the scene's 3D picture black behind a UI that draws: `[RED] shader … (compute) failed to load` and `[RED] the dispatched program has no compute shader`. A texture dump shows the visibility target (RG32Uint) all zero.
- **Cause:** a visibility buffer written by compute rasterization keeps, per pixel, the nearest primitive as one 64-bit value, depth in the high word and the primitive id in the low word, through SURED.D.2D.MAX.U64 on an RG32UI image. The decoder took only the 32-bit sizes, and Metal's texture atomics were bound only on R32 textures. SPIRV-Cross has no 64-bit atomics at all.
- **Fix:** `ASured` sends the U64 form to `EmitSuatom`, which allows only a 2D, 2D-array or 3D max or min with no result (Metal has no 64-bit texture add, fetch or bitwise atomic). `GenerateImageAtomic` writes it as two 32-bit atomics of the same operation on the same texel, low word then high word, a marker that never runs. `dmsh_atomic.wide_atomics` turns each pair into one `texture2d<ulong, access::read_write>::atomic_max` / `atomic_min` (MSL 3.1) and fails the shader on any other access of that image. The host makes RG32Uint images with the shader-atomic usage on Apple GPU family 9, M3 / A17 Pro and later (`pf_atomic`), and the reflection marks such a binding `atom` 2. Older GPUs have no 64-bit texture atomic, and those pipelines do not build there. Ported from uncraftablexd's PRAGMATA bring-up ([PR #1801](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/1801)).
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA: 336 compute shaders, among them `26166d37ed56d6bb` and `3bc957e2739551e7`, dispatched at the title.
- **Check:** `DMSH_TEST_XLATE=<translator> python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py Sured64 WideAtomics`; `test_image_atomic_bounds.py` (the source-size gate).

### Reads past a uniform binding's size

- **Symptom:** the GPU hangs (Metal command-buffer timeouts, `GPU access revoked`, exit 70; on the Mac WindowServer can lose its watchdog check-in and the session restarts) or a draw reads garbage, where a shader reads a uniform slot further than the game bound it. No `[RED]` line.
- **Cause:** Maxwell bounds every constant buffer read by the size the game bound (`nvnCommandBufferBindUniformBuffer`): past it a read gives 0, and games rely on that. The translator declared each slot as `float4 _m0[4096]` behind a plain pointer, and Metal has no bounds of its own, so such a read reached the guest memory after the binding. Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door's `forward.bfsha` variant `f1e0f08cc43e21e3` reads its light count at byte 0x900 of uniform slot 11 and loops over that many lights, filling a 19-entry local array and reading c14 at `0x440 + 0x30 i`; the game binds 2,384 bytes to the slot for some materials and 16 bytes for others (501 of 1,024 bindings sampled in Peach's invisibility-potion scene). On the 16-byte bindings the count was whatever lay past them.
- **Fix:** in the chain and the host, for every game (`TranslationFlags.MetalUniformBounds`, Metal only), in two parts, since a slot's bound size is one value for a draw:
  - **Reads at fixed offsets, in the host, once a binding.** The translator leaves such a read as it is and reports how far they go in each game uniform slot (c[3] to c[16]): the bytes from the slot's start to the end of the highest word read so (`ResourceManager.NoteUniformRead`, `BufferDescriptor.FixedSize`, `fixed` in the translator's report and in the reflection's `cbuf` binding, `nvn_rbind.fixw`). When the game binds a slot shorter than that, the argument-buffer encode copies the binding into an arena block with zeros up to there and points the slot at it (`ub_padded` in `nvn_args.inc`; the bytes are the ones the slot would have pointed at, the deferred updates' or a partly updated block's copy included), and logs the first such shaders with a running count (`uniform slot N of shader K (stage S) is bound with B bytes and read up to byte E at fixed offsets`). Every other binding costs one comparison on the CPU. The code generator refuses a fixed read past the reported extent, so the two cannot disagree silently.
  - **Reads at computed offsets, in the shader.** The word's index is compared with the slot's size in words; out of bounds the read goes to row 0 and gives 0, so it reaches no memory past the binding (`TryGenerateBoundedUniformLoad` in `CodeGen/Spirv/Instructions.cs`). The sizes come from a table in a bank of its own, `DMSH_CBSIZE_BANK` (25), a u32 for each of the 14 slots in four `uint4` (binding kind `cbsize`, `NVN_RB_CBSIZE`), which the host fills from the current bindings at each argument-buffer encode, 0 for an unbound slot (whose reads already go to [the zero bank](rendering.md#an-unbound-uniform-slot-reads-the-zero-bank)), at most 64 KiB. Only a shader with such a read declares the table.

  **The size that bounds a slot is the bound size rounded up to 256 bytes** (`ub_bound` in `nvn_args.inc`), which is what the console's driver gives the GPU: `nvnCommandBufferBindUniformBuffer` writes `(size + 0xff) & ~0xff` in NVN1's driver (method 0x8e0; Sonic Superstars' `sdk`, 0x467170) and `((size + 0xff) >> 8) << 20` in NVN2's (method 0xe02; Ocarina of Time's `sdk`, 0x67bc80). A read between the bound size and the next multiple of 256 gives the memory there, so the padded copy carries those bytes (where the pool reaches that far) and the size table holds the rounded size. The host used the exact size until [GitHub issue #2218](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2218); Ocarina of Time binds 16, 64, 144, 192, 464, 976, 16,960 and 41,600 bytes. Open: what an Ampere GPU returns past the rounded size is not measured (Maxwell gives 0), and whether this is what streaked Ocarina's animated models is not shown: the streaks did not appear on an M-series Mac here with the exact size either (the slingshot aimed at Link's house, the ReDeads of Castle Town).

  The driver segments (c0, c2), the shader's own c1, NVN2's push constants (c[24]) and compute's uniform pointers (`ubo_ptr`, global memory on Maxwell too) are not bounded. The host takes a uniform word for its own proofs only from inside the binding (`ubo_float` refuses a word past the size).
- **History:** the first form (chain `3510ea767778`) compared every read in the shader, fixed offsets too (`(576u < (sizes[2].w >> 2u)) ? c14[144].x : 0.0`). spirv-cross wrote each as a conditional expression, a branch for every read, until [they were written as selects](#bounded-uniform-reads-written-as-select) (chain `eaa843a79149`). The branch form cost little where shaders read few uniform words (NieR:Automata's opening flight, GPU p50 +1.6%) and a fifth of the GPU's time in Donkey Kong Bananza, whose material shaders read many: 14.6 → 17.5-18.1 ms a frame on the Freezer Layer on an M4, which took the game from 55 to 30 fps ([GitHub issue #2066](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2066), the reporter's measurement on their own build). Of the 8.3 million uniform reads in its 23,638 shaders, 95.5% are at fixed offsets, and 73% of the shaders have no other kind.
- **Found on:** Paper Mario: The Thousand-Year Door ([GitHub issue #1860](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/1860)): the reporter found the variant and the short bindings, and padding those bindings with zeros let Peach's scene through on an M3 Ultra Mac and an M4 iPad Pro. Donkey Kong Bananza ([GitHub issue #2066](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2066)): the cost of comparing every read.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_dmsh_abi.py` `test_uniform_bounds_agree` (the translator's bank and slots are the header's); `dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_ampere_mem.py` `test_fixed_uniform_reads_are_bare_and_counted` (with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`: two fixed reads of c[3] give no size table and `fixed` 28). On NieR:Automata's 1,225 shaders built with this change: all 148,022 reads at fixed offsets are bare, all 28,604 at computed offsets are compared (and `select_guards` writes each of them as a select, none refused), 455 shaders declare the size table, and each slot's `fixed` ends in the highest row its source reads at a constant index. Not run in a game yet (open): the host's padding has met no short binding, the potion scene's retest by the reporter of #1860 is open, and so is Bananza's frame time on the Freezer Layer. The first form's numbers, `bench.py` on NieR:Automata's opening flight (M6, two runs each, 60 s window): GPU time p50 6.43 → 6.53 ms (+1.6%), p95 9.12 → 9.70 ms (+6.4%), frame time unchanged at 60 fps. Changing this path changes the chain version: rebuild the translator, update the lock and rebuild the shaders.
- **Computed row bounds:** the translator compares an unsigned row index to the size table's byte count divided by 16 when it can prove the component is in 0–3. The host's size entries are multiples of 16. Comparing the row directly avoids a left shift that could discard the high bits, and the MSL rewriter keeps the load and select. Fixed-offset reads still use the reflected extent. In the author's Mario Kart World docked race (M3 Max, uncapped), the original row optimization reduced scene pass time from 2.43 to 2.34 ms and 2.42 to 2.23 ms in the two run orders. The translator implementation is common to every profile.

### Bounded uniform reads written as select

- **Symptom:** on an M1 Pro (macOS 26), blue, green and white blocks for one or two frames on trees that dither-fade (OCTOPATH TRAVELER's S'warkii, walking behind a tree); a texture dump shows NaN pixels in the scene colour and holes in the normal G-buffer target, which depth of field spreads into blocks. Not seen on an iPhone or on newer Macs. On every GPU, a branch for each uniform word a shader reads.
- **Cause:** the translator's bounded read ([Reads past a uniform binding's size](#reads-past-a-uniform-bindings-size)) is a load and a choice, OpLoad then OpSelect, but spirv-cross writes OpSelect on a scalar as a conditional expression and forwards the load into it: `cond ? read : 0.0`, which reads only when the condition holds. Metal's compiler makes a branch, a load inside it and a phi of each (its LLVM IR at -O1 and -O2, `xcrun metal -S -emit-llvm`), where `select(0.0, read, cond)` is one load and a select with no branch. The M1's compiler gets that form wrong in OCTOPATH TRAVELER's tree material (pixel shader `040208cd98c2ac2f`): its volumetric lightmap word (G-buffer target 5) comes out wrong although every guard is true (all its bindings are full size, and a size table of 64 KiB for every slot changes nothing), and the lighting turns it into NaN. The same shader at -O0, without the guards, or with them as `select` is right; `-fno-fast-math` does not help; only removing the guards of the whole lightmap block (c3[174..177]) fixes it, not either half. Found by bisecting to `26062f9e` (5 against 0 of 96 shots), then the shaders, then the guards inside the one shader. **Open:** which step of Apple's compiler fails is not known, nor whether it is the Metal front end of macOS 26, which built those libraries, or the M1's GPU compiler: a platform fault that the select form avoids and does not fix.
- **Fix:** every game, a chain change. `dmsh_rewrite.select_guards`, called by `dmsh_build.py` on each spirv-cross output, writes every bounded read as `select(0, read, cond)`, the translator's own meaning. Since [GitHub issue #2066](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2066) only reads at computed offsets are bounded in the shader (the fixed-offset reads this was found on are plain loads again, the ones Metal made before reads were bounded; the host bounds them). At a computed offset the index already chooses row 0 when out of bounds (`bool _5 = word < size; select(0.0, c14[_5 ? _3 : 0][_4], _5)`), so the load stays inside the binding without the branch. A bounded read it cannot write as a select fails the shader (`uniform bounds rewrite failed`), so a change in what spirv-cross writes cannot bring the branches back unseen.
- **Found on:** OCTOPATH TRAVELER ([GitHub PR #2127](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2127): the bisection, the shader and the select form, on an M1 Pro).
- **Check:** `python3 dm/gfx/tests/test_dmsh_fetch.py UniformBounds` (the forms spirv-cross writes at a computed offset, the error for an unknown form or a fixed offset compared in the shader). On the M1 Pro, the dog route in S'warkii (walking up and down behind the tree left of the dog by the weapon shop): 5 to 14 of 96 shots with blocks before, 0 of 192 with the fixed-offset reads as selects; not run there with those reads plain (open: the author's retest).

### Reads past a storage buffer bound by address

- **Symptom:** a shader reads another resource's bytes where it indexes a storage buffer past the memory the game mapped there. No `[RED]` line. PRAGMATA's R53 hangar (after the tram ride, "Contact Earth from the Comms Tower") washes out in pink, green and white as you enter and on the stairs up to the upper deck, and it fades when the camera stays still; on the console the hangar is dark blue-grey.
- **Cause:** a storage buffer whose address the shader takes from a game uniform slot (`sbuf_ubo`, `NVN_RB_SBUFU`) or the push constants (`sbuf_push`, `NVN_RB_SBUFQ`) has no size. On the console the shader reads the GPU's page table as far as it indexes from the address: the pages mapped there, and 0 for an unmapped page of a sparse pool, where a write is dropped. The host binds the Metal buffer that holds the address (`mtl_gpu_addr(addr, 1)`): a memory pool, a large pool's chunk, or a sparse pool's mapped run. Metal has no bounds of its own, so a read past that buffer reached whatever lay after it in the GPU's address space. PRAGMATA's GI voxeliser `ef4ad01151918469` reads its surfel colours through such a buffer (`cp_s3`). When the hangar is entered, the buffer's address is the base of a 1 GB sparse pool with one run of 0xc0000 bytes mapped there, and the shader reads 0x150000 bytes and more. The bytes past the run became hot surfel colours, which the GI carried through `c08db9c9` and `bfc21183` / `936be330` into its volume, `845cad29b8a61d04` into a packed colour target, and the lighting draw `10e0a286` into the picture. The reporters traced this chain with texture dumps.
- **Fix:** in the chain and the host, for every game (`TranslationFlags.MetalStorageBounds`, Metal only). Each access to such a buffer compares its word index with the memory the host bound there, `idx + before < total` as one unsigned comparison that bounds both ends (`TryGetBoundedStoragePointer` in `CodeGen/Spirv/Instructions.cs`). Out of bounds, a load reads word 0 and gives 0, written as a select (`dmsh_rewrite.select_guards`, as for [uniform reads](#bounded-uniform-reads-written-as-select)), and a store or an atomic is skipped (an atomic gives 0). The two words come from a table in a bank of its own, `DMSH_SBLEN_BANK` (27): two u32 for each storage slot, binding kind `sblen` (`NVN_RB_SBLEN`), the slot in the reflection's `sbx`. The host fills the table as it encodes each binding (`sblen_put` in `nvn_args.inc`): the words of the buffer before the address, then that plus the words from the address to the buffer's end, or 0 for the dummy. `wide_loads` reads a run of bounded words whole when its first and last words are inside, and word by word otherwise. NVN's own storage buffer table (c[0], with a size) and the pool table of raw global memory are not bounded. `first_store` leaves a bounded store alone, since the host makes first stores only to c[0] slots.
- **Not taken:** a tail of zero pages past the last mapped run ([PR #2626](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2626)). It covered 4 MB past the run with fresh anonymous pages (memory wired for each span, and writes kept), and the reporters' hangar still washed in 2 of 3 runs with it. Their runs came out clean only once three GI kernels also bounded their reads by the binding's length, which is this fix.
- **Open:** a read past the run that would reach a later mapped run after a hole reads 0 here, where the console reads that run (spans over holes are [PR #2509](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/pull/2509)). A read more than 64 MB past the address in a large pool reads 0, past the chunk the lookup makes (`BIG_UNKNOWN`). Not run in a game here: the PRAGMATA build folder is gone and there is no save near the hangar, so the hangar retest is the reporters'. The cost is estimated, not measured: one comparison and one select for each load, and a branch for each store or atomic, in the shaders with such buffers: PRAGMATA's 28,967 (the reporters' count), Orbitals' 11,149 of 23,310, and Pikmin 4's 1 of 15,704 (counted here with the translator).
- **Found on:** PRAGMATA ([GitHub issue #2618](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/2618)): the reporters found the binding (`DM_NVN_SBUFU_DIAG` on their test host), the chain to the screen and the bounded-read experiment.
- **Check:** `t_storage_bounds` (26 checks, under Metal validation, through `mtl_gpu_addr`, `sblen_put` and the real `MapVirtual`). It covers an address inside a pool (the words before it and after it, nothing past the pool), the hangar's binding (a sparse pool with one run at its base, read past the run: zeros), stores and atomics inside and outside, and a dummy binding. With the comparison removed, the hangar case reads non-zero bytes past the run. Also `test_dmsh_storage_bounds.py` (with `DMSH_TEST_XLATE`: an SBUFU load and store bounded beside an unbounded c[0] buffer, an SBUFQ load and atomic, the reflection), `test_dmsh_fetch.py` `UniformBounds` and `test_dmsh_wide.py`. A translator A/B on sampled shaders: Pikmin 4, 675 of 676 identical, the one that changed a compute shader with a written SBUFU at `c[3][0x20]`; Nintendo Switch Sports Resort, 762 of 762 identical.

## Pipelines and caches

### A compile that does not return stalls every flush and draw

- **Symptom:** the pipeline prebuild stops at the same line on every start (`pipeline prebuild: 7000 of 20740 pairs, 1987 built, 5013 already archived, 0 left to the draws, 0 failed`), behind *Preparing pipelines* or, with `DM_PSO_PREPARE=0`, as a black screen while the sound plays on; the NSP script's `[7/7] Pipeline prebuild` does not end (`dmhost` at 0% CPU, `MTLCompilerService` at 100%), and `MTLCompilerService` crash reports (`EXC_CRASH`, `llvm::report_bad_alloc_error`) come every 6 to 10 minutes. The bases built since the last file are never written, so the next start builds them again and stops at the same pair.
- **Cause:** one base Apple's GPU compiler cannot build, and a host lock that let its compile hold everything else. The compiler grows `MTLCompilerService` past several GB until it aborts in `llvm::report_bad_alloc_error`, and Metal hands the request to the relaunched service; only after four aborts (33.7 minutes on an M6 with 16 GB, macOS 27.0) does `newRenderPipelineStateWithDescriptor` return, with `Compilation failed due to an interrupted connection: XPC_ERROR_CONNECTION_INTERRUPTED. This error occurred after multiple retries.` Metal's serializer cannot be written while it captures: the write (`-[_MTLBinaryArchive airntSerializeToURL:options:error:]`) runs on the archive's `_serializeQueue` and then empties the archive (its entries, AIR objects, library cache and file mapping) on its `_queue`, while each function a compile captures adds itself from the compile's completion (`__122-[MTLCompiler compileFunctionRequestInternal:…]_block_invoke`: a new `MTLBinaryEntry` from the request's AIR script and the function's bitcode, mapped by `newArchiverIdWithBinaryKey` before it takes `_queue`, then added under it, before the compile reports back). A compile under way across a write came back with both nil and crashed in `dispatch_data_create_map` (seen in lldb at the crash, the write's thread waiting for `_serializeQueue`). So every compile the archive captured held a read lock (`g_arch_rw`) and a flush its write lock. The flush at the next 1,000 bases waited behind the compile, and every other compile, draw and the present path waited behind the flush. The prepare step also waited for the last compile to return.
- **Fix:** flush periods (`ArchEpoch` in `nvn_archive.inc`). Each period has its own compiler and serializer; a compile the archive captures joins the current period (`arch_capture`) and leaves it when it returns. A flush (`arch_flush`) gives the compiles after it a new period and returns at once; the old period's serializer is written (`arch_write`, on the archive queue) once every compile in it has returned. No compile waits for a flush, and a compile that does not return holds back only its own period's file. The prebuild's flushes every 1,000 bases no longer wait; its last one (`arch_flush_wait`) waits for every period before `prebuilt.tsv` is written. Specializations go through a compiler without a serializer (`g_comp4`). The prepare step waits until every pair is handed to a worker; compiles still under way then (at most one per worker) finish in the background, and the log says `pipeline prepare: every shader pair handed out after … s; N compiles still under way finish in the background`. The prebuild tool still waits for every compile, since it writes them all. The base itself was made buildable by [Front end at -O1](#front-end-at--o1-apples-control-flow-lowering) (11.4 s at run time on the same M6). A base that fails no longer keeps `prebuilt.tsv` from being written (every start compiled it again in the background, half an hour at `-O2`): the pair goes into `pso_archive/prebuild_failed.tsv` with the system build and the stamp it failed under (`pre_fail_load`), and a later start of that system build with that stamp leaves it to the draws without compiling it; another system build (a new GPU compiler) or stamp tries it again. A failure Metal reports has already been retried by Metal itself. The log says `pipeline prebuild: N pairs whose base failed are left to the draws (…prebuild_failed.tsv; M of them failed on an earlier start of this system build, not built again)`. **Open:** a compile still under way when the game exits leaves no result, so a base that takes longer than a session to fail is compiled again in the background on the next start.
- **Found on:** Super Mario Odyssey, update 1.4.1, GitHub issue #294: the base of pair 5641, `3c3435a62e05975b` + `007d7e411ef14ebd` (`alRenderMaterialUber.bfsha` variation 0, 444,013 lines of MSL), found by a reporter's bisect of the pair list with `DM_PSO_PREBUILD=tool`, with that chain's `-O2` front end. On an M6 with 16 GB (macOS 27.0), the prebuild tool on the whole list with the `-O2` library and 8 workers: the old host stopped at 7,000 of 20,740 at 65 s and wrote nothing more in 5 minutes; with flush periods 20,000 pairs were done at 262 s, and 17 files held 18,739 of the other 20,739 bases, only the 2,000 in that compile's period waiting for it (three compiler aborts in 40 minutes). With the `-O1` library (all 20,740 built): 253 s cold and 227 s with the old host, 213 and 225 s with flush periods; the files written gave all 20,740 back, none compiled.
- **Check:** `t_pso_stall`: a compiler that holds one prebuild compile; while it is held, the prebuild hands out every pair and builds the others, a draw's compile returns and a later period's flush writes its file, and a base of a written period still specializes; once released, its period is written and the prebuild stamps. A pair whose compile fails: stamped all the same and listed; not compiled again on a later start of the same system build, also without the stamp; tried again with a list from another system build, and the list removed once it builds. On Odyssey's pair 5641 alone with the `-O2` library (M6, macOS 27.0): Metal's error after 36.6 minutes, the pair listed and the stamp written; the next start had nothing to do (1 s), and one without `prebuilt.tsv` left the pair to the draws without compiling it. The Metal behaviour, by a probe outside the tree on an M6 (macOS 27.0), eight threads compiling 1,500 pipelines: one serializer written every 150 ms while they compile crashed 3 runs of 3 in `newArchiverIdWithBinaryKey`; a write that first waits for the compiles under way but lets new ones start, 6 of 6; one that lets no compile start until it is done, 1 run of 111, during the write itself, with [issue #342](#a-compaction-flush-that-crashes-then-crashes-every-start)'s signature (`dispatch_retain(NULL)` in `materializeAirNTFromOnlineForSlice`); a compiler and serializer per period, none of 118, every pipeline found in the files, also with 121 periods in one run (no memory growth), and making a period took about 30 µs.

### Shader-pair tables in a Mac app

- **Symptom:** a self-contained Mac app (`--platform mac-app`) logs `pipeline prebuild: no .../Resources/dm/shader_pairs.tsv, nothing to build` although every shader library is there; it draws no hybrid stand-ins, and each pipeline seen for the first time compiles on the submit thread (frames of 100–550 ms).
- **Cause:** `dm/mac/pack_app.py` copied the shader map, the prewarm manifest, `c1_full.tsv` and `cb_extent.tsv` beside `DM_NVN_SHMAP`, but not `shader_pairs.tsv` and `pso_pairs.tsv`, which `build_ipa.sh` copies into the IPA and the host's pipeline prebuild reads there (`pso_prebuild`). Without them the app makes no pulling bases. For a profile without `pso_pairs.tsv` a Mac run (`mac_run`) passes the shader split's copy as `DM_PSO_PAIRS`; the app had neither. A full shader-library build does not build the pipeline bases by itself.
- **Fix:** the packer copies both tables when the profile has them, and the split's `pso_pairs.tsv` (`--pso-pairs`, which the NSP script passes) when the profile has none. They stay optional. An app packed before the fix needs repacking. An NVN game's app also carries its pipeline archive made offline for this Mac's GPU, so the first start builds no base: [A Mac app](../development.md#a-mac-app).
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Echoes of Wisdom, update 1.1.0: a 308.4 ms frame, 283.5 ms of it pipeline acquisition and 5.9 ms GPU work; with the tables the offline prebuild built all 7,495 bases. The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild: frames of 100–546 ms before, none of its bases compiled on the first start after.
- **Check:** `test_nsp_mac_app`: `App.test_pipeline_pair_tables`, `App.test_split_pso_pairs` and `App.test_layout` fail without the tables in the packer; `Script.test_pack_command` checks the split's copy is passed only when the profile has no `pso_pairs.tsv`.

### Offline pipeline archive reuse and fallback

- **Symptom:** a truncated `.mtl4a` reaches `MTL4ArchiveImpl::loadFromURL`; the bundle reports coverage with `0 of 0 probed bases found`; rebuilt shader libraries reuse an archive of their old binaries; or packing fails with `pso_offline: no base to build` when every eligible pair has function constants, or when the profile's `shader_pairs.tsv` holds only its header (`--platform mac-app`, [GitHub issue #338](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/338)).
- **Cause:** the archive guard checked only four magic bytes; coverage accepted `found == probed` even when every sampled pair was ineligible; the offline cache key included library paths and profile stamps but omitted library bytes; and a zero-base plan aborted before writing its device remainder list, also when there was no pair at all, so `mac_pso()` never reached its no-archive branch.
- **Fix:** `arch_file_ok` checks the GPU Mach-O load commands and payload ranges, universal slice bounds and the serializer's bundled Metal library ranges before opening the archive. It does not validate the compiler's private payload. `pso_bundle_covers` requires at least one actual lookup and no misses. `pso_offline.py` hashes every consumed library's bytes into the cache key; when all eligible pairs need device specialization, it removes old archives and writes `rest_pairs.tsv` without invoking `metal-tt`. A pair list with no pair takes the same path (old archives removed, stamp written, exit 0), and the app is packed without an archive; pairs that the host would leave out entirely (wrong or missing libraries) still fail.
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Breath of the Wild, during review of its offline archive path with synthetic libraries and pair lists; no new gameplay or iPhone run. The empty pair list on ANIMAL WELL.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` changes vertex and fragment library bytes without changing entries or stamps, and exercises the all-constant plan, the header-only pair list and pairs all left out. `t_pso_bundle` rejects bare headers, truncated archives, malformed load commands and out-of-range universal slices, accepts real offline and serialized archives, and rejects zero-probe coverage.

### A bundled pipeline archive read a page at a time when it opens

- **Symptom:** a gray screen for several seconds at startup, before the pipeline progress screen, on a Mac whose GPU has an archive in the app: `pipeline archive bundled with the app: .../pso/applegpu_g16s.mtl4a, 4405.6 MB, opened in 7978.8 ms`. The same app starts fast on a Mac whose GPU has none, although that Mac's own `pso_archive` folder holds twice as much (38 files, 8,880 MB, about 1.4 s on an M1 Pro).
- **Cause:** Metal's open (`MTL4ArchiveImpl::loadFromURL`, macOS 27) makes a library of every Metal library in the file and hashes all of it with SHA-256 on the calling thread. It reads the whole `__AIR_DATA` segment and `__TEXT,__metallib` section of each GPU image, 2.9 GB of Ocarina of Time's 4.4 GB file (`mincore` after the open). It leaves the GPU code (`__vertex`, `__fragment`, `__compute`) for when a pipeline is taken. A file not in memory is mapped and read one page fault at a time, about 230 MB/s. The device's folder is opened file by file in parallel, so its faults overlap. The bundled archive is one file: 12.5 s cold and 1.5 s warm in a probe on an M4 Max. The bytes Metal reads, not an index, are the cost: the device files' `.keys` index only speeds up lookups after the open.
- **Fix:** `arch_open` (`nvn_archive.inc`) first asks for the ranges Metal will read, 8 MB at a time on every core, from the load commands `arch_file_ok` already checks: each piece is mapped on its own, given `MADV_WILLNEED` and unmapped, so the kernel reads it into the file cache that Metal's mapping shares. Metal then finds it in memory. It is used for every archive file the host opens: the bundled one, the bundle's prewarm files and the device's folder, inside the window that maps them (see [A pipeline archive on an external drive is read into memory](memory.md#a-pipeline-archive-on-an-external-drive-is-read-into-memory)). The first version read each piece into an 8 MB heap buffer: as fast, but `malloc` kept the freed buffers, +99 MB of footprint on a 12-core M6 after the open, and a warm open copied every byte again. One mapping of the whole file is ruled out because Metal maps the whole file too, and two of a 4.4 GB file do not fit an iPhone's address space; `F_RDADVISE` was erratic (0.34–12.9 s cold for the same file).
- **Found on:** [Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md#found-on-this-game) (v131072), its Mac app on an M4 Max, macOS 27. The host's `pso_bundle_open` on the bundled archive, each time a fresh APFS clone (a clone's pages are not in memory): 8.07 / 7.91 s → 1.61 / 1.52 s; warm 1.04–1.15 → 1.09–1.10 s. A device folder of 21 files (1,490 MB) through `pso4_init`: 452 → 220 ms cold, 88 → 100 ms warm. In the game, headless boots of host `f4b7e77c` with and without the change (the app's chain), each on fresh clones of the bundle and of a 23-file device folder: the bundled archive 10.57 / 8.07 s → 1.63 / 1.53 s, the device folder 673 / 460 → 258 / 221 ms. Both sides found the same pipelines: 15 of 15 probed bases, 324 compute pipelines from the bundle, 164 mesh pipelines from the archive and 6,061 indexed pipelines.
- **Measured again** on an M6 (macOS 27, 12 cores) after the rebase onto the mapped reader, a harness opening through the host's code inside its window, each cold run on a fresh APFS clone on an external drive: Divinity: Original Sin 2's bundled archive (373 MB, 231 MB read ahead) 0.99–1.58 s → 0.34 s cold, warm 92–97 → 99–103 ms; 16 of Ocarina of Time's device files opened in parallel (1.8 GB, 746 MB read ahead) 0.93–1.26 → 0.64–0.77 s cold, warm 81–82 → 91–98 ms. Footprint after the open the same on both sides (20 and 26–27 MB).
- **Check:** `t_pso_bundle` checks that the reads of an offline archive and of a serialized one are their records and libraries, less than the file, and that both open through `arch_open`.

### Shader extraction reruns after translation-only updates

- **Symptom:** an unchanged NSP spends minutes in `shader split` after a shader-chain update, on both Mac and iOS builds, although the extracted package is identical.
- **Cause:** the split key included all graphics Python tools, every profile file and the whole decoder host executable. Translation tools, profile notes and the host's compiled `DMSH_CHAIN` label therefore invalidated extraction.
- **Fix:** the NSP script follows the extractor's transitive repository Python imports and fingerprints its profile settings, manual facts, checked/generated tables, modules, RomFS/DLC identities and capture directories. A managed decoder host records its build inputs with only the chain label normalized, bound to its executable's bytes; unknown or replaced binaries use their binary identity. All other host build inputs and rewritten module contents still invalidate conservatively. Package and supplemental-pair digests reject damaged cached outputs. The common split serves Mac and iOS, while translation keeps its own target-specific keys. Existing split caches require one warm-up.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Legends: Z-A.
- **Check:** `test_nsp_incremental.py` covers translation-only hits, transitive readers, extraction settings, auxiliary modules, explicit captures, decoder changes, corrupt outputs and the existing registered/derived, DLC and damaged-input paths. `test_host_msl.py` verifies executable-bound provenance, chain-only reuse and invalidation for decoder/profile/compiler/header changes. `test_nsp_mac.py` checks separate Mac/iOS translation outputs.

### Offline pipeline archive rebuilt after a chain-only update

- **Symptom:** rebuilding an unchanged NSP after a shader-chain update runs `metal-tt` for minutes even when all referenced Metal libraries and pipeline settings remain identical.
- **Cause:** the offline archive key included the chain label, profile stamp, whole builder source and absolute library paths inside `b-<chain>`. These can change without changing the compiler's inputs.
- **Fix:** `pso_offline.py` keys each archive by the complete generated pipeline configuration with library locations normalized to their labels, every referenced library's name and bytes, GPU architecture, platform, minimum OS and Metal translator version. It still validates reflection against the current chain before planning. After a cache hit it writes the current `prebuilt.tsv`, remainder list and archive metadata, so host validation remains current. macOS and iOS use the same builder with separate target keys. Legacy cache keys rebuild once; a changed shader, entry point, pipeline descriptor or compiler target still rebuilds the archive. The cache format has an explicit version for future changes not represented by these inputs.
- **Found on:** Pokémon Legends: Z-A, while measuring repeat Mac app builds. Timings and platform-validation limits are in the game note.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` exercises chain relocation and metadata refresh on both platforms, changed shader bytes and pipeline settings, target and compiler changes, missing archives, stale reflection and legacy keys.

### Offline pipeline archive and profile depth disagree

- **Symptom:** the host logs `pipeline archive bundled with the app: made for ... this run needs ...`, naming different depths, and pipeline bases compile during play despite a successful archive build. There need not be a `[RED]` line.
- **Cause:** offline builds use the profile's `depth_mode` (`gl` when omitted), while runtime vertex entries follow `nvnDeviceSetDepthMode`. An archive built with `main0` cannot supply a near-zero base using `main0_z`. The runtime stamp comparison correctly rejects the mismatch; the missing input is in the profile.
- **Fix:** declare the observed mode in the profile and rebuild the archive. Both vertex entries already exist, so this does not require a shader-chain change. `scripts/regress.py` records the runtime depth and compares it with the tested revision's profile on every NVN smoke run, including quick runs without an archive. It also records bundled archive stamp rejection for any chain, depth or pair-list mismatch. New mismatches fail the check; unchanged baseline mismatches are reported as known and prevent `--record` from marking the game validated. An absent depth log is unknown, not evidence for GL. This detects mismatches on exercised routes; it does not infer unobserved games' modes or edit profiles automatically. For a new game the NSP script does: the heap and address region checks of a provisional or derived profile set the key from the game's own `nvnDeviceSetDepthMode` call in their boot, before the shader steps (Super Mario RPG before this: a 24 minute prebuild at the default mode, then 0 of 34 archive lookups found). A game with no check boot, or one that stops before the call, keeps the default.
- **Found on:** The Legend of Zelda: Echoes of Wisdom, update 1.1.0, [GitHub issue #758](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/758): its note already recorded near-zero depth, but its profile omitted the key. The game note records archive validation and its limits; castle-entry performance remains open.
- **Also confirmed:** missing depth keys on SteamWorld Heist II, NieR:Automata, Portal, DRAGON QUEST III HD-2D Remake, Raging Loop, Subnautica, Subnautica: Below Zero, Batman: Arkham Knight, Blasphemous 2, EA SPORTS FC 27, Paper Mario: The Origami King and Super Smash Bros. Ultimate (13.0.5, a player's Mac app: its 1,316.9 MB archive refused, 31,148 pairs built before the first frame in 397 s). Recorded routes, short isolated boots and the NSP script's check boots select near-zero depth on their registered module builds; their notes record the evidence. The profile corrections have not been measured as rebuilt-archive performance fixes.
- **Also found on:** Cozy Land: the original profile omitted the key; its note records the corrected prebuild and runtime reuse.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` runs the archive builder with the affected games' real profiles and synthetic libraries, checking the stamp and the vertex entry. `dm/tools/tests/test_regress.py` replays runtime depth and rejection logs through a smoke run, checking failure classification and reporting.

### Offline pipeline archive made for another major version

- **Symptom:** `pipeline archive bundled with the app: this system cannot open …/applegpu_<gpu>.mtl4a (deployment target for architecture … found in archive … is not compatible with current OS …): the bases are built on the device`. On macOS 26.6 with Xcode 27.1, `t_pso_bundle` failed 10 checks, from `bundle: opened` on, and a Mac app packed there compiled every base on its first start, as without the archive.
- **Cause:** a Metal 4 pipeline archive opens only on the major system version it was made for. Measured on macOS 27.2 (M4 Pro, `newArchiveWithURL:`): archives that `metal-tt` made with `-macos-version-min` 26.0 or 26.6 were refused, from libraries built for 26.0 or 26.6 alike; those made for 27.0 and 27.2 opened. `pso_offline.py` took the SDK's major version for every platform. That suits the iPhone, whose system is the SDK's, but the Mac app is built for the Mac that builds it, and Xcode 27.1's SDK is 27.0 on macOS 26.6. Also, the Mac host had no deployment target, so clang took the SDK's 27.0, and `pack_app.py` copied it into the app's `LSMinimumSystemVersion`. `metal-tt` also refuses libraries built for a later system than the archive's (`image AIR version (2.9) is bigger than the one of the target (2.8)`, macOS 27.0 libraries for a 26.6 archive); the chain's Mac libraries are built for the building Mac's system, so that Mac's major version is never below them.
- **Fix:** for macOS, `pso_offline.py`'s default `--os-min` is this Mac's major version (`sw_vers`); iOS keeps the SDK's. The Mac host's minimum is `MACOS_MIN` in `dm/mac/min_os.mk` (26.6, as the README asks), on the host's compile lines (`-mmacosx-version-min`, libvpx's too), which the NSP script and `pack_app.py` follow. The host builds with the same warnings at 26.6 as at 27.0 (no unguarded newer API). An archive is still for one major version: an iPhone on iOS 26 cannot open the IPA's 27.0 archive and builds its bases itself (open: one archive per major version would serve both).
- **Found on:** no game: [GitHub issue #673](https://github.com/seedds/nxnative/issues/673), `t_pso_bundle` on an M4 Pro with macOS 26.6.2 and Xcode 27.1.
- **Check:** `dm/tools/tests/test_pso_offline.py` (`test_macos_takes_this_macs_major_version`). `t_pso_bundle` builds its archive with the default and opens it on macOS 27.2: 52 checks pass with its libraries built for 27.0 and for 26.6 (`MACOSX_DEPLOYMENT_TARGET=26.6`), the mirror of the reporter's Mac; the same libraries in an archive made for 26.6 failed the 10 checks. Open: no run on macOS 26.x here.

### Pipeline archive files left behind by a major system update

- **Symptom:** after a major macOS update, every start logs `[RED] pipeline archive file …/pso_archive/p00042.mtl4a not usable (deployment target for architecture … is not compatible with current OS …): its bases are built again`, once per file written on the old system, but no `pipeline prebuild:` line; the game then stalls for one to several seconds whenever a scene shows bases it had before (frames of 1–7 s talking to a character or when bombs go off). The red lines and the missing prebuild come back on every later start; a base a draw built is captured again, so the stalls move on to the scenes not yet played since the update.
- **Cause:** the [same rule as the offline archive](#offline-pipeline-archive-made-for-another-major-version) for the files the host writes: Metal opens a pipeline archive only on the major version it was made for. `pso4_init` skipped a file it could not open but left it on disk. The prebuild's files (`p*.mtl4a`) carry its bases, and `prebuilt.tsv` still matched (the stamp does not hold the system version), so the prebuild was skipped as done for good and those bases were left to the draws, each built on the submit thread (about 0.3 s, M1 Pro) the first time a scene needed it. The refused files also stayed on disk for good.
- **Fix:** a file Metal refuses is removed with its index, as old indexes already were; a refused prebuild file also removes `prebuilt.tsv`, so the next start runs the prebuild again. The log says `… not usable (…): removed, its bases are built again`.
- **Found on:** [The Legend of Zelda: Ocarina of Time](../../dm/games/0400486024DEC000/README.md), M1 Pro with 16 GB, after updating macOS 26.6.2 to 27.2.1: 57 refused files (1.68 GB of 3.1 GB), 7 of them prebuild files, and no prebuild in a 24-minute session, which had 53 stalls of 100 ms or more on new pipelines (39 s in all). On a copy of that archive the fixed host removed the 57 files and `prebuilt.tsv` and ran the prebuild; on main with an empty archive the whole prebuild took 13.4 minutes for 34,649 pairs (10 workers) and 2.7 more for the prewarm lists, once.
- **Check:** `t_pso_archive` (`test_unusable_file_removed`): a prebuild file Metal will not open, its index and `prebuilt.tsv` are removed on the next start, and the other files still give every base. It fails without the fix.

### Mac prewarm pipelines reused after a packed shader library changes
